#### 🍰 [Angel Food Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-angel-food-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.24/serving** · 106 kcal · P1 · C25 · F0 · ⏱ 130 min · 🍽 12  

An ethereally light, cloud-soft sponge built entirely on whipped egg whites — no butter, no yolks, no chemical leavening. Cream of tartar stabilizes the meringue, sifted cake flour and powdered sugar keep the crumb tender, and a whisper of vanilla and almond extract make it taste like sweet air. Cooled upside down so it never collapses.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Liquid egg whites** — about 1.5 cups / 12 large egg whites, at room temperature
- **White sugar** — 1 cup superfine/caster sugar, divided
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup, sifted with the flour
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup cake flour (sift 3 times)
- **Cream of tartar** — 1.5 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Vanilla extract** — 1.5 tsp
- **Almond extract** — 1/4 tsp _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the lower third of the oven and heat to 350F (175C). Have a clean, ungreased 10-inch tube (angel food) pan ready — do NOT grease it; the batter needs to grip the sides to climb.
2. Sift the cake flour with the powdered sugar and salt together three times into a bowl; this aerates them and guarantees no lumps. Set aside.
3. In a spotlessly clean, grease-free metal or glass bowl, beat the room-temperature egg whites on medium until foamy, then add the cream of tartar. Increase to medium-high and beat until soft, billowy peaks form.
4. With the mixer running, rain in the superfine sugar a tablespoon at a time, then beat to firm, glossy peaks that just hold their tip — do not whip to stiff and dry. Beat in the vanilla and almond extract.
5. Sift the flour mixture over the meringue in four additions, gently folding with a balloon whisk or spatula after each — fold just until no streaks remain, deflating the whites as little as possible.
6. Scrape the batter into the ungreased tube pan. Run a thin knife or skewer once through the batter in a circle to pop any large air pockets, then smooth the top.
7. Bake 35-40 minutes, until the top is golden, springs back when lightly pressed, and a skewer comes out clean. The cake should be tall and feel dry on top.
8. Immediately invert the pan onto its feet (or over the neck of a bottle) and let the cake cool completely upside down, about 1.5 hours — this stretches and sets the delicate crumb so it won't collapse.
9. Once fully cool, run a thin knife around the outer and inner edges and the bottom, then turn out. Cut with a serrated knife using a gentle sawing motion.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Jennifer — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-angel-food-cake/)</sub>

#### 🍎 [Apple Cobbler](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-apple-cobbler/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.98/serving** · 142 kcal · P2 · C32 · F2 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 6  

Tender cinnamon-spiced apples bubbling under a golden, buttery biscuit topping with crisp sugared edges. A cozy old-fashioned dessert that's far easier than pie and best served warm with a scoop of ice cream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Apple** — about 6 medium baking apples (Granny Smith + Honeycrisp), peeled and sliced
- **Brown sugar** — for the apple filling
- **Cinnamon** — split between filling and topping
- **Nutmeg** — 0.1 tsp _(optional)_
- **Lemon** — juice, to brighten the apples
- **Cornstarch** — to thicken the filling
- **All-purpose flour** — for the biscuit topping
- **Sugar** — for topping plus sprinkling
- **Baking powder** — 0.15 tsp
- **Salt** — 0.05 tsp
- **Butter** — cold for topping, plus a little for the filling
- **Milk** — cold, to bring the dough together
- **Vanilla extract** — 0.1 tsp _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 375F (190C) and lightly butter a 9x13 baking dish. Peel, core, and slice the apples about 1/4-inch thick.
2. In a large bowl, toss the apple slices with the brown sugar, lemon juice, cornstarch, half the cinnamon, and the nutmeg until evenly coated, then spread them into the baking dish and dot with a little butter.
3. Make the topping: whisk the flour, half the sugar, baking powder, salt, and remaining cinnamon in a bowl. Cut in the cold butter with your fingers or a fork until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs.
4. Stir in the milk and vanilla just until a soft, shaggy dough forms; do not overmix or the topping will turn tough.
5. Drop spoonfuls of the dough over the apples, leaving gaps so the filling can bubble through, then sprinkle the remaining sugar over the top for a crisp, sparkly crust.
6. Bake 40 to 45 minutes, until the topping is golden brown and the apple juices bubble thickly around the edges.
7. Let the cobbler rest at least 15 minutes so the filling sets, then serve warm, ideally with ice cream or a splash of cream.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Mark Pellegrini — CC BY-SA 2.5 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-apple-cobbler/)</sub>

#### 🫐 [Bakery-Style Blueberry Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-blueberry-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.91/serving** · 297 kcal · P4 · C41 · F13 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 12  

Tall, golden-domed blueberry muffins with a tender, buttery crumb, bursting with fresh berries and a crackly sugar top. Buttermilk and lemon zest keep them moist and bright, while a hot-then-moderate oven gives that signature bakery dome.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, about 2.5 cups; reserve 1 tbsp to toss with berries
- **Sugar** — about 1 cup, plus 2 tbsp coarse for tops
- **Butter** — softened, about 1 stick
- **Vegetable oil** — about 1/4 cup, for extra moisture
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Buttermilk** — about 1 cup
- **Blueberries** — about 2 cups fresh; frozen works unthawed
- **Baking powder** — about 2 tsp
- **Baking soda** — about 1/4 tsp
- **Salt** — about 3/4 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — about 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon, brightens the berries _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 425F (220C) and line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners or grease well. Toss the blueberries with the reserved 1 tbsp flour and set aside.
2. Whisk the remaining flour, baking powder, baking soda, and salt in a bowl. In a separate large bowl, cream the softened butter with the sugar and lemon zest until pale and fluffy, 2 to 3 minutes.
3. Beat in the oil, then the eggs one at a time, and finally the vanilla, scraping the bowl until smooth.
4. Add the dry ingredients and the buttermilk in three alternating additions, beginning and ending with the dry. Mix only until just combined; a few streaks of flour are fine. Do not overmix or the muffins will be tough.
5. Gently fold in the floured blueberries with a spatula, distributing them evenly without crushing.
6. Divide the batter among the cups, mounding it high so they nearly overflow for tall domes. Sprinkle the coarse sugar generously over each top.
7. Bake at 425F for 5 minutes, then without opening the oven reduce the temperature to 375F (190C) and bake 15 to 18 minutes more, until golden and a toothpick comes out with a few moist crumbs.
8. Cool in the tin for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. Serve slightly warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-blueberry-muffins/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Banana_cream_pie_%28cropped%29.jpg/480px-Banana_cream_pie_%28cropped%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Banana Cream Pie">

#### 🍌 [Banana Cream Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-banana-cream-pie/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.87/serving** · 220 kcal · P4 · C28 · F11 · ⏱ 285 min · 🍽 8  

A classic diner-style banana cream pie: a crisp blind-baked crust filled with a silky cooked vanilla pastry cream, layered over ripe banana slices, and crowned with billowy fresh whipped cream. Cold, creamy, and not too sweet.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Graham crackers** — about 1.5 cups crumbs for the crust
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp total: 5 melted for crust, plus a knob for the pastry cream
- **Sugar** — about 2/3 cup split between crust and pastry cream
- **Whole milk** — 2 cups for the pastry cream
- **Eggs** — use the yolks only for the custard
- **Cornstarch** — 1/4 cup to set the pastry cream
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp, divided between custard and whipped cream
- **Salt** — pinch for the custard
- **Banana** — ripe but firm, sliced
- **Heavy cream** — 1.5 cups for the whipped topping
- **Powdered sugar** — 3 tbsp to sweeten the whipped cream

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: crush the graham crackers into fine crumbs, stir with 5 tbsp melted butter and 2 tbsp sugar until it looks like wet sand, then press firmly into a 9-inch pie plate. Bake at 350F for 10 minutes and let cool completely.
2. Start the pastry cream: in a saucepan whisk together the remaining sugar, cornstarch, and a pinch of salt, then whisk in the cold milk until smooth and lump-free.
3. Temper the yolks: in a separate bowl whisk the 4 egg yolks. Bring the milk mixture to a gentle simmer over medium heat, whisking, then slowly ladle about half of it into the yolks while whisking, then pour the yolk mixture back into the pan.
4. Cook the custard: whisk constantly over medium heat until it bubbles and thickens to a pudding consistency, about 1-2 minutes after the first bubble. Off heat, whisk in 1 tbsp butter and 1 tsp vanilla.
5. Layer: spread a thin film of pastry cream over the cooled crust, arrange the sliced bananas in an even layer, then pour the remaining warm pastry cream over the top and smooth it flat.
6. Chill: press plastic wrap directly onto the surface of the custard to prevent a skin, and refrigerate at least 4 hours or until fully set.
7. Whip the topping: just before serving, beat the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar and remaining 1 tsp vanilla to soft-to-medium peaks.
8. Finish: spread or pipe the whipped cream over the chilled pie, slice with a warm knife, and serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Public domain · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-banana-cream-pie/)</sub>

#### 🍌 [Banana Nut Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-banana-nut-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.49/serving** · 258 kcal · P4 · C41 · F9 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 12  

Tender, bakery-style muffins made with very ripe bananas and toasted walnuts. Brown sugar and a touch of cinnamon give deep caramel flavor, while melted butter and a gentle hand keep the crumb moist and soft with a high, domed top.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Banana** — very ripe, heavily speckled; about 1.5 cups mashed
- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, about 1.75 cups
- **Brown sugar** — packed, about 3/4 cup
- **White sugar** — about 1/4 cup
- **Butter** — melted, about 1/2 cup (1 stick)
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Walnuts** — toasted and chopped, about 3/4 cup plus some for topping
- **Vanilla extract** — about 1.5 tsp
- **Baking soda** — about 1 tsp
- **Baking powder** — about 1/2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — about 1 tsp
- **Salt** — about 1/2 tsp
- **Greek yogurt** — about 1/4 cup, for extra moisture and tang _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 425F (220C) and line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners. Spread the walnuts on a small tray and toast for 5-6 minutes until fragrant, then chop; reserve a small handful for topping.
2. In a bowl, mash the very ripe bananas with a fork until mostly smooth with a few small lumps. Whisk in the melted butter, brown sugar, white sugar, eggs, Greek yogurt if using, and vanilla until well combined.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, baking powder, cinnamon, and salt.
4. Pour the dry ingredients into the wet and fold gently with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain. Do not overmix or the muffins will turn tough; a few lumps are fine. Fold in the chopped toasted walnuts.
5. Divide the batter evenly among the 12 liners, filling each nearly to the top for tall domes. Scatter the reserved walnuts over the tops.
6. Bake at 425F for 5 minutes, then, without opening the oven, reduce the temperature to 350F (175C) and bake another 15-18 minutes. The initial blast of heat sets a high domed top.
7. Muffins are done when the tops spring back and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out with only a few moist crumbs.
8. Cool in the tin for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. Serve warm or fully cooled.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Famartin — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-banana-nut-muffins/)</sub>

#### 🧁 [Bran Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-bran-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.35/serving** · 190 kcal · P3 · C28 · F7 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 12  

Tender, deeply molasses-scented bran muffins with a moist crumb and plump raisins. Soaking the wheat bran in buttermilk before mixing softens it for a bakery-style texture that never tastes dry or sawdusty.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Rolled oats** — wheat bran (unprocessed miller's bran) — soaked in buttermilk
- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, about 1.5 cups
- **Buttermilk** — 1.5 cups; soaks the bran
- **Brown sugar** — packed light brown sugar, about 3/4 cup
- **Molasses** — unsulphured (mild) molasses, 1/4 cup
- **Vegetable oil** — neutral oil, 1/3 cup
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Baking soda** — 1.5 tsp
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp
- **Raisins** — about 1 cup _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, stir the wheat bran into the buttermilk and let it soak for 15 minutes so the bran softens and hydrates — this is the key to a moist, tender crumb.
2. Heat the oven to 400F (205C) and line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners or grease it well.
3. Whisk the brown sugar, molasses, oil, eggs, and vanilla into the soaked bran mixture until smooth and well combined.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, baking powder, salt, and cinnamon.
5. Add the dry ingredients to the wet and fold with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — do not overmix or the muffins will turn tough and tunneled.
6. Gently fold in the raisins, then divide the batter evenly among the 12 cups, filling each nearly to the top.
7. Bake for 18 to 22 minutes, until the tops dome and spring back and a toothpick comes out clean.
8. Cool in the tin for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack; serve warm with butter.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Joey Doll — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-bran-muffins/)</sub>

#### 🍞 [Bread Machine White Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-bread-machine-white-bread/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.14/serving** · 151 kcal · P4 · C27 · F3 · ⏱ 215 min · 🍽 12  

A soft, golden 1.5 lb sandwich loaf made start-to-finish in a bread machine. Warm milk and butter give it a tender, fine crumb while a touch of sugar feeds the yeast for a tall, even rise. Just layer the ingredients in the right order, press Basic, and walk away.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Milk** — 1 cup + 2 tbsp whole milk, warmed to ~110F (lukewarm)
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp unsalted butter, softened and cut into pieces
- **Sugar** — 2 tbsp granulated sugar
- **Salt** — 1.5 tsp fine salt
- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups bread flour (sub all-purpose in a pinch)
- **Instant yeast** — 2.25 tsp (1 packet) instant / bread machine yeast

**Steps**

1. Pour the warm milk (about 110F, just warm to the touch) into the bread machine pan first, then add the softened butter pieces. Starting with the wet ingredients keeps the dough from clumping at the bottom.
2. Add the sugar and salt to opposite corners of the pan, keeping the salt away from where the yeast will go, since direct contact with salt can kill the yeast.
3. Spoon the bread flour evenly over the liquid so it covers the entire surface, then gently smooth it into a flat layer that fully blankets the wet ingredients.
4. Make a small well in the center of the flour with your finger, making sure it does not reach down to the liquid, and pour the instant yeast into the dry well so it stays separated from the liquid until mixing begins.
5. Lock the pan into the machine and select the Basic / White cycle with a medium crust setting for a 1.5 lb loaf, then press Start.
6. Check the dough about 5 minutes into the first knead: it should form a smooth, slightly tacky ball that clears the sides of the pan. Add flour 1 tablespoon at a time if too sticky, or warm milk 1 teaspoon at a time if too dry.
7. Let the full cycle run undisturbed (roughly 3 to 4 hours including rises and bake); avoid opening the lid during rising and baking so the loaf does not deflate.
8. When the cycle finishes, immediately turn the loaf out onto a wire rack and remove the kneading paddle from the bottom. Let it cool at least 30 minutes before slicing so the crumb sets and does not gum up.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: User Sannse on en.wikipedia — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-bread-machine-white-bread/)</sub>

#### 🍯 [Brown Butter Blondies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-blondies/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.69/serving** · 260 kcal · P3 · C35 · F12 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 16  

Dense, chewy, butterscotch-rich blondies built on browned butter and dark brown sugar, studded with chocolate chips and toasted walnuts. Crackly top, gooey center, and that deep toffee flavor a real blondie is all about — no cocoa, no cake-y crumb.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup / 2 sticks, browned
- **Brown sugar** — 1.5 cups packed dark brown sugar
- **White sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs plus 1 yolk, room temp
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp fine salt
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 1 cup
- **Walnuts** — 3/4 cup, toasted and chopped _(optional)_
- **Flaky sea salt (Maldon-style)** — for finishing _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350F (175C). Line an 8x8 or 9x9 metal pan with parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides, and lightly grease.
2. Melt the butter in a light-colored saucepan over medium heat, swirling often. Keep cooking past the foaming stage until it smells nutty and the milk solids turn golden-brown, about 5-7 minutes. Pour into a large bowl (scrape in all the browned bits) and let cool 5-10 minutes until just warm.
3. Whisk the dark brown sugar and granulated sugar into the warm butter until smooth and glossy. Add the eggs, extra yolk, and vanilla, then whisk vigorously for about 1 minute until the batter lightens slightly and looks emulsified.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt. Add to the wet ingredients and fold with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — do not overmix.
5. Fold in the chocolate chips and toasted walnuts, reserving a small handful to press on top.
6. Scrape the thick batter into the pan and smooth into an even layer. Scatter the reserved chips and nuts over the top.
7. Bake 24-28 minutes, until the top is set and golden with a few cracks and a toothpick in the center comes out with a few moist crumbs (not wet batter). The center should still look slightly underdone — that's the gooey texture.
8. Sprinkle with flaky salt while warm. Cool completely in the pan, at least 1 hour, then lift out by the parchment and cut into 16 squares with a sharp knife.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Wheeler Cowperthwaite — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-blondies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Cherry_Turnovers_split_and_whole_%2836051372664%29.jpg/480px-Cherry_Turnovers_split_and_whole_%2836051372664%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Cherry Turnovers">

#### 🍒 [Cherry Turnovers](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cherry-turnovers/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.80/serving** · 90 kcal · P2 · C18 · F1 · ⏱ 60 min · 🍽 4  

Flaky, golden puff pastry triangles bursting with a glossy, jammy cherry filling brightened with lemon and almond, finished with a simple vanilla glaze. Bakery-style hand pies you can make on a weeknight.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Refrigerated pizza dough** — 1 sheet thawed all-butter puff pastry (pizza-dough is the closest catalog id; use real puff pastry)
- **Frozen cherries** — about 2.5 cups pitted dark sweet cherries, halved
- **Sugar** — about 1/3 cup, adjust to cherry sweetness
- **Cornstarch** — 1 tbsp, slurried
- **Lemon** — juice of half plus 1/2 tsp zest
- **Almond extract** — 1/4 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp, split between filling and glaze
- **Eggs** — 1 egg for egg wash
- **Powdered sugar** — about 1/2 cup for the glaze
- **Milk** — 1-2 tsp to loosen glaze
- **Cinnamon** — pinch in the filling _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a saucepan, combine the cherries, sugar, lemon juice, lemon zest and a pinch of cinnamon. Cook over medium heat, stirring, until the cherries release their juices and bubble, 5-7 minutes.
2. Stir the cornstarch into 1 tbsp cold water to make a slurry, then stir it into the simmering cherries. Cook 1-2 minutes until thick and glossy, then off the heat stir in the almond extract and 1/4 tsp vanilla. Spread on a plate and chill until cool and set, about 20 minutes.
3. Heat the oven to 400F and line a sheet pan with parchment. Roll the thawed puff pastry on a lightly floured surface into a roughly 10-inch square and cut into 4 equal squares.
4. Beat the egg with a splash of water for the egg wash. Brush the edges of each square, then spoon the cooled cherry filling onto one half of each, leaving a 1/2-inch border. Do not overfill.
5. Fold each square corner-to-corner into a triangle, press the edges firmly, then crimp tightly with a fork to seal so the filling does not leak.
6. Brush the tops with egg wash and cut 2-3 small steam vents in each turnover with a sharp knife.
7. Bake 20-25 minutes until deeply golden and puffed. Let cool on the pan 10 minutes so the filling sets and the bottoms crisp.
8. Whisk the powdered sugar with the remaining vanilla and just enough milk to make a thick but pourable glaze. Drizzle over the cooled turnovers and let set before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Willis Lam — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cherry-turnovers/)</sub>

#### 🥧 [Chocolate Cream Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-chocolate-cream-pie/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.16/serving** · 415 kcal · P7 · C42 · F26 · ⏱ 300 min · 🍽 8  

A diner-classic chocolate cream pie: a buttery graham crust filled with a rich, silky cooked chocolate custard thickened with egg yolks and cornstarch, then crowned with billows of fresh whipped cream and chocolate shavings.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Graham crackers** — about 12 full sheets, crushed for the crust
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp total: 5 melted for crust, 1 for custard
- **Sugar** — about 3/4 cup for custard plus 2 tbsp for crust
- **Cornstarch** — about 1/4 cup, the primary thickener
- **Cocoa powder** — about 1/3 cup unsweetened cocoa powder
- **Salt** — 0.05 tsp
- **Whole milk** — 3 cups for the custard base
- **Eggs** — yolks only, tempered into the custard
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — about 4 oz, melted into the hot custard
- **Vanilla extract** — 1.5 tsp total, split between custard and cream
- **Heavy cream** — about 1.5 cups, whipped for the topping
- **Powdered sugar** — about 3 tbsp to sweeten the whipped cream
- **Dark chocolate** — shaved over the top to garnish _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: stir crushed graham crackers with 2 tbsp sugar and 5 tbsp melted butter until the texture of wet sand, then press firmly up the sides and across the bottom of a 9-inch pie plate. Bake at 350F for 10 minutes and cool completely (or chill 20 minutes for a no-bake crust).
2. Whisk the sugar, cornstarch, cocoa powder, and salt together in a saucepan, breaking up any lumps. Slowly whisk in the cold milk until completely smooth so no dry pockets remain.
3. Whisk the 4 egg yolks in a separate bowl. Set the saucepan over medium heat and cook, whisking constantly, until the mixture just begins to thicken and steam, about 5 to 7 minutes.
4. Temper the yolks: ladle about 1 cup of the hot cocoa mixture into the yolks while whisking hard, then pour the warmed yolks back into the saucepan. Cook, whisking constantly, until the custard thickly bubbles and coats the back of a spoon, 1 to 2 minutes more.
5. Off the heat, whisk in the semi-sweet chocolate, 1 tbsp butter, and 1 tsp vanilla until glossy and smooth. Pour into the cooled crust and smooth the top.
6. Press a piece of plastic wrap directly onto the surface of the filling to prevent a skin, then refrigerate until fully set, at least 4 hours or overnight.
7. Whip the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar and remaining 1/2 tsp vanilla to soft-to-medium peaks. Spread or pipe over the chilled pie.
8. Shave the dark chocolate over the top with a peeler, slice with a hot dry knife, and serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: jeffreyw — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-chocolate-cream-pie/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Chocolate_Crinkle_Cookies_%28home_cooking%29.JPG/480px-Chocolate_Crinkle_Cookies_%28home_cooking%29.JPG" alt="Photo of Chocolate Crinkle Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Chocolate Crinkle Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-chocolate-crinkle-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.29/serving** · 158 kcal · P2 · C25 · F6 · ⏱ 285 min · 🍽 24  

Fudgy, brownie-like chocolate cookies rolled in powdered sugar that cracks into a snowy crinkle pattern as they bake. The dough is chilled so it bakes up thick and chewy with crisp, sugar-dusted edges.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Cocoa powder** — 1 cup unsweetened cocoa powder
- **White sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/2 cup neutral oil
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup confectioners' sugar for rolling

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, whisk together the cocoa powder, granulated sugar, and oil until it forms a thick, sandy paste.
2. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then stir in the vanilla, mixing until the batter is smooth and glossy.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt, then fold the dry ingredients into the wet until a soft, sticky dough forms.
4. Cover the dough and chill in the refrigerator for at least 4 hours (or overnight) so it firms up enough to roll and holds its crinkle shape.
5. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper. Put the powdered sugar in a shallow bowl.
6. Scoop tablespoon-sized portions of cold dough, roll into balls, then roll each generously in powdered sugar until fully coated with a thick layer.
7. Place the balls 2 inches apart on the sheets and bake for 10 to 12 minutes, until the tops are cracked and the edges are set but the centers still look slightly soft.
8. Let the cookies cool on the pan for 5 minutes to finish setting, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely.

</details>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/54/Coffee_cake_%28Unsplash%29.jpg/480px-Coffee_cake_%28Unsplash%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Cinnamon Crumb Coffee Cake">

#### 🍰 [Cinnamon Crumb Coffee Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-coffee-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.05/serving** · 489 kcal · P6 · C67 · F22 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 9  

A tender, buttery sour-cream coffee cake with a ribbon of cinnamon-walnut swirl baked through the middle and a thick, craggy brown-sugar streusel crown. No coffee in the cake itself — it's the classic cake you serve WITH coffee, moist for days and impossible to stop at one slice.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, ~2.25 cups total split between batter and streusel
- **Sugar** — granulated, ~1 cup for the batter
- **Brown sugar** — packed, ~3/4 cup split between swirl and streusel
- **Butter** — unsalted, ~12 tbsp split between batter and streusel
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Sour cream** — full-fat, ~1 cup — keeps the crumb moist and tender
- **Vanilla extract** — vanilla extract, ~2 tsp
- **Baking powder** — ~1.5 tsp
- **Baking soda** — ~1/2 tsp
- **Salt** — ~1/2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — ~2.5 tbsp split between swirl and streusel
- **Walnuts** — chopped, for the swirl and topping _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Butter an 8x8 or 9x9 baking pan and line with a parchment sling. Make the streusel first: rub 6 tbsp melted butter into 3/4 cup flour, 1/2 cup brown sugar, 1.5 tsp cinnamon, and a pinch of salt with your fingers until it forms moist, craggy clumps; stir in half the chopped walnuts and chill while you mix the cake.
2. Make the cinnamon swirl: stir together 1/4 cup brown sugar, 1 tbsp cinnamon, and the remaining walnuts in a small bowl. Set aside.
3. Whisk the remaining 1.5 cups flour with the baking powder, baking soda, and salt in a bowl. In a separate large bowl, beat the remaining 6 tbsp softened butter with the granulated sugar until pale and fluffy, 2-3 minutes.
4. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla, scraping the bowl. The mixture should look smooth and light.
5. Add the flour mixture in three additions alternating with the sour cream, beginning and ending with flour. Mix on low just until no dry streaks remain — overmixing makes the cake tough. The batter will be thick.
6. Spread half the batter in the pan in an even layer. Scatter all of the cinnamon swirl mixture over it, then dollop and gently spread the remaining batter on top to cover.
7. Squeeze the chilled streusel into clumps and scatter it thickly and evenly over the surface, pressing lightly so it adheres.
8. Bake 40-50 minutes, until the top is deep golden and a toothpick inserted into the center comes out with just a few moist crumbs. If the streusel browns too fast, tent loosely with foil.
9. Cool in the pan at least 30 minutes before lifting out by the sling and slicing into 9 squares — it firms up and slices cleanly as it cools.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-coffee-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Pecan_%26_cinnamon_crumb_cake_-_GAIL%27s_2025-03-10.jpg/480px-Pecan_%26_cinnamon_crumb_cake_-_GAIL%27s_2025-03-10.jpg" alt="Photo of Cinnamon Streusel Coffee Cake">

#### 🍰 [Cinnamon Streusel Coffee Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cinnamon-streusel-coffee-cake/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.85/serving** · 451 kcal · P4 · C56 · F23 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, buttery sour cream coffee cake with a thick cinnamon-brown sugar streusel both layered through the middle and crowning the top. This is the real bakery-style version: moist crumb from sour cream, a generous ribbon of cinnamon streusel, and a crackly streusel lid. Best served slightly warm with coffee.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour (2 cups for cake, 1/2 cup for streusel)
- **Butter** — 1 1/4 cups unsalted butter, softened (1/2 cup for cake, 3/4 cup cold cubed for streusel)
- **White sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (for the cake batter)
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed light brown sugar (for the streusel)
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Sour cream** — 1 cup full-fat sour cream
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Cinnamon** — 4 tsp ground cinnamon (for the streusel)
- **Pecans** — 3 oz chopped pecans, about 3/4 cup (optional, for the streusel) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C). Grease a 9x13-inch baking pan and line the bottom with parchment, or generously butter a tube/bundt pan. Set out the eggs and sour cream to take the chill off.
2. Make the streusel first: in a bowl, whisk together 1/2 cup flour, the brown sugar, cinnamon, and a pinch of salt. Add 3/4 cup cold cubed butter and rub it in with your fingertips (or cut with a fork) until you have moist, clumpy crumbs ranging from pea-size to small gravel. Stir in the chopped pecans if using. Refrigerate while you make the batter so the clumps hold.
3. Whisk the remaining 2 cups flour with the baking powder, baking soda, and 1/2 tsp salt in a medium bowl.
4. In a large bowl, beat the 1/2 cup softened butter with the granulated sugar on medium-high until pale and fluffy, about 3 minutes. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla, scraping the bowl as needed.
5. On low speed, add the flour mixture in three additions alternating with the sour cream, beginning and ending with flour. Mix just until no dry streaks remain; the batter will be thick. Do not overmix.
6. Spread half the batter evenly in the pan. Scatter a little over half the streusel across the surface to form the middle ribbon. Dollop and gently spread the remaining batter on top, then cover completely with the rest of the streusel, pressing it lightly so it adheres.
7. Bake 38 to 45 minutes (a 9x13) or 50 to 60 minutes (a tube pan), until the top is deep golden, the streusel is set, and a toothpick inserted into the center comes out clean or with a few moist crumbs.
8. Cool in the pan on a rack at least 20 minutes before cutting. Serve warm or at room temperature. Store covered at room temperature up to 3 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cinnamon-streusel-coffee-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d0/Beer_bread_-_loaf_sliced_in_half.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Beer Bread">

#### 🍞 [Classic Beer Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-beer-bread/)
American · dinner · easy  
**$0.29/serving** · 160 kcal · P3 · C27 · F4 · ⏱ 65 min · 🍽 12  

A rustic, no-knead American quick bread with a crackly buttered crust and a tender, faintly malty crumb. Beer does double duty here — its carbonation and yeast give lift and flavor while baking powder guarantees a reliable rise, so there's no proofing and no kneading. Mix, pour, butter the top, and bake. It comes out somewhere between a soft sandwich loaf and a savory biscuit, perfect warm with soup or chili.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 4 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1 1/2 tsp salt
- **Sugar** — 3 tbsp granulated sugar
- **Beer (cooking)** — 12 oz (1 bottle) beer, room temperature — a lager or amber ale
- **Butter** — 1/4 cup (4 tbsp) unsalted butter, melted, for the top

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 375 F (190 C). Grease a 9x5-inch loaf pan thoroughly with butter or line it with parchment, leaving an overhang on the long sides for easy lifting.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, salt, and sugar until evenly combined. Whisking the dry ingredients well is what gives you an even rise, since there's no kneading to distribute the leavening.
3. Pour in the room-temperature beer all at once. Stir with a sturdy spoon or spatula just until no dry streaks of flour remain — the batter will be thick, sticky, and shaggy. Do not overmix; a few lumps are exactly right and keep the crumb tender.
4. Scrape the batter into the prepared loaf pan and smooth the top roughly with the back of the spoon. Pour about half of the melted butter evenly over the surface — it will pool and sink into the cracks as the bread bakes, creating the signature crisp, golden crust.
5. Bake for 30 minutes, then pull the loaf out and drizzle the remaining melted butter over the top. Return to the oven and bake another 15 to 25 minutes, until the top is deep golden brown and a skewer inserted in the center comes out clean. Total bake time is 45 to 55 minutes.
6. An instant-read thermometer should register about 200 to 205 F (93 to 96 C) in the center when it's fully baked. If the top browns too fast before the inside is done, tent loosely with foil.
7. Let the loaf cool in the pan for 10 minutes, then lift it out using the parchment overhang (or run a knife around the edge) and transfer to a wire rack. Cool at least 15 more minutes before slicing — cutting too early makes the crumb gummy.
8. Slice thick and serve warm. It's best the day it's baked, ideal alongside soup, chili, or stew, or simply with more butter.

</details>

<sub>📷 Mark Bonica, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-beer-bread/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Toffee_blondies.jpg/480px-Toffee_blondies.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Congo Bars">

#### 🍫 [Classic Congo Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-congo-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.71/serving** · 293 kcal · P4 · C46 · F11 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 16  

Buttery brown-sugar blondie bars loaded with chocolate chips and toasted walnuts — the New England church-cookbook classic. A chewy, caramel-edged cousin of the chocolate chip cookie, baked in a single pan and cut into squares. No mixer required.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 3/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 2 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, melted
- **Brown sugar** — 2 1/4 cups packed light brown sugar (about 33 tbsp)
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 1 cup (about 16 tbsp) semi-sweet chocolate chips
- **Walnuts** — 1/4 cup chopped walnuts, toasted _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Line a 9x13-inch baking pan with parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides, and lightly butter the parchment. If using walnuts, toast them in the dry oven for 6-8 minutes until fragrant, then chop.
2. Whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt in a medium bowl and set aside.
3. In a large bowl, whisk the melted butter and brown sugar together until smooth and glossy, about 1 minute. The mixture should look like wet sand with no dry lumps.
4. Whisk in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla, beating well after each addition until the batter is thick, shiny, and ribbon-like.
5. Switch to a sturdy spatula and fold in the flour mixture just until no dry streaks remain — do not overmix or the bars turn tough. Fold in the chocolate chips and toasted walnuts.
6. Scrape the thick batter into the prepared pan and spread it into an even layer, pushing into the corners. Scatter a few extra chocolate chips on top if you like.
7. Bake for 28-32 minutes, until the top is golden and set with a few cracks at the edges and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out with a few moist crumbs (not wet batter). The center should still look slightly soft — it firms as it cools.
8. Cool completely in the pan on a wire rack, at least 1 hour, before lifting out by the parchment and cutting into 16 squares. For ultra-clean edges, chill 20 minutes before slicing.

</details>

<sub>📷 Bryan Ochalla, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-congo-bars/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Special:FilePath/Corn_muffins.jpg?width=1024" alt="Photo of Classic Corn Muffins">

#### 🌽 [Classic Corn Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-corn-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.31/serving** · 180 kcal · P3 · C24 · F8 · ⏱ 35 min · 🍽 12  

Tender, golden corn muffins with a real cornbread crumb — sweet but not cakey, with crisp tops and a moist, buttery interior. This is the diner-style classic: a true 1:1 cornmeal-to-flour ratio, buttermilk for tang and lift, and melted butter for richness. Mixed by hand and baked hot so they dome.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Cornmeal (polenta)** — 1 1/2 cups yellow cornmeal, medium grind
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar
- **Baking powder** — 4 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1 tsp salt
- **Buttermilk** — 1 1/4 cups buttermilk
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, melted and slightly cooled
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp honey _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 400 F. Line a 12-cup standard muffin tin with paper liners or grease the wells well with butter or cooking spray.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the cornmeal, flour, sugar, baking powder, baking soda, and salt until evenly combined and no lumps remain.
3. In a separate medium bowl, whisk the eggs until well beaten, then whisk in the buttermilk and honey. Whisk in the melted butter last, pouring it in a slow stream while whisking so it emulsifies rather than seizing into clumps.
4. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry. Using a rubber spatula, fold just until no dry streaks remain — the batter should look thick and slightly lumpy. Do not overmix or the muffins will turn tough and tunnel.
5. Let the batter rest 5 minutes; this hydrates the cornmeal and gives a more tender crumb. Meanwhile the oven finishes heating.
6. Divide the batter evenly among the 12 wells, filling each about three-quarters full. For tall, domed tops, mound the batter slightly in the center.
7. Bake at 400 F for 16 to 20 minutes, until the tops are golden and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean. The edges should pull slightly from the pan.
8. Let the muffins cool in the tin for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack. Serve warm with butter and honey; they are best the day they are baked.

</details>

<sub>📷 Sharon from Sydney, Australia, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-corn-muffins/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Cowboy_Cookies.jpg/480px-Cowboy_Cookies.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Cowboy Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Classic Cowboy Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cowboy-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.95/serving** · 204 kcal · P2 · C30 · F8 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 24  

The real-deal Texas-style cowboy cookie: a big, chewy, hearty oatmeal cookie loaded with semisweet chocolate chips, toasted pecans, and shredded coconut. Crisp at the edges, soft in the middle, with deep brown-sugar-and-cinnamon flavor. This is the bake-sale-winning, lunchbox-stuffing version a confident home baker makes from memory.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Rolled oats** — 2 cups old-fashioned rolled oats
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 2 cups (12 oz) semisweet chocolate chips
- **Pecans** — 1 cup chopped pecans, toasted (about 4 oz)
- **Coconut flakes** — 1 cup sweetened shredded coconut

**Steps**

1. Position racks in the upper and lower thirds of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Line two baking sheets with parchment paper. If your pecans aren't already toasted, spread them on a dry sheet and toast for 6-8 minutes until fragrant, then cool and roughly chop.
2. In a medium bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, baking powder, cinnamon, and salt. Set aside.
3. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter with both sugars on medium speed for 3-4 minutes until light and fluffy. This aeration is what gives the cookies their lift, so don't rush it.
4. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla, scraping down the bowl after each addition until fully combined.
5. Add the dry ingredients and mix on low just until no streaks of flour remain. Do not overmix. By hand, fold in the oats, chocolate chips, toasted pecans, and coconut until evenly distributed; the dough will be thick and chunky.
6. Scoop generous 2-tablespoon mounds (about 2 oz each) and space them 2 inches apart on the prepared sheets. For tidier cookies, roll into balls; for craggy bakery-style edges, leave them rough.
7. Bake 12-14 minutes, rotating the sheets top-to-bottom and front-to-back halfway through, until the edges are set and golden but the centers still look slightly underdone.
8. Let the cookies rest on the hot sheet for 5 minutes to finish setting, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely. They keep in an airtight container for up to 5 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Rex Roof, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cowboy-cookies/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Decorated_sugar_cookies.jpg/480px-Decorated_sugar_cookies.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Cut-Out Sugar Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Classic Cut-Out Sugar Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cut-out-sugar-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.23/serving** · 161 kcal · P2 · C21 · F8 · ⏱ 105 min · 🍽 24  

Crisp-edged, tender-centered roll-and-cut sugar cookies that hold their shape with sharp, defined edges — no spreading, no puffing. The dough is sturdy enough to roll and cut yet rich with butter and vanilla, the kind you decorate with royal icing for the holidays. The secret is plenty of butter, a touch of almond extract, and chilling so the shapes stay crisp.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour, plus more for rolling
- **Baking powder** — 3/4 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 1/2 tsp pure vanilla extract
- **Almond extract** — 1/2 tsp almond extract

**Steps**

1. In a medium bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt. Set aside.
2. In a large bowl with a hand mixer (or a stand mixer fitted with the paddle), beat the softened butter and sugar on medium-high for 2 to 3 minutes until pale and fluffy. Beat in the egg, then the vanilla and almond extract, scraping down the bowl as needed.
3. Add the flour mixture in two additions, mixing on low just until a soft dough comes together and no dry streaks remain. Do not overmix.
4. Divide the dough in half and shape each piece into a flat disk. Roll each disk to about 1/4 inch thick between two sheets of parchment (this avoids adding extra flour). Slide the rolled slabs onto a baking sheet and chill in the refrigerator for at least 1 hour, until firm.
5. Position racks in the upper and lower thirds of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Line two baking sheets with parchment.
6. Working with one chilled slab at a time, peel off the parchment and cut out shapes with cookie cutters. Transfer to the lined sheets, spacing about 1 inch apart. Re-roll and chill scraps as needed. For the sharpest edges, chill the cut shapes on the sheet for 10 minutes before baking.
7. Bake 9 to 11 minutes, rotating the sheets halfway through, until the cookies are set and the edges are just barely golden (pull them before they brown for soft cookies, a touch longer for crisp).
8. Let the cookies cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely before decorating with royal icing or glaze.

</details>

<sub>📷 Julie Anne Workman, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cut-out-sugar-cookies/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Homemade_Confetti_Cake.jpg/480px-Homemade_Confetti_Cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Funfetti Layer Cake">

#### 🎂 [Classic Funfetti Layer Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-funfetti-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.48/serving** · 581 kcal · P5 · C51 · F40 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, fluffy white birthday cake studded with rainbow sprinkles and finished with a silky vanilla buttercream. Made the proper way — with the creaming method, egg whites only for a snowy crumb, and just enough almond extract to give it that nostalgic boxed-mix flavor, only better. The hallmark is using jimmies (the long rod sprinkles), which hold their color and don't bleed into the batter.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups cake flour (or all-purpose; see substitutions)
- **Baking powder** — 2 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 3/4 cups granulated sugar
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/4 cup vegetable oil
- **Eggs** — 4 large egg whites, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tbsp pure vanilla extract
- **Almond extract** — 1/2 tsp almond extract
- **Buttermilk** — 1 cup buttermilk, room temperature
- **Chocolate chips** — 3/4 cup rainbow sprinkles (jimmies), plus more for topping — use the catalog 'chocolate-chips' id as the closest small-bake-add-in
- **Butter** — FROSTING: 1 1/2 cups (3 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — FROSTING: 5 cups powdered sugar
- **Heavy cream** — FROSTING: 1/4 cup heavy cream
- **Vanilla extract** — FROSTING: 2 tsp pure vanilla extract
- **Salt** — FROSTING: 1/4 tsp salt

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (177C). Grease two 9-inch round cake pans, line the bottoms with parchment rounds, then grease and flour the pans. Whisk the cake flour, baking powder, and salt together in a bowl and set aside.
2. In a large bowl with a hand or stand mixer, beat the softened butter, sugar, and oil on high until light, pale, and fluffy, about 3 minutes. Scrape down the bowl.
3. Add the egg whites one at a time, beating well after each, then beat in the vanilla and almond extract until smooth and creamy. The mixture should be airy — this is what gives the cake its lift.
4. With the mixer on low, add the dry ingredients in three additions alternating with the buttermilk in two additions, beginning and ending with the dry. Mix just until combined and no flour streaks remain — do not overmix, or the cake will turn tough.
5. Gently fold in the rainbow sprinkles by hand with a spatula, working quickly so the colors don't bleed into the batter. Divide the batter evenly between the two prepared pans and smooth the tops.
6. Bake for 23 to 26 minutes, until a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean and the tops spring back when lightly pressed. Cool in the pans for 10 minutes, then turn out onto a wire rack and cool completely before frosting.
7. Make the buttercream: beat the softened butter on medium-high for 2 minutes until creamy. Add the powdered sugar 1 cup at a time on low, then add the heavy cream, vanilla, and salt. Beat on high for 3 minutes until light and spreadable; add a splash more cream if too thick.
8. Assemble: level the cake tops if domed. Set one layer on a plate, spread with about 1 cup of frosting, top with the second layer, then frost the top and sides. Press extra sprinkles around the bottom edge and over the top, then chill 20 minutes to set before slicing.

</details>

<sub>📷 Ich, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-funfetti-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4d/Grasshopper_pie_%2814105460720%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Grasshopper Pie">

#### 🥧 [Classic Grasshopper Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-grasshopper-pie/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.68/serving** · 178 kcal · P2 · C17 · F10 · ⏱ 300 min · 🍽 8  

The retro icebox classic done right: a crisp baked chocolate-cookie crust filled with an airy, pale-mint marshmallow-and-cream filling spiked with creme de menthe and white creme de cacao. The crust is baked to set it and deepen its flavor, then the no-cook filling is folded together and chilled until sliceable. Cool, minty, and just boozy enough.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Oreo cookies** — 24 chocolate sandwich cookies (Oreos), for the crust
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp unsalted butter, melted
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Gelatin (unflavored)** — 1 packet (2 1/4 tsp) unflavored gelatin
- **Water** — 1/4 cup cold water, to bloom the gelatin
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large egg yolks
- **Crème de menthe (green)** — 3 tbsp green creme de menthe
- **Rum** — 1 1/2 tbsp white creme de cacao (or substitute light rum)
- **Heavy cream** — 1 1/4 cups cold heavy cream, whipped
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Dark chocolate** — 1/2 oz dark chocolate, for shaved garnish _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Pulse the chocolate sandwich cookies (cream filling and all) into fine crumbs in a food processor, or crush them in a zip-top bag with a rolling pin. You should have about 2 cups of crumbs. Stir in the salt, then drizzle in the melted butter and mix until the crumbs look like wet sand.
2. Press the crumbs firmly and evenly up the sides and across the bottom of a 9-inch pie plate, using the flat bottom of a measuring cup to compact them. Bake for 10 minutes to set the crust, then cool completely on a rack while you make the filling. The crust must be cold before filling goes in.
3. Bloom the gelatin: Sprinkle the gelatin over the 1/4 cup cold water in a small bowl and let it sit 5 minutes until it looks wrinkled and absorbs the water.
4. Make the custard base: In a saucepan, whisk the milk, sugar, and egg yolks together. Cook over medium-low heat, stirring constantly with a spatula, until it thickens slightly and coats the back of the spoon (about 170F), 4 to 6 minutes. Do not let it boil or the yolks will scramble. Off the heat, stir in the bloomed gelatin until fully dissolved.
5. Strain the custard through a fine-mesh sieve into a large bowl. Stir in the creme de menthe, creme de cacao, and vanilla. The mixture will be pale green. Chill in the refrigerator, stirring every 10 minutes, until cool and thickened to the texture of raw egg whites but not set, 25 to 40 minutes. If it sets too far, warm the bowl briefly and whisk smooth.
6. Whip the cream: In a chilled bowl, whip the cold heavy cream to soft-to-medium peaks. Fold about a third of the whipped cream into the cooled mint base to lighten it, then gently fold in the rest in two additions until no streaks remain, keeping the mixture airy.
7. Pour the filling into the cooled crust and smooth the top into a gentle dome. Cover loosely and refrigerate at least 4 hours, preferably overnight, until fully set and sliceable.
8. To serve, shave dark chocolate over the top with a vegetable peeler. Slice with a knife dipped in hot water and wiped between cuts for clean wedges.

</details>

<sub>📷 Julia Payne from Manchester, USA, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-grasshopper-pie/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Glazed_Lemon_Bundt_Cake.jpg/480px-Glazed_Lemon_Bundt_Cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Lemon Bundt Cake">

#### 🍋 [Classic Lemon Bundt Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-lemon-bundt-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.29/serving** · 487 kcal · P6 · C70 · F21 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, buttery sour-cream Bundt cake perfumed with fresh lemon zest and juice, soaked while warm with a tart lemon syrup and finished with a sharp lemon glaze. Dense-but-moist crumb, bright and not too sweet — the kind of cake that gets better on day two.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 2 1/2 cups granulated sugar (2 cups for batter, 1/2 cup for syrup)
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, room temperature
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 3 lemons (about 3 tbsp)
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Sour cream** — 1 cup full-fat sour cream
- **Lemon** — 4 lemons (for ~1/2 cup juice across batter, syrup, and glaze)
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 1/2 cups powdered sugar (for the glaze)

**Steps**

1. Set a rack in the lower-middle of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Generously brush a 10- to 12-cup Bundt pan with softened butter, getting into every crevice, then dust with flour and tap out the excess. A well-greased pan is the difference between a clean release and a disaster.
2. Whisk together the flour, baking powder, baking soda, and salt in a medium bowl. In a separate small bowl, rub the lemon zest into 2 cups of the sugar with your fingertips until fragrant and damp — this releases the citrus oils and is what makes the cake taste deeply of lemon.
3. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter with the lemon sugar on medium-high for 3 to 4 minutes until pale and fluffy. Add the eggs one at a time, beating well after each, then beat in the vanilla and 2 tablespoons of fresh lemon juice. The mixture may look slightly curdled — that's fine.
4. On low speed, add the dry ingredients in three additions alternating with the sour cream (begin and end with flour), mixing just until each addition disappears. Do not overmix once the flour goes in, or the crumb will turn tough. Scrape the bowl as needed.
5. Spoon the thick batter into the prepared pan and smooth the top, pressing it slightly higher around the center tube to encourage an even rise. Rap the pan firmly on the counter twice to release air pockets.
6. Bake for 50 to 60 minutes, until the top is golden, springs back when pressed, and a skewer inserted deep into the cake comes out clean. Tent loosely with foil after 40 minutes if it's browning too fast.
7. While the cake bakes, make the soak: gently warm 1/4 cup lemon juice with the remaining 1/2 cup granulated sugar until the sugar dissolves. When the cake comes out, let it cool in the pan 10 minutes, then poke the top all over with a skewer and brush on the warm syrup. Let it sit 10 more minutes, then invert onto a rack to cool completely.
8. Make the glaze: whisk the powdered sugar with 3 to 4 tablespoons fresh lemon juice until smooth and pourable but still thick enough to coat. Spoon it over the fully cooled cake, letting it run down the ridges. Let the glaze set 15 minutes before slicing.

</details>

<sub>📷 Jumping cheese, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-lemon-bundt-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Molasses-Cookies-2006March09.JPG/480px-Molasses-Cookies-2006March09.JPG" alt="Photo of Classic Molasses Spice Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Classic Molasses Spice Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-molasses-spice-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.28/serving** · 139 kcal · P2 · C20 · F6 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 24  

Soft, chewy old-fashioned molasses spice cookies with crackly sugar-rolled tops. Deep molasses flavor balanced by cinnamon, ginger, and cloves, with a tender center and slightly crisp edge. The hallmark is the granulated-sugar coating that crackles as the cookies spread and set.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking soda** — 2 tsp baking soda
- **Cinnamon** — 1 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Ground ginger** — 1 1/2 tsp ground ginger
- **Ground cloves** — 3/4 tsp ground cloves
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed dark brown sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Molasses** — 1/4 cup unsulphured (dark) molasses
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **White sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar, for rolling

**Steps**

1. Whisk together the flour, baking soda, cinnamon, ginger, cloves, and salt in a medium bowl; set aside.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter and brown sugar with an electric mixer on medium-high until light and fluffy, about 2 to 3 minutes. Add the egg, molasses, and vanilla and beat until fully combined, scraping down the bowl once.
3. Add the dry ingredients to the wet and mix on low just until no dry streaks remain. The dough will be soft and sticky. Cover and chill in the refrigerator for at least 1 hour (or up to 2 days) so it firms up and the flavors deepen.
4. Position racks in the upper and lower thirds of the oven and preheat to 350°F (175°C). Line two baking sheets with parchment paper. Put the granulated sugar for rolling in a small bowl.
5. Scoop the chilled dough into 1 1/2-tablespoon balls and roll each between your palms, then roll generously in the granulated sugar to coat. Place on the prepared sheets about 2 1/2 inches apart, 12 per sheet, to allow for spreading.
6. Bake for 10 to 12 minutes, rotating the sheets top-to-bottom and front-to-back halfway through, until the cookies have puffed, spread, and the tops are crackled but the centers still look slightly soft and underbaked.
7. Let the cookies cool on the baking sheet for 5 minutes to finish setting, then transfer to a wire rack. They firm up as they cool while staying chewy in the center.
8. Store in an airtight container at room temperature with a slice of bread to keep them soft; they keep well for up to 5 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Mark Fickett (Naib), CC BY-SA 2.5, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-molasses-spice-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a4/Morning_Glory_Muffin_%282015%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Morning Glory Muffins">

#### 🧁 [Classic Morning Glory Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-morning-glory-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.66/serving** · 313 kcal · P4 · C39 · F16 · ⏱ 50 min · 🍽 12  

The faithful Nantucket original from 1978: a moist, oil-based muffin packed with shredded carrot, grated apple, raisins, coconut, and walnuts, spiced with cinnamon. Hearty but tender, with a craggy domed top. The kind of bakery-case muffin that actually earns its name as a good-morning treat.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Baking soda** — 2 tsp baking soda
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Carrot** — 2 cups peeled, finely grated carrot (about 4 medium carrots)
- **Apple** — 1 large apple, peeled and grated (about 3/4 cup)
- **Raisins** — 1/2 cup raisins
- **Unsweetened shredded coconut** — 1/2 cup unsweetened shredded coconut
- **Walnuts** — 1/2 cup chopped walnuts
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs
- **Vegetable oil** — 3/4 cup vegetable oil
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350°F (175°C). Line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners or grease it well. Soak the raisins in hot water for 10 minutes while you prep, then drain — this keeps them plump instead of leathery.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, sugar, baking soda, cinnamon, and salt until evenly combined and no lumps of soda remain.
3. Add the grated carrot, grated apple, drained raisins, shredded coconut, and chopped walnuts to the dry mixture. Toss with your fingers or a fork until every piece is lightly coated in flour — this suspends them so they don't sink.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk the eggs, vegetable oil, and vanilla until smooth and slightly thickened.
5. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry mixture. Fold gently with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — the batter will be thick and chunky. Do not overmix, or the muffins will turn tough.
6. Divide the batter evenly among the 12 cups, mounding it high and full to the rim for tall, domed tops.
7. Bake on the center rack for 22 to 28 minutes, until the tops spring back when pressed and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean.
8. Cool in the pan for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack. They taste even better the next day once the flavors meld — store airtight at room temperature for up to 3 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Roozitaa, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-morning-glory-muffins/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Black_and_white_cookie_from_Trader_Joe%27s_01.jpg/480px-Black_and_white_cookie_from_Trader_Joe%27s_01.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic New York Black and White Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Classic New York Black and White Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-black-and-white-cookies/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.68/serving** · 246 kcal · P4 · C38 · F9 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 12  

The real deal: big, soft, cake-like vanilla cookies brightened with a whisper of lemon, each one iced half in glossy vanilla fondant and half in rich chocolate. Tender like a little cake, not crisp — exactly the way the corner bakery and deli counters in New York have made them for generations.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs
- **Buttermilk** — 1/2 cup buttermilk
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest
- **Powdered sugar** — 3 cups powdered sugar (for both icings)
- **Corn syrup** — 2 tbsp light corn syrup (for glossy fondant icing)
- **Cocoa powder** — 3 tbsp unsweetened cocoa powder (for the chocolate half)
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 3 tbsp semi-sweet chocolate chips, melted, for depth in the chocolate icing

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper. In a bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt; set aside.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter and granulated sugar with a mixer until pale and fluffy, about 2-3 minutes. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla and lemon zest, scraping the bowl as needed.
3. Add the flour mixture in three additions, alternating with the buttermilk, beginning and ending with flour. Mix just until a soft, thick batter forms — do not overmix. The batter should be scoopable, not pourable.
4. Scoop 1/4-cup mounds onto the sheets, spacing them at least 3 inches apart (they spread). Smooth the tops slightly with the back of a spoon. Bake 14-17 minutes, until the tops spring back and the bottom edges are just barely golden — they should stay pale on top. Cool completely on a rack. Ice them flat-side up; the flat bottom is the surface you decorate.
5. Make the vanilla icing: whisk 1 1/2 cups powdered sugar with 1 tbsp corn syrup, 1/4 tsp of the vanilla (taken from your bottle), and 2-3 tsp hot water until thick but spreadable and glossy. It should fall in a slow ribbon. Add water a few drops at a time if too stiff.
6. Working one cookie at a time, spread the vanilla icing over exactly half of each cookie's flat side, using a small offset spatula and keeping a clean straight line down the center. Let set while you make the chocolate side.
7. Make the chocolate icing: into the remaining 1 1/2 cups powdered sugar, whisk the cocoa powder, the remaining 1 tbsp corn syrup, the melted chocolate chips, and 3-4 tsp hot water until smooth and glossy. Spread over the bare half of each cookie, meeting the vanilla line cleanly.
8. Let the cookies sit at room temperature until the icing sets firm to the touch, about 1 hour. Store in a single layer; they're best the day they're made but keep, covered, for up to 2 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Mattsjc, CC BY 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-black-and-white-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Gingerbread_Bundt_Cake.jpg/480px-Gingerbread_Bundt_Cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Old-Fashioned Gingerbread Cake">

#### 🍰 [Classic Old-Fashioned Gingerbread Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-gingerbread-cake/)
American · snack · easy  
**$1.08/serving** · 347 kcal · P5 · C45 · F16 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 9  

A deeply spiced, molasses-rich gingerbread cake with a tender, moist crumb and a dark, glossy top. Boiling water bloomed into the batter gives it that signature soft, almost pudding-like interior, while a generous hit of ground ginger, cinnamon, and cloves makes every bite warm and aromatic. This is the real, single-layer snacking cake your grandmother made — humble, honest, and irresistible with a dusting of powdered sugar or a spoonful of softly whipped cream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Molasses** — 1 cup unsulphured (dark) molasses
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed dark brown sugar
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, melted
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Water** — 1 cup boiling water
- **Baking soda** — 1 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Baking powder** — 3/4 tsp baking powder
- **Ground ginger** — 2 tsp ground ginger
- **Cinnamon** — 1 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Ground cloves** — 1/2 tsp ground cloves
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp ground nutmeg
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Butter an 8x8-inch (or 9x9-inch) square baking pan and line it with parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides for easy lift-out.
2. In a medium bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, baking powder, ground ginger, cinnamon, cloves, nutmeg, and salt until evenly combined. Set aside.
3. In a large bowl, whisk the melted butter and dark brown sugar until smooth, then whisk in the molasses and vanilla. Add the eggs one at a time, whisking well after each so the batter is glossy and emulsified.
4. Add the dry ingredients to the wet and whisk just until no dry streaks remain — do not overmix. The batter will be thick.
5. Carefully pour in the 1 cup boiling water and whisk until the batter is smooth and pourable. It will look thin; that is correct and is what gives the cake its moist, tender crumb.
6. Pour the batter into the prepared pan and smooth the top. Bake for 40 to 45 minutes, until the top springs back when lightly pressed and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out with a few moist crumbs (no wet batter).
7. Set the pan on a wire rack and cool for at least 20 minutes. Run a knife around the edges, then lift the cake out using the parchment overhang.
8. Serve warm or at room temperature, dusted generously with powdered sugar or topped with a dollop of lightly sweetened whipped cream. The flavor deepens overnight, so it is even better the next day.

</details>

<sub>📷 HarshLight, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-gingerbread-cake/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Pecan_squares.jpg/480px-Pecan_squares.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Pecan Pie Bars">

#### 🥧 [Classic Pecan Pie Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pecan-pie-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.98/serving** · 148 kcal · P2 · C17 · F8 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 24  

All the deep, buttery, caramel-and-toasted-pecan soul of a Thanksgiving pecan pie, baked as portable bars. A crisp shortbread crust holds a gooey corn-syrup-and-brown-sugar custard packed with pecans. Far easier than rolling pie dough, and they slice into clean squares that travel beautifully.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour (for the shortbread crust)
- **Brown sugar** — 1/4 cup packed light brown sugar (for the crust)
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt (for the crust)
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1.5 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed (for the crust)
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs (for the filling)
- **Corn syrup** — 1 cup light corn syrup (for the filling)
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed light brown sugar (for the filling)
- **Butter** — 1/4 cup unsalted butter, melted (for the filling)
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp pure vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt (for the filling)
- **Pecans** — 2.5 cups (about 12 oz) pecan halves, roughly chopped

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Line a 9x13-inch baking pan with parchment, leaving an overhang on the long sides so you can lift the bars out later. Lightly butter the parchment.
2. Make the crust: In a bowl, whisk together the 2 cups flour, 1/4 cup brown sugar, and 1/2 tsp salt. Cut in the cold cubed butter with a pastry cutter or your fingertips until the mixture looks like coarse, sandy crumbs that just hold together when pressed.
3. Tip the crumbs into the lined pan and press firmly into an even layer across the bottom, packing it tight (a flat-bottomed measuring cup helps). Bake for 18 to 20 minutes, until the edges are pale golden and the surface is set. Leave the oven on.
4. While the crust bakes, make the filling: In a large bowl whisk the eggs until smooth, then whisk in the corn syrup, 3/4 cup brown sugar, melted butter, vanilla, and 1/2 tsp salt until fully combined and no lumps of sugar remain. Stir in the chopped pecans.
5. Pour the pecan filling evenly over the hot, par-baked crust, spreading the nuts so they're distributed corner to corner.
6. Return to the oven and bake 25 to 30 minutes, until the filling is set around the edges and the center jiggles only slightly when you nudge the pan. Don't overbake or the filling turns hard rather than chewy.
7. Cool the pan completely on a wire rack, at least 2 hours, until fully set. For the cleanest slices, chill in the fridge for an hour before cutting.
8. Lift the slab out using the parchment overhang, transfer to a cutting board, and cut into 24 bars with a sharp knife, wiping the blade between cuts. Store airtight at room temperature up to 4 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 jenny, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pecan-pie-bars/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Popovers.jpg/480px-Popovers.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Popovers">

#### 🥯 [Classic Popovers](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-popovers/)
American · breakfast · medium  
**$0.29/serving** · 158 kcal · P6 · C18 · F7 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 6  

Tall, dramatically puffed popovers with crisp, deeply browned shells and a custardy, hollow interior. A simple batter of flour, milk, eggs, and salt transforms in a hot oven through steam alone — no leavening, no fuss. Serve them straight from the pan with butter and jam, or alongside a roast.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup all-purpose flour
- **Milk** — 1 cup whole milk, lukewarm
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp unsalted butter, melted (plus extra for greasing)
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt

**Steps**

1. Bring everything to room temperature first. Cold milk and eggs are the number one cause of squat popovers — let the milk sit out 30 minutes, or warm it gently until just lukewarm to the touch.
2. Place a 6-cup popover pan (or a deep muffin tin) on the middle rack and preheat the oven to 450 degrees F. Heating the pan empty is essential for the dramatic rise.
3. In a bowl, whisk the eggs until frothy, then whisk in the lukewarm milk and melted butter. Add the flour and salt and whisk just until smooth — a few small lumps are fine. Do not overbeat; you want the batter thin, like heavy cream. For best lift, let it rest 15 to 30 minutes.
4. Carefully pull out the hot pan and quickly brush each cup generously with melted butter, coating the bottom and all the way up the sides.
5. Fill each cup about two-thirds full with batter (roughly 1/3 cup each). Work fast so the pan stays hot, then return it to the oven immediately.
6. Bake at 450 degrees F for 20 minutes without opening the door — this sets the initial explosive rise.
7. Reduce the heat to 350 degrees F and bake 15 to 18 minutes more, until the popovers are tall, deep golden brown, and rigid to the touch. Underbaking leaves them soft and they will collapse.
8. Remove from the oven and, if you like them extra crisp, pierce the side of each with a paring knife to let steam escape. Turn out of the pan and serve immediately, while puffed and hot, with butter and jam.

</details>

<sub>📷 Jeremy Noble, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-popovers/)</sub>
