<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/d/d3/S%27mores_bars%2C_The_Sweet_Escape_Patisserie%2C_Toronto_%284050433758%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic S'mores Bars">

#### 🍫 [Classic S'mores Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-smores-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.62/serving** · 211 kcal · P2 · C25 · F11 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 16  

Everything you love about a campfire s'more in bar form: a buttery graham cracker cookie base, a thick layer of milk chocolate, and gooey toasted marshmallow on top — all baked in one pan, no fire required. The dough has real graham crumbs folded right in, so every bite tastes like the genuine thing.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup all-purpose flour
- **Graham crackers** — 1 1/4 cups graham cracker crumbs (about 9 full sheets, finely crushed)
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Dark chocolate** — 7 oz milk chocolate (about two standard bars), broken into pieces
- **Marshmallows (mini or fluff)** — 1 1/2 cups marshmallow fluff or mini marshmallows

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C). Line an 8x8-inch baking pan with parchment paper, leaving an overhang on two sides so you can lift the bars out later. Lightly grease the parchment.
2. Make the graham dough: In a large bowl, cream the softened butter, brown sugar, and granulated sugar together until light and fluffy, about 2 minutes. Beat in the egg and vanilla until smooth.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, graham cracker crumbs, baking powder, and salt. Add the dry ingredients to the wet and mix just until a soft, slightly sticky dough forms — do not overmix.
4. Divide the dough in half. Press one half evenly into the bottom of the prepared pan with lightly floured fingers, building a flat, compact base.
5. Lay the milk chocolate pieces in a single even layer over the dough base, covering it edge to edge so you get melty chocolate in every bite. Spread or dollop the marshmallow fluff over the chocolate (or scatter the mini marshmallows evenly).
6. Flatten the remaining dough into rough patches between your palms and lay them over the marshmallow layer, pressing gently to connect them into a mostly-covering top crust. It's fine if a little marshmallow peeks through.
7. Bake for 30 to 35 minutes, until the top is golden brown and set. If you want a deeper toasted-marshmallow look on any exposed bits, switch to the broiler for the final 1 to 2 minutes and watch closely so it doesn't burn.
8. Cool completely in the pan on a rack, at least 1 hour, so the chocolate and marshmallow firm up. Lift out using the parchment overhang and cut into 16 squares with a sharp knife wiped clean between cuts.

</details>

<sub>📷 Rachel Kramer Bussel, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-smores-bars/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Sour_cream_coffee_cake.jpg/480px-Sour_cream_coffee_cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Sour Cream Coffee Cake">

#### 🍰 [Classic Sour Cream Coffee Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sour-cream-coffee-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.97/serving** · 497 kcal · P6 · C65 · F24 · ⏱ 85 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, buttery bundt-style coffee cake built on a generous amount of full-fat sour cream, which keeps the crumb impossibly moist and gives it a faint tang. A cinnamon-brown-sugar-walnut streusel is layered through the middle and over the top. This is the real deal — the kind of cake that gets better on day two and was made to be served with a pot of coffee, not in it.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour (for the cake batter)
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar (for the batter)
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Sour cream** — 1 1/2 cups full-fat sour cream, room temperature
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed light brown sugar (for the streusel)
- **Cinnamon** — 4 tsp ground cinnamon (for the streusel)
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup walnuts, finely chopped (for the streusel)
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup powdered sugar (for the optional glaze) _(optional)_
- **Milk** — about 2 tbsp milk, to thin the glaze _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Generously butter and flour a 10-cup (10-inch) bundt or tube pan, getting into every crevice so the cake releases cleanly.
2. Make the streusel: in a small bowl, stir together the brown sugar, cinnamon, and finely chopped walnuts until evenly combined. Set aside.
3. Whisk together the flour, baking powder, baking soda, and salt in a medium bowl. In a separate large bowl, beat the softened butter with the granulated sugar on medium-high speed for 3-4 minutes until pale and fluffy.
4. Add the eggs one at a time, beating well after each, then beat in the vanilla. Scrape down the bowl. The mixture should look light and creamy.
5. With the mixer on low, add the flour mixture in three additions, alternating with the sour cream in two additions, beginning and ending with the flour. Mix just until no streaks of flour remain — do not overmix, or the cake will turn tough.
6. Spoon about half the batter into the prepared pan and smooth it level. Sprinkle two-thirds of the streusel evenly over the batter. Top with the remaining batter, spread gently to cover, then scatter the last of the streusel over the top.
7. Bake for 50-60 minutes, until the cake is golden, springs back when lightly pressed, and a skewer inserted into the center comes out clean (a few moist crumbs are fine). If the top browns too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last 15 minutes.
8. Cool in the pan on a wire rack for 20 minutes, then invert onto the rack to finish cooling. If glazing, whisk the powdered sugar with just enough milk to make a pourable glaze and drizzle over the warm cake. Serve slightly warm or at room temperature.

</details>

<sub>📷 Kimberly Vardeman, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sour-cream-coffee-cake/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Whoopie_Pies_%284424256268%29.jpg/480px-Whoopie_Pies_%284424256268%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Whoopie Pies">

#### 🍫 [Classic Whoopie Pies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-whoopie-pies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.73/serving** · 293 kcal · P3 · C35 · F16 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 12  

Two tender, cake-like chocolate cookies sandwiching a fluffy vanilla buttercream-marshmallow filling. This is the genuine New England / Pennsylvania Dutch classic: buttermilk and cocoa give the cakes their soft crumb and deep color, while the filling stays light thanks to whipped butter and a touch of marshmallow-style fluff built from powdered sugar.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Cocoa powder** — 1/2 cup unsweetened cocoa powder
- **Baking soda** — 1 1/4 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Buttermilk** — 1 cup buttermilk
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened (for filling)
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 cups powdered sugar
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract (for filling)
- **Heavy cream** — 2 tbsp heavy cream
- **Salt** — pinch of salt (for filling)

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 375 degrees F and line two baking sheets with parchment paper. In a medium bowl, whisk together the flour, cocoa powder, baking soda, and salt until evenly combined and no streaks of cocoa remain.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter and brown sugar with an electric mixer on medium-high until light and fluffy, about 3 minutes. Beat in the egg and 1 tsp vanilla until smooth, scraping down the bowl.
3. On low speed, add the flour mixture in three additions alternating with the buttermilk, beginning and ending with the flour. Mix just until the batter is smooth and thick; do not overmix.
4. Drop rounded tablespoons of batter onto the prepared sheets, spacing them about 2 inches apart to allow for spreading. Aim for even, mounded portions so the halves match up later.
5. Bake for 10 to 12 minutes, until the tops spring back when lightly pressed and a toothpick comes out clean. Let the cakes cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely before filling.
6. Make the filling: beat the softened butter until creamy, then add the powdered sugar in batches with the 2 tsp vanilla, heavy cream, and pinch of salt. Whip on high for 2 to 3 minutes until light, fluffy, and spreadable.
7. Pair up the cooled cakes by size. Spread or pipe a generous mound of filling onto the flat side of one cake, then gently press a second cake on top so the filling reaches the edges.
8. Serve right away, or chill 20 minutes to set the filling. Store in an airtight container, separated by parchment, for up to 3 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Clever Cupcakes from Montreal, Canada, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-whoopie-pies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Coconut_Cream_Pie_%28Golden_Nugget%29_%2879939882%29.jpg/480px-Coconut_Cream_Pie_%28Golden_Nugget%29_%2879939882%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Coconut Cream Pie">

#### 🥥 [Coconut Cream Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-coconut-cream-pie/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.49/serving** · 379 kcal · P6 · C34 · F24 · ⏱ 330 min · 🍽 8  

A from-scratch coconut cream pie: a buttery blind-baked crust filled with silky coconut pastry cream cooked from egg yolks, coconut milk, and toasted coconut, crowned with lightly sweetened whipped cream and a shower of toasted coconut flakes.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour for the crust (about 1.25 cups)
- **Butter** — cold cubed butter for crust + softened for filling (about 9 tbsp total)
- **Sugar** — granulated sugar for crust + filling (about 2/3 cup)
- **Salt** — pinch for crust and filling
- **Coconut milk** — full-fat canned coconut milk
- **Whole milk** — about 1.5 cups
- **Eggs** — yolks only for the pastry cream
- **Cornstarch** — about 1/4 cup to thicken the custard
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Unsweetened shredded coconut** — toasted, for filling + garnish (about 1.25 cups)
- **Heavy cream** — for the whipped topping (about 1.25 cups)
- **Powdered sugar** — to sweeten the whipped cream (about 3 tbsp)

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: pulse 1.25 cups flour, 1 tsp sugar, and a pinch of salt, then cut in 6 tbsp cold cubed butter until pea-sized. Add 3-4 tbsp ice water and bring together into a disk; chill 30 minutes, then roll out and fit into a 9-inch pie plate, crimping the edge.
2. Blind-bake the crust: line with parchment and pie weights (or dried beans), bake at 375F (190C) for 18 minutes, remove the weights, then bake 10-12 minutes more until golden. Cool completely.
3. Toast the coconut: spread the shredded coconut on a dry sheet pan and bake at 325F (160C), stirring every 2-3 minutes, until evenly golden, about 8 minutes. Reserve about 1/3 for garnish.
4. Heat the dairy: in a saucepan, warm the coconut milk and whole milk with half the remaining sugar until steaming but not boiling.
5. Make the custard base: whisk the 4 egg yolks with the cornstarch and remaining sugar until smooth, then slowly stream in the hot milk while whisking constantly to temper the eggs.
6. Cook the pastry cream: pour everything back into the saucepan over medium heat and whisk nonstop until it thickens and bubbles, 2-3 minutes; cook 1 minute more to remove the starchy taste.
7. Finish the filling: off heat, whisk in 3 tbsp butter, the vanilla, a pinch of salt, and the toasted coconut. Pour into the cooled crust, press plastic wrap onto the surface, and chill at least 4 hours until set.
8. Whip the cream: beat the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar to soft-medium peaks. Spread or pipe over the chilled filling and shower with the reserved toasted coconut just before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Kim Scarborough from Chicago, IL — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-coconut-cream-pie/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Coconut_Macaroons_%285940136052%29.jpg/480px-Coconut_Macaroons_%285940136052%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Coconut Macaroons">

#### 🥥 [Coconut Macaroons](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-coconut-macaroons/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.67/serving** · 215 kcal · P3 · C22 · F14 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 12  

Chewy, golden-edged coconut macaroons bound with sweetened condensed milk and lifted with whipped egg whites for a tender crumb. Crisp on the outside, moist and dense inside, with an optional dark-chocolate dip. A classic, foolproof bake that needs no mixer.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Unsweetened shredded coconut** — about 14 oz / 5 cups shredded coconut
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — scant 1 can (about 10-11 oz) as the binder
- **Liquid egg whites** — 2 large egg whites, whipped to soft peaks
- **Vanilla extract** — 1.5 tsp vanilla extract
- **Almond extract** — 1/4 tsp, deepens the flavor _(optional)_
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — about 2/3 cup, melted for dipping _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 325F (160C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper.
2. In a large bowl, stir together the shredded coconut, sweetened condensed milk, vanilla, almond extract, and salt until every strand of coconut is coated and the mixture is sticky and thick.
3. In a separate clean, grease-free bowl, whisk the egg whites until they hold soft, glossy peaks. This lightens the dense coconut and gives the macaroons a tender interior.
4. Gently fold the whipped whites into the coconut mixture in two additions, keeping as much air as possible while still combining evenly.
5. Using a tablespoon or small cookie scoop, mound packed 1.5-inch portions onto the parchment about 1 inch apart, gently shaping each into a peaked pyramid with damp fingers.
6. Bake 18-22 minutes, until the peaks and edges are deep golden brown while the centers stay pale; rotate the pans halfway through for even color.
7. Let the macaroons cool on the sheets for 10 minutes to set, then transfer to a rack to cool completely.
8. If dipping, melt the chocolate gently, dip the bottom of each cooled macaroon and set on parchment; chill 10 minutes until the chocolate firms.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: NGI197 — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-coconut-macaroons/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Streusel_cake.jpg/480px-Streusel_cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Coffee Cake Crumb Bars">

#### 🍰 [Coffee Cake Crumb Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-coffee-cake-crumb-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.52/serving** · 327 kcal · P3 · C43 · F16 · ⏱ 115 min · 🍽 16  

Classic New York-style crumb cake in shareable bar form: a tender vanilla butter-cake base under a thick, craggy cinnamon brown-sugar crumb topping, dusted with powdered sugar. The crumb is the star — made the real way, by stirring dry ingredients into melted butter so it clumps into big, snackable boulders.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour (for the cake base)
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar (for the cake base)
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/4 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened, for the base
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Sour cream** — 1/2 cup sour cream
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 1/2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, melted, for the crumb topping
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed light brown sugar (for the crumb)
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup granulated sugar (for the crumb)
- **Cinnamon** — 2 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon (for the crumb)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt (for the crumb)
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour (for the crumb topping)
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Line an 8x8-inch baking pan with parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides, and lightly butter the parchment. Make the crumb topping first so it can firm up while you mix the base: in a medium bowl, whisk together the melted butter, brown sugar, 1/4 cup granulated sugar, cinnamon, and 1/4 tsp salt until smooth. Stir in the 2 cups of flour with a fork or spatula until a thick, uniform dough forms. Set aside at room temperature.
2. For the cake base, whisk together the 1 1/4 cups flour, baking powder, baking soda, and 1/2 tsp salt in a small bowl. In a larger bowl, beat the softened butter with the 3/4 cup granulated sugar until pale and fluffy, about 2-3 minutes. Beat in the egg until fully combined, then mix in the sour cream and vanilla. The batter may look slightly curdled — that's fine.
3. Add the dry ingredients to the wet and fold just until no streaks of flour remain; do not overmix. The base batter will be thick, almost like a soft cookie dough. Scrape it into the prepared pan and smooth into an even layer with an offset spatula or the back of a spoon.
4. Take the crumb dough and pinch off pieces, squeezing them in your hand and then breaking them apart over the batter to create crumbs of varying sizes — from pea-sized to large 1-inch boulders. Cover the entire surface; pile it on generously and don't press it down. The thick crumb layer is what makes these coffee cake bars.
5. Bake on the center rack for 38-45 minutes, until the crumb is set and lightly golden and a toothpick inserted into the cake layer (angle it past the crumb) comes out clean or with a few moist crumbs. If the top is browning too fast past 30 minutes, tent loosely with foil.
6. Let the bars cool completely in the pan on a wire rack, at least 1 hour. The base needs to fully set before cutting or the bars will be gummy. For the cleanest crumb topping, don't rush this.
7. Using the parchment overhang, lift the slab out of the pan onto a cutting board. Dust generously with the powdered sugar through a fine sieve, then cut into 16 squares with a sharp knife, wiping the blade between cuts. Store in an airtight container at room temperature for up to 4 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-coffee-cake-crumb-bars/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Cornbread_muffins_at_Elizabeth%27s%2C_New_Orleans.jpg/480px-Cornbread_muffins_at_Elizabeth%27s%2C_New_Orleans.jpg" alt="Photo of Cornbread Muffins">

#### 🌽 [Cornbread Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cornbread-muffins/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.26/serving** · 124 kcal · P2 · C14 · F6 · ⏱ 35 min · 🍽 12  

Golden, tender cornbread muffins with crisp tops and a moist, buttery crumb. Real buttermilk and a high ratio of stone-ground cornmeal give them genuine corn flavor and a light, slightly crumbly texture — just barely sweet, the way a great Southern-leaning muffin should be.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Cornmeal (polenta)** — stone-ground yellow cornmeal, ~1.5 cups
- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, ~1 cup
- **Sugar** — ~3 tbsp; bump to 1/4 cup for sweeter muffins
- **Baking powder** — 1 tbsp
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp, reacts with buttermilk
- **Salt** — 1 tsp
- **Buttermilk** — ~1.25 cups
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp melted, plus extra for the tin
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp, for serving _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 400F (200C) and grease a 12-cup muffin tin generously with butter, or line with paper liners.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the cornmeal, flour, sugar, baking powder, baking soda, and salt until evenly combined.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk the eggs, then whisk in the buttermilk until smooth. Whisk in the melted (but not hot) butter.
4. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry and fold gently with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — the batter should be thick and a little lumpy. Do not overmix, or the muffins turn tough.
5. Let the batter rest 5 minutes so the cornmeal hydrates and the crumb stays tender.
6. Divide the batter among the 12 cups, filling each about three-quarters full.
7. Bake 16 to 19 minutes, until the tops are golden and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean.
8. Cool in the tin 5 minutes, then turn out onto a rack. Serve warm with butter and a drizzle of honey.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Kent Wang — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cornbread-muffins/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/White_chocolate_and_cranberry_cookies_%288212918192%29.jpg/480px-White_chocolate_and_cranberry_cookies_%288212918192%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Cranberry Orange Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Cranberry Orange Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cranberry-orange-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.28/serving** · 145 kcal · P2 · C21 · F6 · ⏱ 55 min · 🍽 24  

Soft, cakey drop cookies studded with tart dried cranberries and brightened with fresh orange zest, finished with a thin orange glaze. The classic holiday-table version a confident home baker makes from scratch — tender crumb, real citrus aroma, and a tangy-sweet drizzle.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Brown sugar** — 1/4 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Orange** — 1 orange — zest in the dough, 2-3 tbsp juice for the glaze
- **Orange juice** — 3 tbsp fresh orange juice (for dough)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp orange zest (use lemon-zest id), plus more for glaze
- **Dried cranberries** — 1 1/4 cups dried cranberries, roughly chopped
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup powdered sugar (for glaze)

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 375°F (190°C). Line two baking sheets with parchment paper. Roughly chop the dried cranberries so they distribute evenly, and zest the orange before juicing it.
2. In a medium bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, baking soda, and salt. Set aside.
3. In a large bowl, cream the softened butter, granulated sugar, and brown sugar with a mixer on medium-high until pale and fluffy, about 3 minutes. Beat in the egg, then the vanilla, orange zest, and 3 tablespoons fresh orange juice until smooth — the mixture may look slightly curdled, which is fine.
4. Add the dry ingredients in two additions, mixing on low just until no dry streaks remain. Fold in the chopped cranberries by hand. Do not overmix — the dough should be soft and thick.
5. Scoop rounded tablespoons of dough (about 1.5 tablespoons each) and space them 2 inches apart on the prepared sheets. Gently round the tops with damp fingers.
6. Bake one sheet at a time for 11 to 13 minutes, until the edges are set and just barely golden but the centers still look soft. Let the cookies rest on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely.
7. Make the glaze: whisk the powdered sugar with 2 to 3 tablespoons of the reserved fresh orange juice (and a pinch of zest) until smooth and pourable but not runny. Add juice a few drops at a time to reach a thick ribbon consistency.
8. Once the cookies are fully cool, drizzle the glaze over the tops with a spoon or pipe it in thin lines. Let the glaze set for about 20 minutes before stacking or storing in an airtight container.

</details>

<sub>📷 Sodanie Chea, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cranberry-orange-cookies/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

---

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Vegan_Orange_Cranberry_Muffins_%285385511880%29.jpg/480px-Vegan_Orange_Cranberry_Muffins_%285385511880%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Cranberry Orange Muffins">

#### 🧁 [Cranberry Orange Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cranberry-orange-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.78/serving** · 264 kcal · P4 · C43 · F8 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 12  

Bakery-style muffins with a tender, buttery crumb, bright orange zest, and pops of tart cranberry — finished with a high-domed crackly top from a hot-oven start. Perfect with coffee.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, about 2.5 cups
- **Sugar** — about 1 cup, plus a little for sprinkling tops
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp
- **Baking soda** — 1/4 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick), melted and cooled
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Buttermilk** — 3/4 cup
- **Orange** — zest of both; 1/3 cup juice from them
- **Vanilla extract** — 1.5 tsp
- **Frozen berries** — fresh or frozen cranberries, 2 cups, halved if large; keep frozen until folding

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 425F and line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners (or grease well). Starting hot gives the muffins a tall, domed top.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, sugar, baking powder, baking soda, and salt. Rub the orange zest into the sugar with your fingertips to release its oils.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk the eggs, then add the melted-and-cooled butter, buttermilk, 1/3 cup fresh orange juice, and vanilla until smooth.
4. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry and fold with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — the batter should be thick and a little lumpy. Do not overmix or the muffins turn tough.
5. Toss the cranberries with a spoonful of the flour (so they don't sink), then gently fold them in. Keep them whole or roughly halved for bursts of tartness.
6. Divide the batter evenly among the cups, filling each nearly to the top for big domes. Sprinkle the tops with a little extra sugar for crunch.
7. Bake at 425F for 5 minutes, then — without opening the door — lower the oven to 375F and bake another 14 to 17 minutes, until golden and a toothpick comes out clean.
8. Cool in the pan 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. Serve warm or at room temperature.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Veganbaking.net from USA — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cranberry-orange-muffins/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f9/Devil%27s_Food_Cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Devil's Food Cake">

#### 🍫 [Devil's Food Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-devils-food-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.07/serving** · 454 kcal · P5 · C62 · F22 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 12  

A deeply dark, ultra-moist chocolate layer cake with a tender, almost fudgy crumb. The cocoa is bloomed in hot coffee to amplify the chocolate, buttermilk and baking soda give it the classic tang and reddish-mahogany color, and it's finished with a glossy chocolate buttercream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, ~2 cups
- **Dutch process cocoa** — Dutch-process cocoa, ~3/4 cup, bloomed in hot coffee
- **Sugar** — granulated sugar, ~2 cups
- **Brown sugar** — light brown sugar, ~1/2 cup, for moisture and depth
- **Baking soda** — ~2 tsp; reacts with cocoa and buttermilk
- **Baking powder** — ~1 tsp
- **Salt** — ~1.25 tsp
- **Eggs** — room temperature
- **Buttermilk** — ~1 cup, room temperature
- **Vegetable oil** — ~1/2 cup; keeps the crumb moist
- **Brewed coffee** — ~1 cup hot brewed coffee, poured over cocoa to bloom
- **Vanilla extract** — ~2 tsp
- **Butter** — unsalted, softened, for the buttercream (~1.5 sticks)
- **Powdered sugar** — ~3 cups, for buttercream
- **Cocoa powder** — ~2/3 cup unsweetened cocoa for buttercream
- **Heavy cream** — ~1/4 cup, to loosen buttercream
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — melted into buttercream for extra gloss and depth _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350F (175C). Grease two 9-inch round cake pans, line the bottoms with parchment, and grease again. In a bowl or large measuring cup, whisk the Dutch cocoa with the hot brewed coffee until smooth, then set aside to cool slightly; this blooms the cocoa for deeper chocolate flavor.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, granulated sugar, brown sugar, baking soda, baking powder, and salt, breaking up any brown sugar lumps.
3. In another bowl, whisk the eggs, buttermilk, oil, and vanilla until smooth, then whisk in the cooled cocoa-coffee mixture.
4. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry and whisk just until no streaks remain; the batter will be thin and pourable, which is correct. Do not overmix.
5. Divide the batter evenly between the pans and bake 28-34 minutes, until a toothpick inserted in the center comes out with a few moist crumbs and the tops spring back when lightly pressed.
6. Cool the cakes in the pans 10 minutes, then turn out onto a rack, peel off the parchment, and cool completely before frosting.
7. Make the buttercream: beat the softened butter until pale and fluffy, then beat in the sifted powdered sugar and cocoa in batches. Add the optional melted, cooled chocolate chips, then the heavy cream and a pinch of salt, and beat until light and spreadable.
8. Level the cake tops if domed. Place one layer on a plate, spread with frosting, top with the second layer, then frost the top and sides. Let set 15 minutes before slicing.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Lili — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-devils-food-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Reverse_Chocolate_Chunk_Cookie_-_GAIL%27s_2025-07-14.jpg/480px-Reverse_Chocolate_Chunk_Cookie_-_GAIL%27s_2025-07-14.jpg" alt="Photo of Double Chocolate Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Double Chocolate Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-double-chocolate-cookies/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.78/serving** · 141 kcal · P2 · C21 · F6 · ⏱ 55 min · 🍽 18  

Rich, fudgy double chocolate cookies built on melted dark chocolate and Dutch-process cocoa, then loaded with pools of semi-sweet chips. Crackly tops, soft brownie-like centers, and deep chocolate flavor in every bite.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Dark chocolate** — 6 oz dark chocolate (60-70%), chopped
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Dutch process cocoa** — 6 tbsp Dutch-process cocoa powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp fine salt
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 3/4 cup (about 4.5 oz) semi-sweet chocolate chips

**Steps**

1. Set up a double boiler: combine the chopped dark chocolate and butter in a heatproof bowl set over a pan of barely simmering water. Stir until fully melted and glossy, then remove from heat and let cool for 10 minutes until just warm to the touch.
2. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, Dutch-process cocoa, baking soda, and salt. Set aside.
3. In a large bowl, beat the eggs, granulated sugar, and brown sugar with a hand or stand mixer on high for 3 to 4 minutes, until the mixture is pale, thick, and ribbons off the whisk. This step builds the signature crackly top.
4. Reduce speed to low and blend in the cooled melted chocolate mixture and the vanilla until smooth and uniform.
5. Add the dry ingredients and fold by hand with a spatula just until no flour streaks remain. Fold in the semi-sweet chocolate chips. The batter will be thick and fudgy; chill it for 20 to 30 minutes so it firms up enough to scoop.
6. Meanwhile, preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper.
7. Scoop heaping tablespoons of dough (about 1.5 tbsp each), spacing them 2 inches apart, 9 per sheet. Bake one sheet at a time for 10 to 12 minutes, until the tops are crackled and the edges are set but the centers still look slightly soft.
8. Cool on the baking sheet for 5 minutes to finish setting, then transfer to a wire rack. For extra-glossy tops, press a few reserved chips onto each cookie right after baking.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-double-chocolate-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/cb/French_silk_pie_%286610443047%29.jpg" alt="Photo of French Silk Pie">

#### 🥧 [French Silk Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-french-silk-pie/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.10/serving** · 469 kcal · P4 · C39 · F33 · ⏱ 360 min · 🍽 10  

The classic American chocolate silk pie: a flaky blind-baked all-butter crust filled with an impossibly smooth, airy dark-chocolate mousse, then crowned with billows of lightly sweetened whipped cream and chocolate shavings. The silk comes from creaming butter with sugar, then whipping in cooled melted chocolate and eggs that are gently cooked over a water bath until thick — no raw eggs, no gelatin, no shortcuts.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour, for the crust
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) cold unsalted butter, cubed, for the crust
- **Sugar** — 1 tsp granulated sugar, for the crust
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt, for the crust
- **Water** — 3 tbsp ice water, for the crust
- **Dark chocolate** — 4 oz bittersweet chocolate (60-70%), chopped, for the filling
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened, for the filling
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar, for the filling
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp pure vanilla extract
- **Heavy cream** — 1 cup cold heavy cream, for the topping
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for the whipped cream

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: Whisk the flour, 1 tsp sugar, and salt. Cut in the cold cubed butter with your fingers or a pastry cutter until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs with some pea-sized bits. Drizzle in the ice water a tablespoon at a time, tossing, until the dough just holds together when squeezed. Flatten into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour.
2. Roll and blind-bake: Heat the oven to 375F (190C). Roll the dough on a floured surface to a 12-inch round and fit it into a 9-inch pie plate, crimping the edge. Prick the base all over with a fork, line with parchment, and fill with pie weights or dried beans. Bake 18 minutes, remove the weights and parchment, then bake 10-12 minutes more until the crust is golden and fully dry. Cool completely.
3. Melt the chocolate: Set the chopped chocolate in a heatproof bowl over barely simmering water (or microwave in short bursts), stirring until smooth. Set aside to cool to just warm — it must not be hot when it meets the eggs or it will scramble them.
4. Cream the base: In a large bowl, beat the softened 1 cup butter with the 1 cup granulated sugar on high until very pale and fluffy, 3-4 minutes. Beat in the cooled melted chocolate and the vanilla until uniform.
5. Cook the eggs gently (this is the silk): Add the eggs one at a time, but rather than leaving them raw, transfer the mixture to a bowl over the simmering water and whisk constantly, scraping the sides, until it thickens slightly and reaches 160F (71C), about 4-5 minutes. This cooks the eggs safely while keeping the texture silky. Remove from heat.
6. Whip to silk: Return the warm mixture to the mixer (or use a hand mixer) and beat on medium-high for a full 5 minutes, until the filling is thick, glossy, lightened in color, and holds soft ribbons. Don't rush this — the long whip is what makes it airy.
7. Fill and chill: Scrape the filling into the cooled crust and smooth the top. Refrigerate until completely set, at least 4 hours or overnight.
8. Top and serve: Whip the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar to soft-to-medium peaks. Pile or pipe it over the chilled pie, finish with chocolate shavings if you like, and serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Corey Taratuta, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-french-silk-pie/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a4/Cake-for-30%2C000-Wikipedia-edits.jpg" alt="Photo of Funfetti Birthday Cake">

#### 🎂 [Funfetti Birthday Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-funfetti-birthday-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.99/serving** · 425 kcal · P6 · C52 · F22 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, buttery vanilla layer cake studded with rainbow sprinkles and finished with a fluffy cream cheese-vanilla buttercream. The classic celebration cake — soft crumb, double-vanilla flavor, and a hit of almond extract for that nostalgic "birthday cake" taste.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour (cake flour is even better if you have it)
- **Sugar** — 1 3/4 cups granulated sugar for the cake
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) softened unsalted butter for the cake
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/4 cup oil keeps the crumb moist alongside the butter
- **Eggs** — 3 whole eggs + 1 extra white, room temperature
- **Milk** — 1 cup whole milk or buttermilk, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tbsp vanilla extract total across batter and frosting
- **Almond extract** — 1/4 tsp almond extract for that signature birthday-cake note
- **Baking powder** — 2 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp fine salt
- **Chocolate chips** — 1/2 cup rainbow jimmies/sprinkles for the batter — use real long sprinkles, not nonpareils, so they don't bleed
- **Powdered sugar** — 4 cups powdered sugar for the buttercream
- **Cream cheese** — 4 oz softened cream cheese for tang and structure in the frosting

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350F (175C). Grease two 8-inch round pans, line the bottoms with parchment, and grease again. Whisk the flour, baking powder, and salt together and set aside.
2. Beat the softened butter, oil, and granulated sugar on medium-high for 3-4 minutes until pale and fluffy. Add the eggs and extra white one at a time, then beat in the vanilla and almond extract, scraping the bowl as you go.
3. With the mixer on low, add the flour mixture in three additions, alternating with the milk and beginning and ending with flour. Mix only until just combined — overmixing makes a tough, gummy cake.
4. Toss the sprinkles with a teaspoon of the measured flour, then gently fold them in by hand so they stay distinct and don't streak the batter.
5. Divide the batter evenly between the pans and smooth the tops. Bake 24-28 minutes, until the centers spring back and a toothpick comes out with a few moist crumbs. Cool in the pans 10 minutes, then turn out onto a rack and cool completely.
6. Make the buttercream: beat the remaining softened butter and the cream cheese until smooth, then add the powdered sugar a cup at a time on low. Beat in the remaining vanilla and a pinch of salt, then whip 2 minutes until light and spreadable.
7. Level the cooled cakes if domed. Set one layer on a plate, spread with a generous layer of frosting, top with the second layer, and frost the top and sides in a smooth or swirled coat.
8. Press extra sprinkles around the bottom edge and scatter more over the top. Chill 20 minutes to set the frosting before slicing into 12 wedges.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Y2kcrazyjoker4 — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-funfetti-birthday-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a5/GermanChocolateCake.jpg" alt="Photo of German Chocolate Cake">

#### 🍫 [German Chocolate Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-german-chocolate-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.86/serving** · 506 kcal · P6 · C71 · F24 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, mild-chocolate layer cake crowned with the cake's defining coconut-pecan filling — a cooked custard of evaporated milk, egg yolks, brown sugar, butter, toasted pecans, and shredded coconut. Despite the name, it's an American classic born from German's Sweet Chocolate; the buttermilk crumb stays soft and the filling does double duty between layers and on top.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar for the cake batter
- **Cocoa powder** — 3/4 cup unsweetened cocoa powder
- **Baking soda** — 1.5 tsp baking soda
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1 tsp salt, split between batter and filling
- **Eggs** — 2 whole for batter, 4 yolks for the coconut-pecan filling
- **Buttermilk** — 1 cup buttermilk
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/2 cup neutral oil for a moist crumb
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla, for batter and filling
- **Brewed coffee** — 1 cup hot brewed coffee to bloom the cocoa
- **Evaporated milk** — 12 oz can, for the coconut-pecan filling
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed light brown sugar for the filling
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1.5 sticks) butter for filling and pan
- **Unsweetened shredded coconut** — 1.5 cups sweetened shredded coconut
- **Pecans** — 1 cup chopped pecans, toasted

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350F. Butter two 9-inch round pans, line bottoms with parchment, and toast the pecans in the oven for 6-8 minutes until fragrant, then chop and set aside.
2. Make the cake batter: whisk flour, granulated sugar, cocoa, baking soda, baking powder, and 1/2 tsp salt in a large bowl. In another bowl whisk 2 whole eggs, buttermilk, oil, and 1 tsp vanilla until smooth.
3. Pour the wet into the dry and whisk just until combined, then stir in the hot coffee — the batter will be thin; that's correct. Divide between the pans and bake 28-32 minutes until a toothpick comes out with a few moist crumbs. Cool 10 minutes, then turn out onto a rack to cool completely.
4. Make the coconut-pecan filling: in a saucepan whisk the evaporated milk, brown sugar, 4 egg yolks, butter, 1/2 tsp salt, and 1 tsp vanilla. Cook over medium heat, stirring constantly, 10-12 minutes until thickened and golden — it should coat the back of a spoon.
5. Off the heat, stir in the shredded coconut and toasted pecans. Spread the filling on a plate and cool until thick and spreadable, about 30 minutes.
6. Level the cooled cakes if domed. Set one layer on a plate and spread with a third of the filling, top with the second layer, and pile the remaining filling on top, spreading to the edges but leaving the sides bare — the traditional look.
7. Let the cake set at room temperature 20 minutes before slicing so the filling firms up.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Tracy Hunter from Kabul, Afghanistan — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-german-chocolate-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Gingerbread_Cookies_on_Table.jpg/480px-Gingerbread_Cookies_on_Table.jpg" alt="Photo of Gingerbread Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Gingerbread Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-gingerbread-cookies/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.34/serving** · 136 kcal · P2 · C19 · F6 · ⏱ 175 min · 🍽 24  

Crisp-edged, chewy-centered cut-out gingerbread spiced with ginger, cinnamon, and cloves and deeply flavored with molasses and brown sugar. The dough is chilled so it rolls cleanly and holds sharp shapes — perfect for decorating.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 3 cups all-purpose
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed
- **Molasses** — 2/3 cup unsulphured
- **Eggs** — 1 egg
- **Ground ginger** — 1 tbsp
- **Cinnamon** — 1.5 tsp
- **Ground cloves** — 1/2 tsp
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp
- **Baking soda** — 3/4 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Powdered sugar** — for royal icing to decorate _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a bowl, whisk together the flour, ground ginger, cinnamon, cloves, nutmeg, baking soda, and salt; set aside.
2. Beat the softened butter with the brown sugar until light and fluffy, 2-3 minutes, then beat in the molasses and egg until smooth.
3. Add the dry ingredients in two additions, mixing on low just until a stiff dough forms and no flour streaks remain — do not overwork it.
4. Divide the dough in half, flatten each into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 2 hours (or overnight) so it firms up and the spices bloom.
5. Heat the oven to 350F and line baking sheets with parchment. Roll one disk on a lightly floured surface to about 1/4 inch thick, keeping the other chilled.
6. Cut out shapes, transfer to the sheets leaving 1 inch between cookies, and re-chill the cut cookies 10 minutes so they keep crisp edges.
7. Bake 8-10 minutes, until the edges are set and the centers look just dry; bake longer for crisp cookies, less for chewy ones.
8. Cool on the sheet 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely before decorating with royal icing made from the powdered sugar.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: GutMaze — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-gingerbread-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Pop-Tarts-Cherry.jpg/480px-Pop-Tarts-Cherry.jpg" alt="Photo of Homemade Pop Tarts">

#### 🍓 [Homemade Pop Tarts](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pop-tarts/)
American · breakfast · medium  
**$0.89/serving** · 406 kcal · P4 · C45 · F23 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 8  

Flaky, buttery from-scratch toaster pastries with a jammy fruit filling and a sweet vanilla glaze. Tender layered crust, crimped edges, and a shower of sprinkles — far better than the boxed kind, and you control how much filling and sugar goes in.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, about 2 cups
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) cold, cubed
- **Sugar** — 2 tbsp in the dough
- **Salt** — 1 tsp
- **Eggs** — 1 egg for the egg wash
- **Jam** — thick fruit jam such as strawberry or raspberry, about 2/3 cup
- **Cornstarch** — 1 tbsp, whisked into jam so filling sets
- **Powdered sugar** — for the glaze, about 1.5 cups
- **Milk** — 2-3 tbsp to thin the glaze
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp in the glaze
- **Chocolate chips** — rainbow sprinkles to finish; use whatever decorating sprinkles you have _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: whisk 2 cups flour, 2 tbsp sugar, and 1 tsp salt. Cut in the cold cubed butter with your fingertips or a pastry cutter until you have pea-sized pieces with some flat flakes. Drizzle in 4-6 tbsp ice water a little at a time, tossing, just until the dough holds together when squeezed — do not overwork it.
2. Divide the dough in half, press each into a rectangle, wrap, and chill at least 30 minutes so the butter firms up and the flour hydrates.
3. Stir 1 tbsp cornstarch into the jam so the filling won't bubble out, then roll each dough half on a floured surface into a roughly 9x12 inch rectangle. Trim the edges square and cut each sheet into 8 equal rectangles (about 3x4 inches), giving you 16 pieces total.
4. Beat the egg with a splash of water. Brush egg wash around the border of 8 rectangles, then spoon about 1 tbsp jam into the center of each, leaving a 1/2 inch border. Lay a second rectangle on top, press out air, and seal the edges firmly with the tines of a fork.
5. Prick the top of each tart a few times with the fork so steam can escape and they bake flat instead of puffing open. Chill the assembled tarts for 20-30 minutes — cold pastry bakes up flakier.
6. Heat the oven to 375F (190C). Brush the tops with the remaining egg wash and bake on a parchment-lined sheet for 22-28 minutes, until golden brown. Cool completely on a rack before glazing or the glaze will melt.
7. Whisk the powdered sugar with the vanilla and just enough milk to make a thick but spreadable glaze. Spread it over each cooled tart, leaving a little border, and scatter sprinkles on top before the glaze sets. Let the glaze firm up for a few minutes before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Public domain · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pop-tarts/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Lemon_Poppy_Seed_Muffins_%283937150356%29.jpg/480px-Lemon_Poppy_Seed_Muffins_%283937150356%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Lemon Poppy Seed Muffins">

#### 🧁 [Lemon Poppy Seed Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-lemon-poppy-seed-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.77/serving** · 246 kcal · P4 · C39 · F8 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 12  

Tender, bakery-style muffins bursting with bright lemon zest and crunchy poppy seeds, topped with a tangy lemon glaze. Buttermilk keeps the crumb soft and moist while the zest does the real flavor work.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 2.5 cups all-purpose flour
- **White sugar** — about 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Poppy seeds** — 3 tablespoons
- **Baking powder** — 2 teaspoons
- **Baking soda** — 1/4 teaspoon
- **Salt** — 3/4 teaspoon
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) melted, slightly cooled
- **Buttermilk** — 3/4 cup
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 3 lemons, rubbed into the sugar
- **Lemon** — 3 lemons, for zest and juice
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 teaspoon
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup, for the glaze
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 2-3 tablespoons fresh lemon juice for glaze; use the zested lemons _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 425F (220C) and line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners. The high starting heat gives the muffins a tall, domed top.
2. In a large bowl, rub the lemon zest into the granulated sugar with your fingertips until damp and fragrant. This releases the citrus oils and is the key to deep lemon flavor.
3. Whisk the flour, poppy seeds, baking powder, baking soda, and salt into the lemon sugar until evenly combined.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk the eggs, melted butter, buttermilk, vanilla, and 2 tablespoons of juice from the zested lemons until smooth.
5. Pour the wet ingredients into the dry and fold gently with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain. Do not overmix, a few lumps are fine, or the muffins will turn tough.
6. Divide the batter among the 12 cups, filling each nearly to the top for bakery-style domes.
7. Bake at 425F for 5 minutes, then reduce the oven to 350F (175C) without opening the door and bake 13-16 minutes more, until golden and a toothpick comes out clean.
8. Cool in the pan 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. Whisk the powdered sugar with enough lemon juice to make a pourable glaze and drizzle over the muffins once they are just warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Katrin Gilger — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-lemon-poppy-seed-muffins/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

---

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Vegan_Magic_Bars_%285384914673%29.jpg/480px-Vegan_Magic_Bars_%285384914673%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Magic Cookie Bars">

#### 🍫 [Magic Cookie Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-magic-cookie-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.87/serving** · 245 kcal · P3 · C28 · F14 · ⏱ 100 min · 🍽 16  

The classic "seven layer" bar: a buttery graham cracker crust layered with chocolate chips, shredded coconut, and toasted nuts, all glued together by a generous pour of sweetened condensed milk that bakes into a chewy, gooey caramel. No mixing bowl required - you just build it in the pan.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Graham crackers** — about 1.5 cups crumbs, ~12 full sheets
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick), melted
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — one 14 oz can
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 1 cup
- **Unsweetened shredded coconut** — 1.33 cups; sweetened flake is traditional if you have it
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup, roughly chopped
- **White chocolate chips** — 1/2 cup for the butterscotch/white layer _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350 F (325 F if using a glass pan). Line a 9x13-inch baking pan with foil or parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides so you can lift the bars out later.
2. Crush the graham crackers into fine crumbs (a zip bag and rolling pin works) and stir together with the melted butter until the mixture looks like wet sand.
3. Press the crumb mixture firmly and evenly into the bottom of the lined pan to form a compact crust; use the flat bottom of a measuring cup to pack it down.
4. Scatter the chocolate chips evenly over the crust, followed by the optional white chocolate chips, then the chopped walnuts, and finally an even blanket of shredded coconut.
5. Slowly and evenly drizzle the entire can of sweetened condensed milk over the top so it seeps into all the layers - do not stir.
6. Bake for 25 to 30 minutes, until the coconut on top is golden brown and the edges are bubbling.
7. Cool the pan completely on a rack, then chill at least 1 hour so the condensed milk sets - cutting warm gives a gooey mess.
8. Lift out using the foil overhang and cut into 16 squares with a sharp knife, wiping the blade between cuts for clean edges.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Veganbaking.net from USA — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-magic-cookie-bars/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Kek_marmar_20230421_121632.jpg/480px-Kek_marmar_20230421_121632.jpg" alt="Photo of Marble Pound Cake">

#### 🍰 [Marble Pound Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-marble-pound-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.74/serving** · 425 kcal · P6 · C55 · F21 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 10  

A tender, buttery loaf where rich vanilla and cocoa batters are swirled into dramatic marbled veins. Built on the classic pound-cake method — creaming softened butter and sugar until pale, then folding in eggs and flour — for a fine, moist crumb with a golden crust. Slices beautifully and keeps for days.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup / 2 sticks unsalted, softened
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated
- **Eggs** — large, room temperature
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups all-purpose
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk, room temp
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Cocoa powder** — 3 tbsp unsweetened cocoa powder, sifted

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 325F (165C). Grease a 9x5-inch loaf pan and line with parchment, leaving an overhang on the long sides. Whisk the flour, baking powder, and salt together in a bowl.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter and sugar with an electric mixer on medium-high for 4-5 minutes until very pale and fluffy. This aerates the batter and is what gives pound cake its lift, so don't rush it.
3. Add the eggs one at a time, beating well after each and scraping the bowl, then beat in the vanilla. The batter should be smooth and thick.
4. On low speed, add the flour mixture in three additions, alternating with the milk and beginning and ending with flour. Mix just until each addition disappears — overmixing makes the cake tough.
5. Scoop about one-third of the batter into a separate bowl and gently fold in the sifted cocoa until evenly colored. Loosen it with 1-2 tsp milk if it stiffens up too much.
6. Spoon the two batters into the pan in alternating dollops, layering vanilla and chocolate. Drag a butter knife or skewer through in a few figure-eight passes to create the marble — swirl just a few times so the colors stay distinct.
7. Bake for 60-70 minutes, until the top is domed and golden and a skewer inserted in the center comes out with only a few moist crumbs. Tent loosely with foil after 45 minutes if the top is browning too fast.
8. Cool in the pan for 15 minutes, then lift out using the parchment and cool completely on a rack before slicing. The crumb sets as it cools, so resist cutting it warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-marble-pound-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b4/Pflaumenkuchen.jpg" alt="Photo of Marian Burros's Original Plum Torte">

#### 🫐 [Marian Burros's Original Plum Torte](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-plum-torte/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.42/serving** · 251 kcal · P3 · C32 · F12 · ⏱ 65 min · 🍽 8  

The legendary New York Times plum torte — a tender, buttery one-bowl cake studded with halved Italian prune plums that sink and caramelize into the batter as it bakes. A crackly cinnamon-sugar lid forms on top, the fruit turns jammy and tart, and the whole thing comes together in about ten minutes of actual work. Originally published by Marian Burros, it ran in the Times every fall for years by popular demand. It is genuinely one of the best things you can do with a bowl of plums.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar, plus 1-2 tbsp for the topping
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup all-purpose flour, sifted
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — pinch of salt
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs
- **Plum sauce** — 12 small Italian prune plums, halved and pitted (this is fresh fruit — measured as 24 halves; not a pantry sauce)
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon, for the topping
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1 tbsp fresh lemon juice, for the topping

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350°F (175°C). Lightly butter a 9- or 10-inch springform pan. If you only have a regular round cake pan, line the bottom with a parchment circle so the torte releases cleanly.
2. Cream the softened butter and 3/4 cup sugar together in a large bowl until pale and fluffy, about 2-3 minutes with a hand mixer. This is the only step that needs real attention — well-creamed butter is what gives the torte its tender crumb.
3. Add the flour, baking powder, salt, and both eggs all at once. Beat just until the batter is smooth and uniform — it will be thick. Do not overmix once the flour is in.
4. Scrape the batter into the prepared springform pan and smooth the top. It is a thin layer; that is correct.
5. Halve and pit the prune plums. Arrange them skin-side up, cut-side down, over the batter, packing them in fairly tightly — they shrink as they bake. Press them lightly into the batter.
6. Sprinkle the top evenly with the lemon juice, then the cinnamon, then 1-2 tbsp sugar (more if your plums are very tart). The sugar and cinnamon form the signature crackly top.
7. Bake for 45 to 55 minutes, until the cake is golden, the center is set, and a toothpick inserted into the cake (not a plum) comes out clean. The fruit will be bubbling and jammy.
8. Cool on a rack at least 15-20 minutes before releasing the springform. Serve warm or at room temperature, plain or with a spoon of lightly whipped cream. It is even better the next day — wrap and keep at room temperature, or freeze well-wrapped for up to 3 months.

</details>

<sub>📷 Jüppsche, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-plum-torte/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Molasses-Cookies-2006March09.JPG/480px-Molasses-Cookies-2006March09.JPG" alt="Photo of Molasses Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Molasses Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-molasses-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.28/serving** · 130 kcal · P2 · C18 · F6 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 24  

Soft, chewy old-fashioned molasses cookies with crackly sugar-crusted tops and a deep warmth from molasses, cinnamon, ginger, and cloves. Crisp at the edges, tender in the middle.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, about 2 1/4 cups
- **Butter** — 1 1/2 sticks, softened
- **Brown sugar** — packed, about 1 cup
- **Molasses** — unsulphured, about 1/3 cup
- **Eggs** — 1 egg
- **Baking soda** — 2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — 1 1/2 tsp
- **Ground ginger** — 1 tsp
- **Ground cloves** — 1/2 tsp
- **Allspice** — 1/4 tsp _(optional)_
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp
- **Turbinado sugar** — for rolling; or granulated sugar

**Steps**

1. Whisk together the flour, baking soda, cinnamon, ground ginger, ground cloves, allspice, and salt in a bowl and set aside.
2. In a large bowl, cream the softened butter with the brown sugar until light and fluffy, about 2-3 minutes.
3. Beat in the molasses, egg, and vanilla until fully combined and smooth, scraping down the bowl.
4. Add the dry ingredients and mix just until a soft dough forms with no streaks of flour. Cover and chill at least 1 hour (the dough is soft and chilling prevents over-spreading).
5. Preheat the oven to 350F and line baking sheets with parchment. Pour the turbinado sugar into a small bowl.
6. Scoop the dough into rounded tablespoon balls, roll each in the coarse sugar to coat, and space about 2 inches apart on the sheets.
7. Bake 9-11 minutes, until the tops are puffed and crackled and the edges are just set but the centers still look slightly underdone.
8. Let cool on the sheet 5 minutes so they finish setting, then transfer to a rack. They firm up chewy as they cool.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Naib — CC BY-SA 2.5 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-molasses-cookies/)</sub>

#### 🍞 [Monkey Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-monkey-bread/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.56/serving** · 333 kcal · P6 · C68 · F4 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 10  

Pull-apart cinnamon-sugar bread made from scratch: pillowy yeasted dough torn into bite-size pieces, rolled in cinnamon sugar, stacked in a bundt pan, and baked under a bubbling brown-butter caramel that soaks into every nook. Sticky, gooey, and meant to be pulled apart with your hands.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 3.5 cups all-purpose flour
- **Whole milk** — 1 cup, warmed to about 110F
- **Instant yeast** — 2.25 tsp (one packet)
- **White sugar** — 1/4 cup for the dough
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Butter** — 2 tbsp softened for dough + 3/4 cup (1.5 sticks) for caramel
- **Salt** — 1 tsp
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed, for caramel + coating
- **White sugar** — 1/3 cup for the cinnamon-sugar coating
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tbsp total, dough coating + caramel
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp in the caramel
- **Walnuts** — 1/2 cup chopped, scattered between layers _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk to about 110F (just barely warm to the touch). Stir in the instant yeast and a pinch of the dough sugar, then let sit 5 minutes until foamy. In a large bowl whisk together flour, the 1/4 cup dough sugar, and salt.
2. Add the yeast-milk, eggs, and 2 tbsp softened butter to the flour. Mix into a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes by hand (or 6 minutes in a stand mixer) until smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky. Add flour a tablespoon at a time only if it stays sticky.
3. Shape into a ball, place in a greased bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot 1 to 1.5 hours until doubled in size.
4. Make the cinnamon-sugar coating: combine the 1/3 cup white sugar, 1/4 cup of the brown sugar, and 1.5 tbsp cinnamon in a bowl. Grease a bundt or tube pan well with butter.
5. Punch down the dough and pinch off pieces about 1 to 1.5 inches across (roughly walnut-sized). Roll each piece in the cinnamon-sugar to coat, then layer loosely in the bundt pan, scattering chopped walnuts between layers if using.
6. Make the caramel: melt the remaining butter in a saucepan over medium heat, then stir in the remaining brown sugar, remaining cinnamon, and vanilla. Cook 1-2 minutes until smooth and bubbling, then pour evenly over the dough balls in the pan.
7. Cover loosely and let rise again 30-45 minutes until the dough balls look puffy and have risen near the rim. Meanwhile heat the oven to 350F.
8. Bake 30-35 minutes until deep golden and the caramel is bubbling vigorously. If the top browns too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last 10 minutes.
9. Let cool in the pan just 5-10 minutes, then invert onto a plate so the caramel runs down. Serve warm and pull apart by hand.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Andrew Currie — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-monkey-bread/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Oatmeal_Raisin_cookie.jpg/480px-Oatmeal_Raisin_cookie.jpg" alt="Photo of Oatmeal Raisin Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Oatmeal Raisin Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-oatmeal-raisin-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.32/serving** · 196 kcal · P3 · C27 · F9 · ⏱ 70 min · 🍽 24  

Chewy, golden oatmeal raisin cookies with crisp edges and a soft center, made with creamed brown butter-style softened butter, plump raisins, and warm cinnamon. The brown sugar and rolled oats give them that classic chewy texture, while a short rest of the dough deepens the flavor.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks), softened
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed light brown sugar
- **White sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **All-purpose flour** — 1.5 cups all-purpose flour
- **Rolled oats** — 3 cups old-fashioned rolled oats
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Cinnamon** — 1.5 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp freshly grated nutmeg _(optional)_
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Raisins** — 1.5 cups raisins
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup chopped walnuts _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Soak the raisins in a small bowl of hot water for 10 minutes, then drain well. This plumps them so they stay soft and chewy in the baked cookies.
2. In a large bowl, cream the softened butter with the brown sugar and granulated sugar using a hand mixer for 2-3 minutes, until light and fluffy. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, cinnamon, nutmeg, and salt. Add to the wet ingredients and mix on low just until combined, with no dry streaks remaining.
4. Fold in the rolled oats, drained raisins, and chopped walnuts by hand until evenly distributed. Cover and chill the dough for at least 30 minutes (up to 24 hours) to firm it up and deepen the flavor.
5. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper.
6. Scoop the dough into 2-tablespoon mounds, spacing them about 2 inches apart. Slightly flatten the tops since these cookies do not spread much.
7. Bake one sheet at a time for 11-13 minutes, until the edges are golden but the centers still look slightly underdone.
8. Let the cookies rest on the hot sheet for 5 minutes to finish setting, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Gaurav Dhwaj Khadka — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-oatmeal-raisin-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Blood_Orange_Chiffon_Cake_with_Mango_Curd_%28Unsplash%29.jpg/480px-Blood_Orange_Chiffon_Cake_with_Mango_Curd_%28Unsplash%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Orange Chiffon Cake">

#### 🍊 [Orange Chiffon Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-orange-chiffon-cake/)
American · snack · medium  
**$0.67/serving** · 324 kcal · P6 · C48 · F13 · ⏱ 150 min · 🍽 12  

A tall, cloud-light orange chiffon cake — the classic American oil-and-egg-white sponge brightened with fresh orange juice and zest. Tender, springy, and moist with a delicate citrus perfume, it bakes in an ungreased tube pan and cools upside down to keep its lofty height. Plain or with a simple orange glaze, it's the cake a confident home baker is proud to set out.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups cake flour (sifted; or all-purpose minus 2 tbsp per cup)
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar, divided (1 cup for the batter, 1/2 cup for the whites)
- **Baking powder** — 1 tbsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1 tsp salt
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/2 cup neutral vegetable oil
- **Eggs** — 7 large eggs, separated (yolks for batter, whites whipped to meringue)
- **Orange juice** — 3/4 cup fresh orange juice (from about 3 oranges)
- **Orange** — 3 oranges — zest all 3 (about 2 tbsp packed zest), juice for the 3/4 cup above
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Cream of tartar** — 1/2 tsp cream of tartar (stabilizes the egg whites)

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the lower third of the oven and preheat to 325°F (165°C). Have a clean, dry 10-inch (16-cup) angel food / tube pan ready — do NOT grease it; the batter needs to grip the sides to climb.
2. Zest the oranges, then juice them to get 3/4 cup juice. In a large bowl, sift together the cake flour, 1 cup of the sugar, baking powder, and salt.
3. Make a well in the dry ingredients and add, in order: the oil, egg yolks, orange juice, vanilla, and orange zest. Whisk until completely smooth and satiny, about 1 minute. Set aside.
4. In a spotlessly clean bowl, beat the egg whites with the cream of tartar on medium until foamy. Gradually rain in the remaining 1/2 cup sugar and increase to high; beat to stiff but not dry peaks — the meringue should hold a firm tip that curls just slightly.
5. Scoop about a third of the meringue into the yolk batter and whisk it in to lighten. Add the remaining meringue and fold gently with a spatula, turning the bowl, until just combined with no white streaks. Do not deflate it.
6. Pour the batter into the ungreased tube pan. Run a knife or thin spatula through the batter in a circle to pop large air pockets, then smooth the top.
7. Bake at 325°F for 55 to 65 minutes, until the top is golden, springs back when lightly pressed, and a skewer comes out clean. The cake should be domed and pulling slightly from the center tube.
8. Immediately invert the pan onto its feet (or hang the center tube over a bottle neck) and cool completely upside down, about 1.5 to 2 hours. This stretches and sets the crumb so it stays tall.
9. Once fully cool, run a thin knife around the outer edge and center tube, lift out the cake, and release the bottom. Serve plain, dusted with powdered sugar, or with a quick orange glaze.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-orange-chiffon-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Parker_House_Rolls_--_2026-05-31.jpg/480px-Parker_House_Rolls_--_2026-05-31.jpg" alt="Photo of Parker House Rolls">

#### 🥐 [Parker House Rolls](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-parker-house-rolls/)
American · dinner · medium  
**$0.35/serving** · 270 kcal · P5 · C35 · F12 · ⏱ 165 min · 🍽 12  

The classic American dinner roll born at Boston's Parker House hotel in the 1870s — soft, buttery, faintly sweet, enriched with milk and egg, then folded into their signature half-moon shape and brushed with melted butter inside and out. Pull-apart tender with a glossy golden top and a fold that hides a layer of butter.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 4 cups all-purpose flour, plus more for dusting
- **Whole milk** — 1 cup whole milk, warmed to about 110F
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup unsalted butter total: 4 tbsp melted into the dough, 8 tbsp melted for folding and brushing
- **Sugar** — 3 tbsp granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, room temperature
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant yeast (one 1/4 oz packet)
- **Salt** — 1 1/2 tsp fine salt
- **Flaky sea salt (Maldon-style)** — 1/2 tsp flaky sea salt for the tops (optional) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk to about 110F (just hotter than body temperature, not steaming) and stir in the sugar. In a large bowl or stand mixer, whisk together the flour, instant yeast, and fine salt. Add the warm milk, the egg, and 4 tbsp of the melted-and-slightly-cooled butter.
2. Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes by hand (or 6-7 minutes on medium-low with a dough hook) until smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky but not sticky. The dough should pass the windowpane test: a small piece stretches thin without tearing. Add flour a tablespoon at a time only if it stays wet.
3. Shape into a ball, place in a lightly buttered bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot until doubled, about 1 to 1 1/2 hours.
4. Gently deflate the dough and turn it onto a lightly floured surface. Divide into 12 equal pieces (about 70g each for evenness). Roll each into a tight ball, then flatten and roll into an oval roughly 4 inches long and 3 inches wide.
5. Brush each oval with melted butter from the remaining 8 tbsp. Use the back of a knife or a chopstick to press a crease just off-center, then fold the larger flap over the smaller so the edges almost meet, forming the signature half-moon. Press the fold lightly to seal.
6. Arrange the folded rolls fold-side-down and barely touching in a buttered 9x13-inch pan (or two 9-inch round pans). Cover loosely and let rise until puffy and the rolls are kissing, 35-45 minutes. Near the end, heat the oven to 375F.
7. Brush the tops generously with melted butter and sprinkle with flaky sea salt if using. Bake 18-22 minutes, until deep golden brown and an internal temperature of about 195F.
8. Brush the hot rolls with the last of the melted butter for shine and flavor. Cool in the pan 5 minutes, then serve warm — they pull apart at the fold.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-parker-house-rolls/)</sub>
