<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/2022-01-01_00_57_47_A_Hershey_Kiss_cookies_in_the_Dulles_section_of_Sterling%2C_Loudoun_County%2C_Virginia.jpg/480px-2022-01-01_00_57_47_A_Hershey_Kiss_cookies_in_the_Dulles_section_of_Sterling%2C_Loudoun_County%2C_Virginia.jpg" alt="Photo of Peanut Butter Blossoms">

#### 🍪 [Peanut Butter Blossoms](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-peanut-butter-blossoms/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.32/serving** · 139 kcal · P3 · C15 · F8 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 36  

The classic American holiday cookie: a soft, chewy peanut butter cookie rolled in sugar and crowned with a chocolate kiss pressed in straight from the oven so it goes molten then sets up into a glossy dome. Faithful to the original Hershey's bake-off recipe, with a proper creamed butter-and-peanut-butter base and a sugar-rolled crust for crackle.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Peanut butter** — 3/4 cup creamy peanut butter
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar, plus extra for rolling
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Milk** — 2 tbsp whole milk
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 3/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Dark chocolate** — about 36 milk chocolate kisses (8 oz), unwrapped

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 375°F (190°C). Line two baking sheets with parchment. Unwrap all the chocolate kisses and keep them nearby — you want to be fast once the cookies come out.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter and peanut butter together until smooth, about 1 minute. Add the granulated sugar and brown sugar and cream until light and fluffy, 2 to 3 minutes, scraping down the bowl once.
3. Beat in the egg, milk, and vanilla until fully combined and smooth.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, baking powder, and salt. Add to the wet ingredients and mix on low just until a soft, slightly sticky dough forms — do not overmix.
5. Pour a few tablespoons of granulated sugar into a small bowl. Roll the dough into 1-inch balls (about 1 tablespoon each), then roll each ball in the sugar to coat. Space them about 2 inches apart on the prepared sheets.
6. Bake one sheet at a time for 8 to 10 minutes, until the cookies are set at the edges and lightly golden but still soft in the center. Do not overbake — they firm up as they cool.
7. Immediately remove from the oven and press one chocolate kiss firmly into the center of each cookie so it sinks in and the edges crack slightly. The kiss will look melty — that's correct.
8. Let the cookies rest on the sheet for 3 to 4 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack. Cool completely (and ideally let the kisses fully re-set, about 1 hour) before stacking or storing, or the chocolate will smear.

</details>

<sub>📷 Famartin, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-peanut-butter-blossoms/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

---

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Sticky_Bun_and_Nuts_Loaf_2300px.jpg/480px-Sticky_Bun_and_Nuts_Loaf_2300px.jpg" alt="Photo of Pecan Sticky Buns">

#### 🍯 [Pecan Sticky Buns](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-sticky-buns/)
American · breakfast · hard  
**$1.20/serving** · 205 kcal · P5 · C41 · F2 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 12  

Pillowy enriched-dough buns baked upside-down over a bubbling brown-sugar caramel studded with toasted pecans, then flipped so the glaze drips down the sides. A true overnight-friendly bake with a tight cinnamon swirl and a glossy, sticky top.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, ~4 cups for dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (1 packet)
- **Whole milk** — 1 cup, warmed to 110F
- **Sugar** — 1/4 cup for dough
- **Eggs** — room temperature
- **Butter** — softened for dough + caramel + filling
- **Salt** — 1 tsp fine salt
- **Brown sugar** — packed, for caramel + cinnamon filling
- **Corn syrup** — 1/4 cup light, keeps caramel glossy
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp into the caramel _(optional)_
- **Pecans** — 1 1/2 cups, toasted and chopped
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tbsp ground for the swirl
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp in caramel

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk to about 110F and whisk in the yeast and a pinch of the sugar; let it foam for 5 minutes. In a large bowl combine the flour, remaining dough sugar, and salt, then add the yeast mixture, eggs, and 4 tbsp softened butter. Mix to a shaggy dough.
2. Knead 8-10 minutes by hand (or 6 minutes in a stand mixer) until smooth, elastic, and tacky but not sticky. Cover and let rise in a warm spot until doubled, about 1 to 1.5 hours.
3. Make the caramel: melt 8 tbsp butter with most of the brown sugar, the corn syrup, honey, and vanilla until smooth and bubbling, about 2 minutes. Pour into a 9x13 pan and scatter the toasted chopped pecans evenly over the top.
4. Punch down the dough and roll on a floured surface into a roughly 12x18 inch rectangle. Spread with the remaining 4 tbsp softened butter, then sprinkle evenly with the remaining brown sugar mixed with the cinnamon, leaving a 1 inch border at the far edge.
5. Starting from a long edge, roll the dough tightly into a log and pinch the seam closed. Using a length of unflavored floss or a sharp serrated knife, cut into 12 even rolls.
6. Arrange the rolls cut-side up over the caramel in the pan, leaving a little space between them. Cover and let rise until puffy and touching, 45-60 minutes (or refrigerate overnight and let come to room temp before baking).
7. Bake at 350F for 30-35 minutes until deeply golden and the caramel bubbles up the sides; tent with foil if browning too fast. The center buns should read 190F.
8. Cool in the pan for just 3-5 minutes, then invert onto a rimmed platter or board so the caramel and pecans drip down over the buns. Scrape any caramel left in the pan back on top.
9. Let rest 10 minutes so the glaze sets slightly, then pull apart and serve warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Derek Ramsey (Ram-Man) — CC BY-SA 2.5 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-sticky-buns/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Pumpkin_muffin_%2831373163026%29.jpg/480px-Pumpkin_muffin_%2831373163026%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Pumpkin Muffins">

#### 🎃 [Pumpkin Muffins](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pumpkin-muffins/)
American · breakfast · easy  
**$0.47/serving** · 244 kcal · P3 · C36 · F10 · ⏱ 40 min · 🍽 12  

Tender, deeply spiced bakery-style pumpkin muffins with high domed tops. Real pumpkin puree keeps them moist for days, while a blend of cinnamon, ginger, nutmeg, and cloves gives that classic fall warmth. One bowl, no mixer needed.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, about 1.75 cups
- **Pumpkin puree (canned)** — pure pumpkin puree, not pie filling (~1.25 cups)
- **White sugar** — granulated sugar, about 0.75 cup
- **Brown sugar** — packed light brown sugar, about 0.5 cup
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Vegetable oil** — neutral oil, about 0.5 cup
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp
- **Baking soda** — 0.5 tsp
- **Salt** — 0.5 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — 1.5 tsp
- **Ground ginger** — 0.5 tsp
- **Nutmeg** — 0.25 tsp, freshly grated if possible
- **Ground cloves** — scant 0.25 tsp _(optional)_
- **Turbinado sugar** — for sprinkling tops, optional crunch _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Preheat oven to 425F and line a 12-cup muffin tin with paper liners (or grease well). The hot start gives the muffins a fast lift for tall, domed tops.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, baking soda, salt, cinnamon, ginger, nutmeg, and cloves until evenly blended.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk the pumpkin puree, white sugar, brown sugar, eggs, oil, and vanilla until smooth and uniform.
4. Pour the wet mixture into the dry ingredients and fold gently with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain. Do not overmix or the muffins will turn dense and rubbery.
5. Divide the batter evenly among the 12 cups, filling each nearly to the top. Sprinkle the tops with turbinado sugar for a crackly crust if using.
6. Bake at 425F for 5 minutes, then without opening the oven reduce the temperature to 350F and bake another 15 to 18 minutes, until a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean.
7. Let the muffins cool in the tin for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Luca Nebuloni — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pumpkin-muffins/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Pumpkin_five_spice_sweet_roll_with_cream_cheese_sweet_roll_icing.jpg/480px-Pumpkin_five_spice_sweet_roll_with_cream_cheese_sweet_roll_icing.jpg" alt="Photo of Pumpkin Roll">

#### 🎃 [Pumpkin Roll](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pumpkin-roll/)
American · snack · medium  
**$1.04/serving** · 283 kcal · P5 · C30 · F17 · ⏱ 150 min · 🍽 10  

A classic fall spiral cake: a thin, warmly spiced pumpkin sponge rolled around a tangy cream cheese filling. The trick is rolling the cake while it's still hot so it "remembers" the curl and won't crack, then unrolling, filling, and re-rolling once cool. Dusted with powdered sugar and sliced into pretty pinwheels.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — large, room temperature
- **White sugar** — 1 cup granulated for the sponge
- **Pumpkin puree (canned)** — 2/3 cup pure pumpkin (not pie filling)
- **All-purpose flour** — 3/4 cup all-purpose
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tsp ground
- **Ground ginger** — 1/2 tsp
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp ground
- **Ground cloves** — 1/4 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp
- **Cream cheese** — 8 oz block, softened, for the filling
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp unsalted, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 cup for filling, plus extra for dusting and rolling
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp extract
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup chopped, optional, sprinkled on batter before baking _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Preheat oven to 375F. Line a 15x10 jelly-roll pan with parchment, then grease the parchment. Lay out a clean thin kitchen towel and dust it generously with powdered sugar.
2. Whisk together the flour, baking powder, baking soda, cinnamon, ginger, nutmeg, cloves, and salt in a bowl. Set aside.
3. In a large bowl, beat the eggs and granulated sugar on high until thick and pale, about 2-3 minutes, then beat in the pumpkin puree. Gently fold in the dry ingredients just until no streaks remain.
4. Spread the batter evenly into the prepared pan. If using, sprinkle the chopped walnuts over the top. Bake 13-15 minutes, until the cake springs back when lightly touched in the center.
5. Immediately invert the hot cake onto the powdered-sugar towel and peel off the parchment. Starting from a short end, roll the cake up in the towel while still hot. Cool completely, seam-side down, on a rack (about 1 hour).
6. Make the filling: beat the cream cheese, softened butter, 1 cup powdered sugar, and vanilla until smooth and fluffy.
7. Gently unroll the cooled cake just until flat. Spread the filling evenly over the surface, leaving a 1/2-inch border at the far end.
8. Re-roll the cake snugly (without the towel this time), wrap in plastic, and chill at least 1 hour to firm up.
9. Dust with powdered sugar, trim the ends, and slice into 1-inch pinwheels to serve.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Chelsea Nesvig — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-pumpkin-roll/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Raspberry_Shortbread_Bars.jpg/480px-Raspberry_Shortbread_Bars.jpg" alt="Photo of Raspberry Crumble Bars">

#### 🍓 [Raspberry Crumble Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-raspberry-crumble-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.49/serving** · 257 kcal · P2 · C36 · F12 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 16  

Buttery shortbread that pulls double duty as both the pressed base and the craggy crumble top, sandwiching a layer of bright raspberry jam. One bowl, one dough, no mixer required — the kind of bake-sale bar that disappears before it fully cools. A whisper of almond and lemon zest sharpens the raspberry so it tastes like fruit rather than just sweet.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cut into cubes
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Almond extract** — 1/4 tsp almond extract
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest (about 1/2 lemon)
- **Jam** — 1 cup good raspberry jam or preserves
- **Raspberries** — 1 cup fresh raspberries (one 6 oz container), optional _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 375 F (190 C). Line an 8x8 or 9x9-inch baking pan with parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides so you can lift the bars out later. Lightly butter the parchment.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, sugar, salt, baking powder, and lemon zest. Add the cold cubed butter and cut it in with a pastry cutter or your fingertips until the mixture looks like coarse, sandy crumbs with some pea-size bits of butter remaining.
3. Whisk the egg with the vanilla and almond extract, then drizzle it over the flour mixture. Toss and rub with your hands until the dough just comes together into clumps — it should hold when pressed but still be crumbly, not a smooth ball.
4. Press a little under two-thirds of the dough firmly and evenly into the bottom of the prepared pan to form the base. Pack it tight with the flat bottom of a measuring cup so the layer is compact.
5. Stir the jam to loosen it, then spread it over the base in an even layer, stopping about 1/4 inch from the edges. Scatter the fresh raspberries over the jam if using, pressing them in lightly.
6. Squeeze the remaining dough into clumps and crumble it over the top, leaving gaps so the jam peeks through. Don't cover it completely — the open patches are what give these bars their look.
7. Bake for 35 to 40 minutes, until the crumble top is golden and the jam is bubbling at the edges. If the top browns too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last 10 minutes.
8. Cool completely in the pan on a rack — at least 2 hours — so the jam sets. Lift out using the parchment, then cut into 16 squares with a sharp knife, wiping the blade between cuts.

</details>

<sub>📷 Justina Elumeze, CC BY 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-raspberry-crumble-bars/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Vegan_Magic_Bars_%285384914673%29.jpg/480px-Vegan_Magic_Bars_%285384914673%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Seven Layer Magic Bars">

#### 🍫 [Seven Layer Magic Bars](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-seven-layer-magic-bars/)
American · snack · easy  
**$1.03/serving** · 228 kcal · P3 · C26 · F13 · ⏱ 170 min · 🍽 24  

The classic Eagle Brand "Magic Cookie Bar" — also known as Hello Dolly or Seven Layer Bars. A buttery graham cracker crust is layered with sweetened shredded coconut, chocolate and butterscotch-style chips, and chopped pecans, all bound by a poured-over blanket of sweetened condensed milk that bakes into chewy, caramelized, sliceable bars. No bowl, no creaming — you build it right in the pan, layer by layer.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, melted
- **Graham crackers** — 1 1/2 cups graham cracker crumbs (about 12 full sheets, finely crushed)
- **Unsweetened shredded coconut** — 1 1/3 cups sweetened shredded coconut
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 1 cup semi-sweet chocolate chips
- **White chocolate chips** — 1 cup butterscotch chips (sub white-chocolate chips)
- **Pecans** — 1 cup pecans, coarsely chopped (about 4 oz)
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — 1 (14 oz) can sweetened condensed milk

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 350°F (175°C). Line a 9x13-inch baking pan with foil or parchment, leaving an overhang on two sides for easy lifting later.
2. Pour the melted butter evenly over the bottom of the pan. Scatter the graham cracker crumbs over the butter and press into an even, compact layer with the back of a measuring cup or your fingers. This is the crust — don't pre-bake it.
3. Sprinkle the shredded coconut evenly over the crust in a single even layer.
4. Scatter the chocolate chips and butterscotch (white) chips evenly over the coconut, then top with the chopped pecans, distributing everything edge to edge.
5. Slowly and evenly drizzle the entire can of sweetened condensed milk over the top, covering as much surface as possible. Do NOT stir — the milk soaks down through the layers as it bakes. Gently press the surface so the loose toppings adhere.
6. Bake 25 to 30 minutes, until the top is lightly golden and the edges are bubbling and just browned. The center will still look slightly soft — that's correct; it sets as it cools.
7. Cool completely in the pan on a wire rack at least 2 hours (or refrigerate 1 hour) so the condensed milk firms up and the bars slice cleanly.
8. Using the overhang, lift the slab out and cut into 24 bars with a sharp knife, wiping the blade between cuts for clean edges.

</details>

<sub>📷 Veganbaking.net, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-seven-layer-magic-bars/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/e6/Smith_Island_Cake_-_2018_-_Sarah_Stierch.jpg" alt="Photo of Smith Island Cake">

#### 🍰 [Smith Island Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-smith-island-cake/)
American · snack · hard  
**$1.21/serving** · 587 kcal · P7 · C85 · F26 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 12  

Maryland's official state dessert: a towering cake of 8 to 10 paper-thin tender yellow layers stacked with a glossy cooked chocolate fudge frosting that firms up between every layer. Born on Smith Island in the Chesapeake Bay, the thin layers were said to survive the boat trip out to the watermen better than a tall fluffy cake. It is humble in ingredients but genuinely impressive on the plate, and the cooked fudge icing (not buttercream) is what makes it the real thing.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 5 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Milk** — 1 cup whole milk, room temperature
- **Evaporated milk** — 1 can (12 oz) evaporated milk, for the fudge frosting
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar, for the fudge frosting
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, for the fudge frosting
- **Cocoa powder** — 3/4 cup unsweetened cocoa powder, for the fudge frosting
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract, for the fudge frosting
- **Salt** — pinch of salt, for the fudge frosting

**Steps**

1. Make the batter: Heat the oven to 350F. Grease three 9-inch round cake pans (you will reuse them in batches). Whisk together the 2 1/2 cups flour, baking powder, and 1/2 tsp salt. In a large bowl, beat the 1 cup softened butter with the 2 cups sugar until pale and fluffy, 3 to 4 minutes. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the 2 tsp vanilla. Add the flour mixture in three additions alternating with the 1 cup milk, beginning and ending with flour, mixing just until smooth.
2. Bake the thin layers: For each layer, spread a scant 3/4 cup of batter very thinly and evenly to the edges of a greased pan (an offset spatula helps). Bake 8 to 10 minutes, just until the layer is set and barely golden; do not let it brown or dry out. The goal is 8 to 10 ultra-thin layers, so work in batches, cooling and re-greasing pans between rounds.
3. Cool the layers: Run a knife around each layer, turn out onto a rack, and cool completely. Stack the cooled layers with parchment or wax paper between them so they do not stick while you finish the rest.
4. Make the cooked fudge frosting: In a heavy saucepan, combine the evaporated milk, 1 1/2 cups sugar, 1/2 cup butter, 3/4 cup cocoa, and a pinch of salt. Cook over medium heat, stirring constantly, until the butter melts and the mixture comes to a gentle boil.
5. Cook to a spreadable fudge: Let it boil gently, stirring often, for about 5 to 7 minutes until slightly thickened and glossy. Remove from heat and stir in the 2 tsp vanilla. Let the frosting cool and thicken, stirring now and then, until it is the consistency of thick fudge sauce that coats a spoon but is still spreadable; if it stiffens too much, rewarm gently.
6. Stack the cake: Place one layer on a cake plate and spread a thin, even layer of warm fudge frosting (about 3 tablespoons) to the edges. Repeat, stacking all the layers and frosting each one, keeping the stack straight as it grows tall.
7. Frost the outside: Pour and spread the remaining fudge frosting over the top and down the sides, smoothing it so it drips slightly and seals the whole cake. The frosting will set up firm and shiny as it cools.
8. Set and serve: Let the cake stand at room temperature (or chill briefly) until the frosting firms between the layers, at least 1 hour. Slice into thin wedges with a sharp knife to show off all the layers.

</details>

<sub>📷 Missvain, CC BY 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-smith-island-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Dinner_rolls_-_Massachusetts.jpg/480px-Dinner_rolls_-_Massachusetts.jpg" alt="Photo of Soft Pull-Apart Dinner Rolls">

#### 🍞 [Soft Pull-Apart Dinner Rolls](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-dinner-rolls/)
American · dinner · medium  
**$0.25/serving** · 203 kcal · P4 · C32 · F6 · ⏱ 150 min · 🍽 12  

Pillowy, buttery, golden-topped dinner rolls baked in a single pan so they pull apart in soft, fluffy clouds. An enriched milk-and-butter dough with a touch of egg gives them a tender crumb and a faint sweetness — the kind you tear into warm at the table.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 3 1/2 cups all-purpose or bread flour
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (1 packet)
- **Whole milk** — 1 cup, warmed to ~110F
- **Butter** — 6 tbsp total: 4 in dough, 2 melted for brushing
- **Eggs** — 1 large, room temperature
- **White sugar** — 3 tbsp
- **Kosher salt** — 1 1/4 tsp
- **Honey** — 1 tsp brushed on top for shine (optional) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk to about 110F (lukewarm, not hot). Stir in the sugar and yeast and let it sit 5-10 minutes until foamy. If it does not foam, the yeast is dead or the milk was too hot — start over.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour and salt. Add the foamy milk mixture, the egg, and 4 tbsp softened butter. Mix until a shaggy dough forms.
3. Knead 8-10 minutes by hand (or 5-6 minutes in a stand mixer with the dough hook) until the dough is smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky but not sticky. Add flour a tablespoon at a time only if needed.
4. Place the dough in a lightly oiled bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot for 60-90 minutes, until doubled in size.
5. Punch down the dough and divide into 12 equal pieces (about 65g each). Shape each into a tight ball by cupping and dragging it against the counter to build surface tension, then arrange in a buttered 9x13 inch pan.
6. Cover loosely and let the rolls proof a second time for 30-45 minutes, until puffy and touching. Meanwhile, heat the oven to 375F.
7. Bake 18-22 minutes, until the tops are deep golden brown and the internal temperature reaches about 190F. Rotate the pan halfway through for even color.
8. Brush the warm rolls generously with the remaining 2 tbsp melted butter (and honey, if using) for a glossy, soft top. Cool 5 minutes, then pull apart and serve warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-dinner-rolls/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fa/20061001_sugar_cookies.jpg" alt="Photo of Sugar Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Sugar Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-sugar-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.24/serving** · 178 kcal · P2 · C25 · F8 · ⏱ 100 min · 🍽 24  

Buttery, tender classic cut-out sugar cookies with crisp edges and a soft center. Creamed butter and sugar, real vanilla, and a proper chill give them clean shapes and a melt-in-your-mouth crumb — perfect plain or iced.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks), softened
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp pure vanilla extract
- **Almond extract** — 1/4 tsp, for classic bakery flavor _(optional)_
- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Powdered sugar** — for a simple icing, with a splash of milk _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, cream the softened butter and granulated sugar together with a mixer on medium-high for 2-3 minutes until pale and fluffy.
2. Beat in the egg, vanilla, and almond extract (if using) until fully combined, scraping down the bowl.
3. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, and salt. Add to the wet ingredients in two additions, mixing on low just until a soft dough forms — do not overmix.
4. Divide the dough in half, flatten into discs, wrap, and chill at least 1 hour (or up to 2 days) so the butter firms up and shapes hold.
5. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line baking sheets with parchment. Roll one disc on a lightly floured surface to about 1/4 inch thick and cut out shapes.
6. Transfer cookies to the sheets, spacing 1 inch apart. Re-chill the cut cookies for 10 minutes if the dough has softened.
7. Bake 8-10 minutes, until the edges are just barely golden and the centers look set but pale — they firm up as they cool.
8. Cool on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely.
9. If icing, whisk powdered sugar with a few drops of milk and vanilla to a spreadable glaze, decorate the fully cooled cookies, and let set.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Jennifer — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-sugar-cookies/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Thumbprint_Cookies_%287712226646%29.jpg/480px-Thumbprint_Cookies_%287712226646%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Thumbprint Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Thumbprint Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-thumbprint-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.32/serving** · 181 kcal · P2 · C25 · F8 · ⏱ 35 min · 🍽 12  

Buttery shortbread cookies rolled in a light coating, pressed with a thumb to make a well, and filled with a glossy pool of fruit jam. Tender, crumbly, and not too sweet — a classic that bakes up in under half an hour.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 stick (1/2 cup) unsalted, softened
- **Sugar** — about 1/2 cup white sugar
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, yolk for dough + a little white for coating
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **All-purpose flour** — about 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Jam** — about 1/3 cup raspberry or strawberry jam for filling
- **Walnuts** — finely chopped, for coating (or use almonds) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C) and line a baking sheet with parchment. Separate the egg, keeping the yolk for the dough and saving the white in a small bowl.
2. Beat the softened butter and sugar together until pale and fluffy, 2-3 minutes. Beat in the egg yolk and vanilla until smooth.
3. Add the flour and salt and mix on low just until a soft, cohesive dough forms — do not overmix. If it feels sticky, chill the dough 15-20 minutes.
4. Roll the dough into 12 walnut-sized balls (about 1 inch each). For a coated version, roll each ball through the lightly beaten egg white, then in the chopped nuts.
5. Place the balls 2 inches apart on the sheet. Press a deep well into the center of each with your thumb or the rounded end of a wooden spoon.
6. Spoon roughly 1/2 teaspoon of jam into each well, filling it just below the rim so it does not overflow while baking.
7. Bake 12-15 minutes, until the edges are set and just turning golden; the centers should stay pale. If the wells puffed shut, gently re-press them right out of the oven.
8. Cool on the sheet 5 minutes to firm up, then transfer to a rack to cool completely before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Ralph Daily from Birmingham, United States — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-thumbprint-cookies/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

---

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/23/Depression_Cake.JPG" alt="Photo of Wacky Depression Cake">

#### 🍫 [Wacky Depression Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-wacky-depression-cake/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.23/serving** · 243 kcal · P3 · C40 · F9 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 9  

The classic 1930s-40s eggless, milkless, butterless chocolate cake — mixed right in the pan with no bowl. Its tender, springy crumb comes from the reaction of vinegar and baking soda, and it bakes up moist and deeply chocolatey despite using only pantry staples. A genuine piece of American baking history that tastes far better than its humble ingredient list suggests.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Cocoa powder** — 1/4 cup unsweetened cocoa powder
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/3 cup vegetable oil
- **Vinegar** — 1 tbsp white vinegar
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Water** — 1 cup cold water

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C). You do not need to grease an ungreased 8x8-inch metal or glass baking pan — the cake is mixed directly in it.
2. Add the flour, sugar, cocoa powder, baking soda, and salt directly into the ungreased pan. Whisk or stir with a fork until evenly combined and no streaks of cocoa remain, then smooth the dry mixture flat and level.
3. Using the back of a spoon, make three wells in the dry mixture: one large and two small. Pour the vegetable oil into the large well, the vinegar into one small well, and the vanilla into the other small well. (Keeping the vinegar separate until the very end protects the leavening reaction.)
4. Pour the cold water evenly over everything. Stir with a fork, working from the center outward, until the batter is smooth and uniform with no dry pockets in the corners — about 1 minute. Do not overmix; a few tiny lumps are fine. The batter will be thin and bubble slightly as the vinegar hits the baking soda.
5. Quickly scrape down the sides and level the batter. Place the pan in the oven immediately so the leavening is still active.
6. Bake for 30 to 35 minutes, until the top springs back when lightly pressed and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean or with a few moist crumbs.
7. Cool the cake in the pan on a wire rack for at least 20 minutes before cutting. Serve straight from the pan dusted with powdered sugar, or frost once fully cooled.

</details>

<sub>📷 Lee Khatchadourian-Reese, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-wacky-depression-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/White_chocolate_and_macadamia_cookies.jpg/480px-White_chocolate_and_macadamia_cookies.jpg" alt="Photo of White Chocolate Macadamia Cookies">

#### 🍪 [White Chocolate Macadamia Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-white-chocolate-macadamia-cookies/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.61/serving** · 191 kcal · P3 · C22 · F11 · ⏱ 95 min · 🍽 24  

The classic bakery-style cookie: golden, chewy in the center with crisp edges, studded with sweet white chocolate and buttery macadamia nuts. A proper creaming method and an overnight chill give these their signature depth and structure.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups all-purpose flour
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed light brown sugar
- **White sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **White chocolate chips** — 1 cup white chocolate chips (or chopped white chocolate)
- **Cashews** — 1 cup roasted macadamia nuts, roughly chopped (cashews as closest catalog match)

**Steps**

1. Whisk the flour, baking soda, and salt together in a medium bowl and set aside.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter with the brown sugar and granulated sugar on medium-high speed for 2 to 3 minutes, until pale, light, and fluffy. This aeration is what gives the cookies their tender lift.
3. Beat in the eggs one at a time, then the vanilla, scraping down the bowl until the batter is smooth and emulsified.
4. Add the dry ingredients and mix on low just until no dry streaks remain. Do not overmix. Fold in the white chocolate chips and chopped macadamia nuts by hand.
5. Cover and chill the dough for at least 1 hour, ideally overnight. Chilling firms the butter so the cookies spread less and deepens the flavor. When ready to bake, preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment.
6. Scoop the dough into 2-tablespoon mounds (about 1.5 oz each), spacing them 2 inches apart on the prepared sheets. Press a few extra nuts and chips on top of each for a bakery look.
7. Bake one sheet at a time for 11 to 13 minutes, until the edges are set and lightly golden but the centers still look slightly underdone and soft. They will finish setting as they cool.
8. Let the cookies rest on the hot sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely. Store in an airtight container for up to 4 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Andie712b, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-white-chocolate-macadamia-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Costata_Romanesco_zucchini_and_zucchini_bread.jpg/480px-Costata_Romanesco_zucchini_and_zucchini_bread.jpg" alt="Photo of Zucchini Bread">

#### 🍞 [Zucchini Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-zucchini-bread/)
American · snack · easy  
**$0.50/serving** · 243 kcal · P3 · C35 · F11 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 12  

A moist, tender quick bread laced with grated zucchini, warm cinnamon and nutmeg, and toasted walnuts. The zucchini melts into the crumb, keeping each slice soft for days while adding almost no flavor of its own — just deep, golden, lightly spiced sweetness.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Zucchini** — about 2 medium, grated to ~1.5 packed cups
- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose, ~1.5 cups
- **Sugar** — ~3/4 cup granulated
- **Brown sugar** — ~1/2 cup packed light brown
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Vegetable oil** — ~1/2 cup neutral oil
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp
- **Baking soda** — 3/4 tsp
- **Baking powder** — 1/2 tsp
- **Cinnamon** — 1.5 tsp ground
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp freshly grated
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp
- **Walnuts** — ~1/2 cup chopped, toasted _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Grease a 9x5-inch loaf pan and line the bottom with parchment, leaving an overhang for easy lifting.
2. Grate the zucchini on the large holes of a box grater (no need to peel). Pile it in a clean kitchen towel and squeeze firmly to remove excess water — discard the liquid. You want about 1.5 packed cups of squeezed zucchini.
3. In a large bowl, whisk the eggs, granulated sugar, brown sugar, oil, and vanilla until smooth and slightly thickened, about 1 minute. Stir in the grated zucchini.
4. In a separate bowl, whisk together the flour, baking soda, baking powder, cinnamon, nutmeg, and salt.
5. Add the dry ingredients to the wet and fold with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — do not overmix, or the bread will turn dense and tough. Gently fold in the toasted walnuts if using.
6. Scrape the batter into the prepared pan and smooth the top. Bake for 50 to 60 minutes, until the top is domed and golden and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out with only a few moist crumbs.
7. Cool in the pan on a rack for 15 minutes, then lift out using the parchment and cool completely on the rack before slicing — this lets the crumb set so it slices cleanly.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Temaciejewski — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-zucchini-bread/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/40/Homemade_salmon_bagel_sandwich_1.jpg" alt="Photo of Homemade Bagels">

#### 🥯 [Homemade Bagels](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-homemade-bagels/)
American (Jewish-American) · breakfast · medium  
**$0.26/serving** · 281 kcal · P8 · C58 · F1 · ⏱ 160 min · 🍽 8  

Chewy, glossy New York-style bagels made from scratch with a stiff, low-hydration dough. The defining move is a quick boil in baking-soda-and-honey water before baking, which sets the crust and gives that classic dense, chewy bite. Top with everything seasoning or leave them plain.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — high-gluten or bread flour, about 4.5 cups
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant/rapid-rise yeast
- **Honey** — 2 tbsp total: ~1 tbsp in dough (or use barley malt syrup), 1 tbsp in boiling water
- **Salt** — 2 1/2 tsp fine salt
- **Water** — ~1 1/2 cups warm water for the dough
- **Baking soda** — 2 tbsp for the boiling water bath
- **Everything bagel seasoning** — everything bagel seasoning for topping _(optional)_
- **Eggs** — 1 egg beaten for egg wash so toppings stick _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: In a large bowl, stir together the bread flour, instant yeast, and salt. Add 1 tablespoon honey and the warm water, then mix into a shaggy mass. The dough should be quite stiff and dry, not sticky; add water 1 teaspoon at a time only if there's dry flour left.
2. Knead vigorously on an unfloured counter for 8-10 minutes until smooth and very firm. Stiff bagel dough is hard work; rest it for 2 minutes mid-knead if it fights back. Place in a lightly oiled bowl, cover, and let rise 1 to 1.5 hours until puffy but not doubled.
3. Divide and shape: Punch down and divide into 8 equal pieces (about 113 g each). Roll each into a tight ball, then poke a thumb through the center and gently stretch the hole to about 1.5 inches wide, since it will shrink as it proofs and bakes.
4. Proof and chill: Place shaped bagels on a parchment-lined, lightly oiled tray. Cover and rest 20-30 minutes until slightly puffy. For best flavor and crust, refrigerate uncovered 8-24 hours; otherwise proceed once they float (drop one in water to test).
5. Boil: Bring a wide pot of water to a gentle boil and add 2 tablespoons baking soda plus 1 tablespoon honey. Boil bagels 2-4 at a time, 60 seconds per side for chewy, 30 seconds for softer. Lift out with a slotted spoon and drain briefly.
6. Top: Transfer boiled bagels to a parchment-lined baking sheet. While still wet (or brushed with beaten egg wash), sprinkle generously with everything bagel seasoning, pressing lightly so it adheres.
7. Bake at 450F (230C) for 18-22 minutes, rotating the pan halfway, until deep golden brown and the crust sounds hollow when tapped.
8. Cool on a wire rack at least 20 minutes before slicing; bagels finish setting their crumb as they cool, so cutting too early makes them gummy.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: 에그란 — CC BY 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-homemade-bagels/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/56/Sugar_Cream_Pie_in_Indiana_-_Stierch.jpg" alt="Photo of Hoosier Sugar Cream Pie">

#### 🥧 [Hoosier Sugar Cream Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-hoosier-sugar-cream-pie/)
American (Midwestern) · snack · medium  
**$0.57/serving** · 315 kcal · P2 · C51 · F11 · ⏱ 200 min · 🍽 8  

Indiana's official state pie — a silky, custard-like "desperation pie" with no eggs. A cream and sugar filling is thickened with flour, poured into a flaky single crust, dusted with cinnamon and dotted with butter, then baked until the top blisters golden and the center sets to a barely-jiggling, caramel-sweet cream. Old-fashioned, frugal, and genuinely one of the great American pies.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour, divided (1 1/4 cups for crust, ~4 tbsp for filling)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (8 tbsp) cold unsalted butter for the crust + ~5 tbsp for the filling and topping
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt, divided between crust and filling
- **Water** — 3-4 tbsp ice water for the crust
- **Heavy cream** — 2 cups heavy cream
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar
- **Brown sugar** — 1/4 cup packed light brown sugar (for deeper caramel notes) _(optional)_
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon, for dusting the top
- **Nutmeg** — 1/4 tsp freshly grated nutmeg, for dusting _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: Whisk 1 1/4 cups flour with 1/2 tsp salt. Cut in 1/2 cup (8 tbsp) cold cubed butter with your fingers or a pastry cutter until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs with some pea-sized bits. Drizzle in 3-4 tbsp ice water, tossing just until the dough holds together when squeezed. Pat into a disk, wrap, and chill 30 minutes.
2. Roll the chilled dough on a floured surface into a 12-inch round. Drape into a 9-inch pie plate, trim to a 1-inch overhang, tuck under, and crimp the edge. Freeze the shell for 15 minutes. Meanwhile, set a rack in the lower-middle of the oven and heat to 375F (190C).
3. Blind-bake: Line the shell with parchment and fill with pie weights or dried beans. Bake 15 minutes, remove the parchment and weights, and bake 5-7 minutes more until the bottom looks dry and pale-gold. Set aside. Keep the oven at 375F.
4. Make the filling: In a heavy saucepan off the heat, whisk together 1 cup sugar, the optional 1/4 cup brown sugar, ~4 tbsp flour, and 1/4 tsp salt until no lumps remain. Slowly whisk in the 2 cups heavy cream until smooth.
5. Cook the cream: Set the pan over medium heat and cook, whisking constantly and scraping the corners, until it thickens to a loose pudding that coats the back of a spoon, about 8-12 minutes. Do not let it boil hard or it will break — keep it at a bare simmer. Off the heat, whisk in 2 tbsp butter and the 2 tsp vanilla until glossy.
6. Pour the warm filling into the par-baked shell. Dot the surface with the remaining 3 tbsp butter cut into small bits, then dust evenly with 1 tsp cinnamon and the optional nutmeg.
7. Bake at 375F for 25-30 minutes, until the top is browned and bubbling in spots and the center jiggles only slightly when nudged — it will look loose but sets as it cools. If the crust edge browns too fast, shield it with foil.
8. Cool completely on a rack, at least 2-3 hours, so the filling fully sets to a sliceable cream. Serve at room temperature or lightly chilled.

</details>

<sub>📷 Sarah Stierch, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-hoosier-sugar-cream-pie/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Atlantic_Beach_Pie_finished.jpg/480px-Atlantic_Beach_Pie_finished.jpg" alt="Photo of Atlantic Beach Pie">

#### 🥧 [Atlantic Beach Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-atlantic-beach-pie/)
American (Southern / North Carolina coastal) · snack · easy  
**$1.15/serving** · 420 kcal · P7 · C42 · F26 · ⏱ 320 min · 🍽 8  

The cult-favorite North Carolina coastal pie from Bill Smith of Crook's Corner: a buttery, salty saltine-cracker crust filled with a tart lemon-lime sweetened-condensed-milk custard set in the oven, then chilled and topped with lightly sweetened whipped cream and a shower of flaky sea salt. It tastes like key lime pie's sharper, saltier cousin — the salty-sweet contrast is the whole point.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Saltine crackers** — about 6 oz / 1.5 sleeves saltine crackers (roughly 60 crackers)
- **Sugar** — 3 tbsp granulated sugar
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, softened
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — one 14 oz can sweetened condensed milk
- **Eggs** — 4 large egg yolks (save the whites for another use)
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1/4 cup fresh lemon juice (about 2 lemons)
- **Bottled lime juice** — 1/4 cup fresh lime juice (about 3-4 limes), for the classic lemon-lime blend
- **Heavy cream** — 1 cup cold heavy whipping cream, for the topping
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for the whipped cream
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp vanilla extract, for the whipped cream
- **Flaky sea salt (Maldon-style)** — 1/2 tsp flaky sea salt, for finishing

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Crush the saltines into coarse crumbs with some pea-sized pieces remaining — pulse in a food processor or smash in a zip-top bag. You want texture, not a fine powder.
2. Stir the sugar into the cracker crumbs, then add the softened butter. Knead and mash everything together with your hands until the mixture holds together when squeezed, like wet sand. Press it firmly and evenly up the sides and across the bottom of a 9-inch pie plate. Chill the crust in the fridge for 15 minutes to firm up.
3. Bake the crust for 18 minutes, until it is fragrant and lightly golden. Set it aside to cool slightly while you make the filling. Leave the oven on.
4. Make the custard: whisk the egg yolks until smooth, then whisk in the sweetened condensed milk until fully blended. Whisk in the lemon juice and lime juice. The acid will instantly thicken the mixture to a pourable custard — this is the chemistry that sets the pie.
5. Pour the filling into the warm crust and smooth the top. Bake for 16 minutes, until the custard is set around the edges but still has a slight jiggle in the center. Do not let it brown or crack.
6. Cool the pie to room temperature, then refrigerate until thoroughly cold and set, at least 4 hours or overnight. A fully chilled pie slices cleanly.
7. Just before serving, whip the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar and vanilla to soft, billowy peaks. Spread or pipe it over the chilled pie.
8. Finish with a generous sprinkle of flaky sea salt over the cream. Slice with a warm, dry knife and serve cold.

</details>

<sub>📷 Joy, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-atlantic-beach-pie/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Kentucky_Chocolate_walnut_pie.JPG/480px-Kentucky_Chocolate_walnut_pie.JPG" alt="Photo of Classic Kentucky Derby Pie">

#### 🥧 [Classic Kentucky Derby Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-derby-pie/)
American (Southern) · snack · medium  
**$1.72/serving** · 517 kcal · P6 · C48 · F33 · ⏱ 135 min · 🍽 8  

The real deal: a flaky single-crust pie filled with a gooey, chess-pie-style custard of eggs, brown sugar, melted butter and a whisper of flour, spiked with bourbon and vanilla, then packed with toasted English walnuts and semi-sweet chocolate chips. Baked until the top crackles and the center is just set with a soft, fudgy middle. Serve barely warm with unsweetened whipped cream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/4 cups all-purpose flour (for the crust)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) cold unsalted butter, cubed (for the crust)
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt (for the crust)
- **Sugar** — 1 tbsp granulated sugar (for the crust)
- **Water** — 3 tbsp ice water (for the crust)
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs
- **White sugar** — 1/2 cup granulated sugar
- **Brown sugar** — 1/2 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, melted and slightly cooled (for the filling)
- **All-purpose flour** — 1/4 cup all-purpose flour (for the filling)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp pure vanilla extract
- **Bourbon** — 2 tbsp (1 oz) bourbon
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt (for the filling)
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup English walnuts, toasted and roughly chopped
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 1 cup semi-sweet chocolate chips

**Steps**

1. Make the crust: Whisk the 1 1/4 cups flour, 1/2 tsp salt and 1 tbsp sugar. Cut in the cold cubed butter with your fingers or a pastry blender until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs with some pea-sized bits. Drizzle in the ice water a tablespoon at a time, tossing, until the dough just holds together when squeezed. Pat into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 30 minutes.
2. Roll the chilled dough on a floured surface into a 12-inch round. Fit it into a 9-inch pie plate, trim the overhang to about 1 inch, tuck it under, and crimp the edge. Chill the shell in the freezer for 15 minutes while you make the filling, and set a rack in the lower third of the oven. Preheat to 350F (175C).
3. Toast the walnuts on a dry baking sheet for 6 to 8 minutes until fragrant, then chop roughly. Leave the oven on at 350F.
4. Make the filling: In a bowl, whisk the 2 eggs until light. Whisk in the granulated sugar, brown sugar, 1/4 cup flour and 1/4 tsp salt until smooth. Whisk in the melted butter, vanilla and bourbon until glossy and fully combined.
5. Scatter the toasted walnuts and chocolate chips evenly over the bottom of the chilled crust. Pour the filling over the top; it will settle around the nuts and chips and float some to the surface.
6. Bake at 350F for 45 to 55 minutes, until the top is golden and crackly and the center is set with just a slight jiggle. If the crust edge browns too fast, shield it with foil after 30 minutes.
7. Cool the pie on a rack for at least 1 hour so the gooey center firms up; it slices best barely warm or at room temperature.
8. Serve in wedges with a dollop of lightly sweetened or unsweetened whipped cream. Store leftovers loosely covered at room temperature for up to 2 days.

</details>

<sub>📷 Markmark28, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-derby-pie/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/Bundt_cake_%2812546036764%29.jpg/480px-Bundt_cake_%2812546036764%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Kentucky Butter Cake">

#### 🧈 [Kentucky Butter Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kentucky-butter-cake/)
American (Southern) · snack · easy  
**$0.86/serving** · 542 kcal · P5 · C76 · F24 · ⏱ 135 min · 🍽 12  

The real-deal Kentucky butter cake: a tender, dense, buttermilk pound-style Bundt cake that's poked all over while still warm and drenched in a warm vanilla butter syrup. The syrup soaks in and crystallizes into a delicate sugary crust on the outside while keeping the inside almost custardy-moist. Pure, simple, butter-forward — no frosting needed.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 3 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar (for the cake)
- **Salt** — 1 tsp salt
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/2 tsp baking soda
- **Buttermilk** — 1 cup buttermilk, room temperature
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened — for the cake
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract (for the cake)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter — for the butter syrup
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (for the butter syrup)
- **Water** — 3 tbsp water (for the butter syrup)
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract (for the butter syrup)

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 325F (165C). Generously grease and flour a 10- to 12-cup Bundt or tube pan, getting into every crevice — this cake is dense and clings, so thorough greasing is essential for a clean release.
2. Whisk together the flour, 2 cups sugar, salt, baking powder, and baking soda in a large bowl. Add the softened butter (1 cup), buttermilk, eggs, and 2 tsp vanilla.
3. Beat with an electric mixer on low until the dry ingredients are moistened, then increase to medium and beat for 3 full minutes, scraping the bowl once or twice. The batter will be thick and creamy.
4. Scrape the batter into the prepared pan and smooth the top. Bake for 55 to 65 minutes, until a toothpick inserted into the center comes out clean and the top springs back when pressed.
5. Near the end of baking, make the butter syrup: combine the 1/2 cup butter, 1 cup sugar, and water in a small saucepan over medium-low heat. Stir until the butter melts and the sugar dissolves and the mixture is steaming — do NOT let it boil, or the syrup will turn grainy. Remove from heat and stir in 2 tsp vanilla.
6. Leave the baked cake in its pan. Use a long skewer or thin knife to poke deep holes all over the cake, right down to the bottom of the pan.
7. Slowly pour the warm butter syrup over the warm cake, letting it soak into the holes a little at a time so it fully absorbs. Reserve a few tablespoons if you like a glazed top.
8. Let the cake cool completely in the pan, at least 1 hour — this lets the syrup set into a sugary crust and firms the crumb. Run a thin knife around the edges and center tube, then invert onto a serving plate. Dust with powdered sugar if desired and serve.

</details>

<sub>📷 James Petts, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-kentucky-butter-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Mississippi_mud_pie_%287963392610%29.jpg/480px-Mississippi_mud_pie_%287963392610%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Mississippi Mud Pie">

#### 🥧 [Mississippi Mud Pie](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-mississippi-mud-pie/)
American (Southern) · snack · medium  
**$1.45/serving** · 441 kcal · P5 · C45 · F27 · ⏱ 300 min · 🍽 10  

The real-deal Southern classic: a crunchy Oreo crumb crust holding a dense, fudgy baked chocolate filling — gooey and almost-brownie in the center, with crackly edges — finished with billows of lightly sweetened whipped cream and a shower of chocolate shavings. Rich, deeply chocolatey, and worth every fork.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Oreo cookies** — 24 Oreo cookies (about 1 standard sleeve), filling and all, for the crust
- **Butter** — 1 cup + 2 tbsp unsalted butter (5 tbsp melted for the crust, 13 tbsp for the filling)
- **Dark chocolate** — 6 oz semisweet or bittersweet chocolate, chopped, for the filling
- **Semi-sweet chocolate chips** — 1/4 cup semi-sweet chocolate chips, folded into the filling for pockets of melt
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar
- **Eggs** — 4 large eggs, room temperature
- **Cocoa powder** — 1/4 cup unsweetened cocoa powder
- **All-purpose flour** — 1/2 cup all-purpose flour
- **Instant coffee** — 1 tsp instant coffee or espresso powder, to deepen the chocolate
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Heavy cream** — 1 1/4 cups cold heavy cream, for the whipped topping
- **Powdered sugar** — 3 tbsp powdered sugar, for the whipped cream
- **Pecans** — 2 oz pecans, toasted and chopped, for garnish (optional) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C). Pulse the Oreos (cream filling included) in a food processor to fine crumbs, or crush them in a zip bag with a rolling pin. Stir in 5 tbsp of the melted butter until the crumbs look like wet sand. Press firmly and evenly up the sides and across the bottom of a 9-inch pie dish or springform pan. Bake 10 minutes, then set aside to cool while you make the filling.
2. Make the fudge base: combine the chopped 6 oz chocolate and the remaining 13 tbsp butter in a heatproof bowl set over a saucepan of barely simmering water (or microwave in 30-second bursts). Stir until fully melted and glossy, then remove from the heat and stir in the instant coffee and salt. Let it cool for about 5 minutes so it won't cook the eggs.
3. In a separate large bowl, whisk the granulated sugar and eggs together vigorously for a full minute, until pale, thick, and slightly foamy. This is what gives the pie its signature crackly top.
4. Pour the warm (not hot) melted chocolate mixture into the egg mixture and whisk until smooth. Whisk in the vanilla.
5. Sift the cocoa powder and flour over the batter and fold in gently with a spatula just until no dry streaks remain — don't overmix. Fold in the 1/4 cup chocolate chips.
6. Pour the filling into the cooled crust and smooth the top. Bake at 350F for 38 to 45 minutes, until the edges are set and puffed but the center still has a slight jiggle and a toothpick comes out with moist, fudgy crumbs (not wet batter). The center will firm up as it cools.
7. Cool the pie completely at room temperature, about 1 hour, then chill in the refrigerator for at least 3 hours (or overnight) so the filling sets to a dense, sliceable fudge.
8. Just before serving, whip the cold heavy cream with the powdered sugar to soft-to-medium peaks. Spread or pipe it over the chilled pie, then shower with shaved chocolate and the toasted pecans if using. Slice with a hot, clean knife for tidy wedges.

</details>

<sub>📷 cyclonebill, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-mississippi-mud-pie/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Sheet_cake_with_chopped_nuts.jpg/480px-Sheet_cake_with_chopped_nuts.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Texas Sheet Cake">

#### 🍫 [Classic Texas Sheet Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-texas-sheet-cake/)
American (Texan) · snack · easy  
**$0.72/serving** · 218 kcal · P2 · C27 · F12 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 24  

The real deal: a thin, fudgy chocolate buttermilk sheet cake topped with a warm pecan cocoa icing that's poured over the cake while both are still hot, so it sets into a glossy, almost-fudge shell. A potluck legend from the Texas Hill Country — one big pan that feeds a crowd.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Sugar** — 2 cups granulated sugar
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Baking soda** — 1 tsp baking soda
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter — for the cake batter
- **Water** — 1 cup water
- **Cocoa powder** — 1/4 cup unsweetened cocoa powder — for the cake batter
- **Buttermilk** — 1/2 cup buttermilk
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, lightly beaten
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract — for the cake batter
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter — for the icing
- **Cocoa powder** — 1/4 cup unsweetened cocoa powder — for the icing
- **Milk** — 6 tbsp whole milk
- **Powdered sugar** — 4 cups powdered sugar (one 1-lb box)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract — for the icing
- **Pecans** — 1 cup chopped pecans, toasted

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350°F. Grease an 18x13-inch rimmed sheet pan (half-sheet / jelly-roll pan) and set aside. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, granulated sugar, salt, and baking soda.
2. Make the cooked chocolate base: in a saucepan, combine 1 cup butter, 1 cup water, and 1/4 cup cocoa. Bring to a rolling boil over medium heat, stirring, then immediately remove from the heat.
3. Pour the hot cocoa-butter mixture over the dry ingredients and stir until just combined and smooth — it will be a thin, glossy batter.
4. Whisk in the buttermilk, beaten eggs, and 1 tsp vanilla until fully incorporated. Do not overmix; stop as soon as the batter is uniform.
5. Pour the batter into the prepared sheet pan and spread evenly into the corners. Bake for 18 to 22 minutes, until the top springs back and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean. Start the icing during the last 5 minutes of baking.
6. Make the icing: in a saucepan, melt 1/2 cup butter with 1/4 cup cocoa and the 6 tbsp milk, stirring until smooth and just bubbling at the edges. Remove from the heat. Whisk in the powdered sugar one cup at a time until smooth, then stir in 1 tsp vanilla and the toasted pecans.
7. As soon as the cake comes out of the oven, pour the warm icing over the hot cake and gently spread to the edges with an offset spatula. Pouring while both are hot lets the icing soak into the surface and set with a fudgy sheen.
8. Let the cake cool completely, at least 1 hour, so the icing sets before cutting into 24 squares.

</details>

<sub>📷 Jeff Sandquist, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-texas-sheet-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/Empanada_-_Stu_Spivack.jpg/480px-Empanada_-_Stu_Spivack.jpg" alt="Photo of Empanada Dough">

#### 🥟 [Empanada Dough](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-empanada-dough/)
Argentine · meal-prep · medium  
**$0.19/serving** · 170 kcal · P4 · C24 · F6 · ⏱ 50 min · 🍽 12  

A tender, flaky homemade masa para empanadas built the traditional way: cold butter cut into flour, bound with an egg and a splash of vinegared ice water. It rolls out smooth, crimps into a clean repulgue, and bakes up golden and sturdy enough to hold any filling without leaking. Makes roughly 12 discs.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, about 3 cups / 375g
- **Butter** — cold unsalted butter, cut into cubes (~6 tbsp); traditional masa uses lard
- **Eggs** — 1 egg
- **Salt** — 1.5 tsp
- **Water** — about 1/2 cup ice-cold water, added gradually
- **Vinegar** — 1 tsp white vinegar for tenderness _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour and salt until evenly combined.
2. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the flour with your fingertips (or cut with a pastry blender) until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs with some pea-sized bits remaining.
3. In a small bowl, beat the egg with the vinegar and about 1/2 cup ice-cold water.
4. Pour the wet mixture into the flour and stir, then bring it together by hand, adding a splash more cold water only if the dough won't cohere. Do not overwork it.
5. Turn out onto a clean surface and knead just 5-6 times into a smooth ball; the dough should be soft but not sticky.
6. Wrap tightly in plastic and rest in the fridge for at least 30 minutes (up to overnight) to relax the gluten and firm the butter.
7. Roll the chilled dough out on a lightly floured surface to about 2-3mm thick and cut into 5-inch (13cm) rounds using a bowl or cutter; gather and re-roll the scraps once.
8. Fill, fold, and seal each disc with a fork press or twisted repulgue crimp. Bake filled empanadas at 400F (200C) for 20-25 minutes until deeply golden, brushing with egg wash for shine.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: stu_spivack — CC BY-SA 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-empanada-dough/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/No-knead_sourdough_bread_in_Griswold_Dutch_Oven.jpg/480px-No-knead_sourdough_bread_in_Griswold_Dutch_Oven.jpg" alt="Photo of No-Knead Dutch Oven Bread">

#### 🍞 [No-Knead Dutch Oven Bread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-no-knead-dutch-oven-bread/)
Artisan / European · snack · easy  
**$0.09/serving** · 170 kcal · P6 · C35 · F1 · ⏱ 1005 min · 🍽 10  

A bakery-style artisan loaf with a crackly, blistered crust and an open, chewy crumb — made with just flour, water, yeast, and salt. No kneading, no machine: a slow overnight ferment does the work, and a preheated Dutch oven traps steam for that crust.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 430g bread flour (or all-purpose); ~3 1/3 cups
- **Water** — 345g lukewarm water (~1 1/2 cups), about 70-75% hydration
- **Instant yeast** — 1/2 tsp instant yeast; uses only a tiny pinch for the long cold ferment
- **Salt** — 1 1/2 tsp fine salt (~9g)
- **Cornmeal (polenta)** — for dusting the parchment so the loaf releases cleanly _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, instant yeast, and salt. Add the lukewarm water and stir with a spatula or your hand until no dry flour remains — it will be a shaggy, sticky, wet dough. Do not knead.
2. Cover the bowl tightly with plastic wrap or a lid and leave at room temperature for 12 to 18 hours, until the surface is bubbly and the dough has roughly doubled and looks loose and webbed. A longer rise builds more flavor.
3. Generously flour a work surface and scrape the dough out onto it. With floured hands, fold the dough over onto itself 3 or 4 times to shape it into a loose round, then flip it seam-side down and tuck the edges under to build surface tension.
4. Place the shaped dough seam-side down on a sheet of parchment dusted with cornmeal or flour. Cover loosely with a towel and let rise 1 to 2 hours, until puffy and slowly springing back when poked.
5. About 30 minutes before baking, set a heavy Dutch oven (with its lid) on the middle rack and preheat the oven to 475F (245C) so the pot gets blazing hot.
6. Carefully remove the hot Dutch oven. Score the top of the loaf with a sharp blade or knife (one deep slash or a cross), then lift the dough by the parchment and lower it, parchment and all, into the pot.
7. Cover with the lid and bake 30 minutes. The trapped steam sets a thin, glossy crust and lets the loaf spring up.
8. Remove the lid and bake another 12 to 18 minutes, until the crust is deep golden brown and the loaf sounds hollow when tapped (internal temp about 205-210F).
9. Lift the bread out by the parchment and cool on a wire rack for at least 1 hour before slicing — cutting too soon makes the crumb gummy.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-no-knead-dutch-oven-bread/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Sachertorte_in_Vienna.jpg/480px-Sachertorte_in_Vienna.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Viennese Sachertorte">

#### 🍫 [Classic Viennese Sachertorte](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sachertorte/)
Austrian · snack · hard  
**$1.59/serving** · 257 kcal · P4 · C32 · F13 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 12  

The genuine Viennese chocolate cake: a dense yet tender butter-and-chocolate sponge leavened only by whipped egg whites, split and brushed with warmed apricot jam, then enrobed in a glossy chocolate glaze. Traditionally served with a cloud of unsweetened whipped cream. This is the real thing — no baking powder shortcuts, no buttercream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Dark chocolate** — 5.3 oz (150 g) good-quality dark couverture chocolate, ~55% cacao, finely chopped — for the batter
- **Butter** — 11 tbsp (150 g) unsalted butter, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — scant 3/4 cup (110 g) powdered sugar, sifted — creamed with the butter
- **Eggs** — 6 large eggs, separated, at room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract (or seeds of 1/2 vanilla pod)
- **Sugar** — scant 2/3 cup (120 g) granulated sugar — for whipping the egg whites
- **All-purpose flour** — 1 cup (130 g) all-purpose flour, sifted
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Apricot jam** — about 3/4 cup + 1 tbsp (250 g) apricot jam, for the filling and glaze layer
- **Dark chocolate** — 7 oz (200 g) dark chocolate, chopped — for the chocolate glaze
- **Sugar** — 2/3 cup (135 g) granulated sugar — for the glaze sugar syrup
- **Water** — 1/2 cup (125 ml) water — for the glaze sugar syrup
- **Heavy cream** — 1 cup (240 ml) cold heavy cream, lightly whipped unsweetened, to serve _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 340F (170C). Butter a 9-inch (23 cm) springform pan, line the base with parchment, and lightly flour the sides. Melt the 150 g batter chocolate gently over a bain-marie or in short microwave bursts, then set aside to cool until just barely warm.
2. Cream the softened butter with the sifted powdered sugar until pale and fluffy, 3-4 minutes. Beat in the egg yolks one at a time, then the vanilla. Stir in the cooled melted chocolate until smooth and uniform.
3. In a clean, grease-free bowl, whip the egg whites with a pinch of salt to soft peaks, then rain in the 120 g granulated sugar a spoonful at a time, whipping to a glossy, firm-but-not-dry meringue. This meringue is the only leavening, so do not deflate it.
4. Fold about a third of the meringue into the chocolate base to lighten it. Sift the flour over the mixture and fold in alternately with the remaining meringue, working gently with a spatula just until no streaks remain.
5. Scrape the batter into the pan, level the top, and bake 50-60 minutes until a skewer comes out with a few moist crumbs and the center springs back lightly. The top will dome and may crack — that is correct. Cool 10 minutes, then unmould and cool completely on a rack, top-side down so it flattens.
6. Slice the cooled cake horizontally into two even layers. Warm the apricot jam with a splash of water until fluid, then push it through a sieve. Brush the cut layer generously, sandwich the cake back together, then brush the entire top and sides with a thin, even coat of the warm jam. Let it set 15-20 minutes so the glaze has a smooth base to grip.
7. Make the glaze: bring the 135 g sugar and water to a boil and simmer 5 minutes to a light syrup (about 220F/104C). Off the heat, stir in the 200 g chopped chocolate until completely melted and glossy, then let it cool, stirring, until it thickly coats the back of a spoon but still flows.
8. Set the cake on a rack over a tray and pour the glaze over the center in one motion, using a few decisive passes of an offset spatula to sheet it over the top and down the sides. Do not over-spread — the fewer strokes, the glassier the finish. Leave to set at room temperature (do not refrigerate, or the glaze will dull).
9. Let the glaze firm up for at least a few hours. Serve in slices at room temperature with a quenelle of softly whipped, unsweetened cream alongside.

</details>

<sub>📷 Rizka, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sachertorte/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/84/Linzer_cookies_%285234191379%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Linzer Cookies">

#### 🍪 [Linzer Cookies](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-linzer-cookies/)
Austrian · snack · medium  
**$0.68/serving** · 311 kcal · P3 · C41 · F15 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 12  

Buttery almond shortbread sandwich cookies scented with cinnamon and lemon zest, filled with raspberry jam and finished with a snowfall of powdered sugar around a peekaboo window. A classic Austrian holiday cookie that tastes like a tiny Linzer torte.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted, softened
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated
- **Almond flour** — 1 cup finely ground almond flour
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, room temperature
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon
- **Cinnamon** — 1/2 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Jam** — about 2/3 cup seedless raspberry jam for filling
- **Powdered sugar** — for dusting the tops

**Steps**

1. Cream the softened butter and granulated sugar with a mixer on medium until pale and fluffy, 3 to 4 minutes. Beat in the egg, vanilla, and lemon zest until smooth.
2. Whisk the all-purpose flour, almond flour, cinnamon, and salt together in a bowl, then add to the butter mixture in two additions, mixing on low just until a soft dough comes together. Do not overwork it.
3. Divide the dough in half, flatten each into a disk, wrap in plastic, and chill at least 1 hour (or up to 2 days) until firm enough to roll.
4. Roll one disk between two sheets of parchment to about 1/8 inch thick. Cut out rounds with a 2-inch fluted or round cutter; these are the bottoms. Re-roll scraps as needed.
5. Roll and cut the second disk the same way, then use a small cutter to stamp a window out of the center of each of these tops. Transfer all cookies to parchment-lined sheets and chill 15 minutes so they hold their shape.
6. Bake at 350F (175C) for 10 to 12 minutes, until the edges are just barely golden. The cut-out tops will bake a minute or two faster, so watch them. Cool completely on the pans.
7. Dust only the window tops generously with powdered sugar while they sit on a rack (dusting first keeps sugar off the jam).
8. Spread about 1 teaspoon raspberry jam over each solid bottom cookie, then gently press a sugared top onto each so the jam shows through the window.
9. Let the assembled cookies set for 30 minutes so the jam firms up; they keep in an airtight tin for up to 5 days and the flavor deepens overnight.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Simon S — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-linzer-cookies/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9b/G%C3%A2teau_basque.jpg" alt="Photo of Gâteau Basque">

#### 🍰 [Gâteau Basque](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-g-teau-basque/)
Basque (French) · snack · medium  
**$0.79/serving** · 378 kcal · P5 · C45 · F19 · ⏱ 200 min · 🍽 10  

The classic cake of the French Basque country: two layers of rich, almost cookie-like buttery short pastry enclosing a thick vanilla-and-rum pastry cream, baked until deeply golden and traditionally scored with a crosshatch or Basque cross on top. Crisp at the edges, tender within, and not too sweet — the version a Basque grandmother would actually make.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour (for the pastry)
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened, for the pastry
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar for the pastry
- **Eggs** — 2 whole eggs for the pastry, plus 1 yolk reserved separately for the egg wash
- **Baking powder** — 1 1/2 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Almond flour** — 1/4 cup almond flour (traditional in many Basque versions)
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla extract (split between pastry and cream)
- **Rum** — 1 oz (2 tbsp) dark rum for the pastry cream
- **Whole milk** — 2 cups whole milk for the pastry cream
- **Cornstarch** — 4 tbsp cornstarch to set the pastry cream

**Steps**

1. Make the pastry cream first so it can chill: in a saucepan, warm the whole milk with 1/2 cup of the sugar (8 tbsp from the total) until steaming but not boiling. In a bowl, whisk the 2 whole eggs' worth of yolks-and-a-little-effort — here whisk 1 reserved yolk plus 1 whole egg with the cornstarch and a pinch of the sugar until pale. Temper by whisking in the hot milk a little at a time, then return everything to the pan.
2. Cook the cream over medium heat, whisking constantly, until it thickens and boils for a full 60 seconds (this cooks out the cornstarch). Off heat, whisk in 1 tsp vanilla and the dark rum. Scrape into a bowl, press plastic wrap directly onto the surface, and refrigerate until cold and set, at least 2 hours.
3. Make the pastry: cream the softened butter with the remaining sugar until light, 3-4 minutes. Beat in 1 whole egg, the lemon zest, and 1 tsp vanilla. In a separate bowl whisk together the flour, almond flour, baking powder, and salt, then add to the butter mixture and mix just until a soft, sticky dough forms. Divide into two discs (one slightly larger), wrap, and chill at least 1 hour.
4. Heat the oven to 350°F (175°C). Butter a 9-inch springform or cake pan. Roll the larger dough disc between sheets of parchment into a circle that lines the bottom and comes about 1 inch up the sides; press it into the pan, patching as needed — this dough is forgiving.
5. Whisk the chilled pastry cream smooth, then spread it evenly over the base, leaving a 1/2-inch border of bare pastry around the edge so the two layers can seal.
6. Roll the second disc into a 9-inch circle for the lid. Lay it over the cream, then pinch the top and bottom edges of pastry together to seal. Brush the top with the reserved egg yolk beaten with a teaspoon of water.
7. Score the traditional pattern: drag a fork in a crosshatch across the top, or trace a Basque cross (lauburu). Prick a few small steam vents through the lid with a skewer.
8. Bake 40-50 minutes, until deeply golden brown all over (a pale gâteau basque is underbaked). If the top colors too fast, tent loosely with foil for the last 10 minutes.
9. Cool completely in the pan, then at least 4 hours or overnight before slicing — the cake firms up and the flavors meld. Serve at room temperature, dusted with nothing or just a whisper of powdered sugar.

</details>

<sub>📷 Thomon, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-g-teau-basque/)</sub>
