<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Culture..._a_bath_bun_and_a_pot_of_tea%2C_Bath%2C_United_Kingdom_%289605677635%29.jpg/480px-Culture..._a_bath_bun_and_a_pot_of_tea%2C_Bath%2C_United_Kingdom_%289605677635%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Bath Buns">

#### 🍞 [Bath Buns](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-bath-buns/)
British · snack · medium  
**$0.18/serving** · 66 kcal · P3 · C11 · F2 · ⏱ 155 min · 🍽 10  

A traditional West Country sweet bun: a rich, brioche-like enriched dough studded with currants and lemon zest, topped with crunchy sugar nibs and finished with a sticky milk-and-sugar glaze. Lightly scented with caraway in the old Bath style.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — strong white bread flour, ~450g
- **Instant yeast** — 7g fast-action dried yeast
- **Whole milk** — ~150ml warm whole milk
- **Butter** — 85g softened unsalted butter
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **White sugar** — 55g caster sugar in the dough
- **Salt** — 1 tsp fine salt
- **Raisins** — 100g currants (or substitute mixed candied peel for half)
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon
- **Caraway seeds** — 1 tsp, traditional Bath scenting (optional) _(optional)_
- **Turbinado sugar** — crushed sugar nibs / nib sugar for the top, ~40g
- **Milk** — 1 tbsp for the egg wash and glaze

**Steps**

1. Warm the whole milk until just lukewarm, stir in the yeast and a pinch of the caster sugar, and let it sit for 5-10 minutes until frothy. Meanwhile, beat 1 whole egg plus 1 yolk together (reserve the spare white).
2. In a large bowl, whisk the bread flour, remaining caster sugar, and salt. Make a well, pour in the frothy milk and beaten egg, and bring together into a shaggy dough, then knead 8-10 minutes until smooth and elastic (it will be soft and slightly sticky).
3. Add the softened butter a little at a time, kneading it fully into the dough between additions until the dough is glossy and supple, about 5 more minutes. Cover and prove in a warm place for 1 to 1.5 hours until doubled.
4. Knock back the dough and knead in the currants, lemon zest, and caraway seeds until evenly distributed. Divide into 10 equal pieces (about 80g each) and shape each into a tight, rounded bun.
5. Space the buns well apart on lined baking trays, cover loosely, and prove again for 30-45 minutes until puffy and almost doubled. Heat the oven to 200C / 400F.
6. Beat the reserved egg white with 1 tbsp milk and brush over the risen buns, then scatter generously with the crushed sugar nibs. Bake for 15-18 minutes until deep golden and hollow-sounding when tapped underneath.
7. While the buns bake, dissolve 2 tbsp caster sugar in 1 tbsp milk and warm briefly to make a glaze. Brush the hot buns with the sweet glaze as they come out of the oven for the traditional sticky shine, then cool on a wire rack.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Richard Allaway from France — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-bath-buns/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/58/Blackberry_Pear_Crumble_%284747800717%29.jpg/480px-Blackberry_Pear_Crumble_%284747800717%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Blackberry Crumble">

#### 🫐 [Blackberry Crumble](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-blackberry-crumble/)
British · snack · easy  
**$2.87/serving** · 337 kcal · P3 · C48 · F15 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 6  

Juicy blackberries baked under a golden, buttery oat-and-flour crumble. The fruit cooks down into a glossy, jammy filling brightened with lemon, while the topping turns crisp and caramelized — proper British comfort pudding, best with a scoop of ice cream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Blackberries** — about 6 cups fresh blackberries
- **Frozen berries** — optional: top up with frozen blackberries if short on fresh _(optional)_
- **Sugar** — split: ~1/3 cup for fruit, rest for topping
- **Cornstarch** — 2 tbsp to thicken the juices
- **Lemon** — juice of half a lemon
- **Lemon zest** — zest of half a lemon
- **All-purpose flour** — about 1 cup all-purpose flour for topping
- **Instant oat packets** — about 3/4 cup oats for the crumble
- **Brown sugar** — about 1/2 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Butter** — 8 tbsp / 1 stick cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Cinnamon** — 1/2 tsp
- **Salt** — pinch for topping and fruit

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 375 F (190 C) and lightly butter a 9-inch baking dish. Pull the cubed butter from the fridge so it stays cold until the last moment.
2. Make the filling: in a bowl, gently toss the blackberries with about 1/3 cup of the white sugar, the cornstarch, lemon juice, and lemon zest until the berries are evenly coated. Tip into the baking dish and spread level.
3. Make the crumble: in a separate bowl, stir together the flour, oats, brown sugar, remaining white sugar, cinnamon, and a pinch of salt.
4. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the dry mix with your fingertips (or cut with a fork) until it clumps into a coarse, pebbly rubble with pea-sized pieces — do not overwork it into a paste.
5. Scatter the crumble evenly over the berries in a thick, craggy layer, leaving a few gaps so the fruit can bubble up through the top.
6. Place the dish on a rimmed baking sheet to catch drips and bake for 40 to 45 minutes, until the topping is deep golden and the blackberry juices are bubbling thickly around the edges.
7. Let it rest at least 15 to 20 minutes so the filling sets up and thickens before serving — it will be molten straight from the oven.
8. Serve warm, ideally with vanilla ice cream or cold cream poured over the top.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Neil Conway from Oakland, USA — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-blackberry-crumble/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/40/Cheese_scones.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic British Cheese Scones">

#### 🧀 [Classic British Cheese Scones](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cheese-scones/)
British · snack · easy  
**$0.61/serving** · 198 kcal · P5 · C28 · F7 · ⏱ 35 min · 🍽 8  

Tall, golden, properly cheesy scones with a sharp cheddar crust and a soft, fluffy crumb. The classic British teatime bake — rubbed-in cold butter, a generous hand with mature cheddar, and a hot oven for maximum rise. Split, butter while warm, and eat the same day.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups (about 280g) all-purpose flour
- **Baking powder** — 1 tbsp baking powder (use if your flour is plain/all-purpose, not self-raising)
- **Mustard powder** — 1/2 tsp English mustard powder, for savory depth
- **Cayenne pepper** — 1/4 tsp cayenne pepper _(optional)_
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp fine salt
- **Butter** — 4 tbsp (1/4 cup / 55g) cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Sharp cheddar block** — 5 oz (about 140g) mature/sharp cheddar, grated — keep a small handful back for the tops
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg
- **Milk** — about 1/2 cup whole milk, plus a little extra for glazing

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 425F (220C) and line a baking sheet with parchment. A hot oven is essential for a good rise.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, baking powder, mustard powder, cayenne, and salt. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the flour with your fingertips until the mixture looks like coarse breadcrumbs — work quickly so the butter stays cold.
3. Stir in most of the grated cheddar (reserve a small handful for the tops), keeping the cheese coated in flour so it distributes evenly.
4. Crack the egg into a measuring jug and beat lightly, then add enough milk to reach about 3/4 cup total liquid. Reserve a tablespoon of this for glazing. Pour the rest into the flour and bring it together with a knife, then your hands, into a soft, slightly shaggy dough — do not overwork it.
5. Tip onto a lightly floured surface and gently pat (don't roll hard) to about 1 inch / 2.5 cm thick. Cut out rounds with a 2.5-inch cutter pressed straight down without twisting, so the scones rise level. Gather scraps, pat out again, and cut until you have 8.
6. Place the scones close together on the sheet (touching helps them rise tall). Brush the tops with the reserved egg-milk glaze and scatter over the reserved cheddar.
7. Bake for 12 to 15 minutes, until well risen and deep golden with crisp cheesy edges. They should sound hollow when tapped underneath.
8. Cool on a wire rack for a few minutes. Serve warm, split and spread with butter. Best eaten the day they're made.

</details>

<sub>📷 jemsweb, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-cheese-scones/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9c/Bowl_of_digestive_biscuits.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic British Digestive Biscuits">

#### 🍪 [Classic British Digestive Biscuits](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-digestive-biscuits/)
British · snack · easy  
**$0.10/serving** · 81 kcal · P1 · C10 · F4 · ⏱ 55 min · 🍽 24  

The genuine McVitie's-style digestive: a sturdy, lightly sweet semi-wholemeal biscuit with a nutty, malty crumb and a clean snap. Coarse wholemeal flour and fine oatmeal give it that signature sandy texture, while a little brown sugar and just enough butter keep it tender but firm enough to dunk in tea without collapsing. Pricked, baked until deep gold, and crisp all the way through.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups wholemeal (whole-wheat) flour — for an authentic semi-wholemeal biscuit; sub half with white flour for a lighter crumb
- **Rolled oats** — 3/4 cup rolled oats, ground fine in a blender to a coarse oatmeal (medium oatmeal is traditional)
- **Brown sugar** — 1/4 cup packed light brown sugar
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Baking soda** — 1/4 tsp baking soda
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp fine salt
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) cold unsalted butter, cut into cubes
- **Milk** — 3 tablespoons whole milk (about 3/16 cup), plus a little more if needed

**Steps**

1. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment. Blitz the rolled oats in a blender or food processor until they reach a coarse, sandy oatmeal — a few small flecks left is exactly right, not a fine powder.
2. In a large bowl whisk together the wholemeal flour, ground oats, brown sugar, baking powder, baking soda, and salt, breaking up any lumps of sugar with your fingers.
3. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the dry mix with your fingertips (or pulse in the processor) until the mixture looks like damp, coarse breadcrumbs with no butter pieces larger than a pea.
4. Drizzle in the milk a tablespoon at a time, stirring, then bring the dough together with your hands. It should just clump into a firm, slightly stiff dough — add a teaspoon more milk only if it won't hold. Do not overwork it. Press into a disc, wrap, and chill 20 minutes.
5. Roll the dough out on a lightly floured surface to a scant 1/4 inch (5-6 mm) thick. Cut rounds with a 2.5-inch (6-7 cm) cutter, re-rolling scraps once. Place on the trays and prick each biscuit several times all over with a fork — this stops them doming and gives the classic look.
6. Bake for 15-18 minutes, rotating the trays halfway, until the biscuits are an even deep golden brown at the edges and firm in the center. They will still feel slightly soft when hot but crisp as they cool.
7. Cool on the tray for 5 minutes to set, then transfer to a wire rack to cool completely — they reach their final hard, snappy texture only once fully cold. Store in an airtight tin for up to 2 weeks.

</details>

<sub>📷 LearningLark, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-digestive-biscuits/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Scones_cream_jam.jpg/480px-Scones_cream_jam.jpg" alt="Photo of Cream Scones">

#### 🧈 [Cream Scones](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cream-scones/)
British · breakfast · easy  
**$0.24/serving** · 152 kcal · P3 · C33 · F0 · ⏱ 33 min · 🍽 8  

Tender, flaky scones made the easy way — no cold butter to cut in. Heavy cream does double duty as the fat and the liquid, giving a rich, downy crumb with a golden, sparkling-sugar top. Best warm with jam and clotted cream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, ~2 cups
- **Sugar** — granulated, ~1/3 cup in the dough
- **Baking powder** — 1 tablespoon
- **Salt** — 1/2 teaspoon
- **Heavy cream** — ~1 1/4 cups cold, plus a little for brushing
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 teaspoon
- **Raisins** — use dried currants if you can; ~2/3 cup _(optional)_
- **Turbinado sugar** — for sparkling tops _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 425F (220C) and line a baking sheet with parchment. Keep the cream in the fridge until the moment you need it — cold cream makes a flakier scone.
2. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, granulated sugar, baking powder, and salt until evenly combined. If using currants, toss them in now so they get coated with flour.
3. Stir the vanilla into the cold heavy cream, then pour it into the dry ingredients. Using a fork or flexible spatula, stir gently just until a shaggy, mostly-cohesive dough forms — a few dry patches are fine. Do not overmix.
4. Turn the dough and any loose crumbs onto a lightly floured surface. Gently knead 4 or 5 times, pressing stray bits in, just until it holds together. Pat into a round disk about 1 inch thick.
5. Cut the disk into 8 wedges with a sharp knife or bench scraper, pressing straight down without twisting so the edges stay clean and rise tall.
6. Arrange the wedges on the baking sheet a couple inches apart. Brush the tops with a little extra cream and sprinkle generously with turbinado sugar.
7. Bake for 15 to 18 minutes, until the tops are golden and the bottoms are firm and lightly browned. Rotate the pan halfway through for even color.
8. Cool on the pan for 5 minutes, then serve warm with jam, butter, or clotted cream.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Takeaway — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cream-scones/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Scones_and_tea.jpg/480px-Scones_and_tea.jpg" alt="Photo of English Scones">

#### 🧈 [English Scones](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-english-scones/)
British · snack · easy  
**$0.13/serving** · 72 kcal · P2 · C14 · F1 · ⏱ 28 min · 🍽 8  

Classic British tea-time scones — tall, fluffy, and lightly sweet with a golden glazed top and a soft, tender crumb that pulls apart in two. Built on cold butter rubbed into flour and just enough milk to bind, they bake in 12 minutes and beg to be split and slathered with jam and clotted cream.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 350g plain (all-purpose) flour
- **Baking powder** — 4 tsp baking powder for a tall rise
- **Butter** — 85g cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Sugar** — 3 tbsp caster sugar
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp fine salt
- **Whole milk** — 175ml whole milk, plus a splash to bring the dough together
- **Bottled lemon juice** — 1 tsp lemon juice to lightly sour the milk and boost lift _(optional)_
- **Eggs** — 1 beaten egg for the glaze

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 220C/425F and line a baking sheet with parchment. Stir the lemon juice into the milk and set aside for a few minutes to lightly sour, then warm the milk slightly (a few seconds in the microwave) — barely warm milk gives a better rise.
2. Tip the flour, baking powder, salt and sugar into a large bowl and whisk to combine. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the flour with your fingertips until the mixture looks like fine breadcrumbs, working quickly so the butter stays cold.
3. Make a well in the centre and pour in most of the milk. Use a cutlery knife to cut and stir the milk through until it forms a soft, slightly shaggy dough, adding the last splash of milk only if it looks dry. Do not overwork it — handle as little as possible.
4. Tip the dough onto a lightly floured surface and gently fold it over 2 or 3 times just to smooth it, then pat (do not roll hard) to an even 2.5cm / 1-inch thickness. Keeping it thick is what makes scones tall.
5. Dip a 5cm / 2-inch round cutter in flour and stamp out scones with a clean straight push down — do not twist, or the sides seal and they rise lopsided. Gather and gently re-pat the offcuts to cut the rest; expect 8 scones.
6. Place the scones close together on the sheet so they support each other as they rise. Brush the tops only with beaten egg, avoiding drips down the sides which would glue them to the tray.
7. Bake for 11-13 minutes until well risen and deep golden on top. Cool on a wire rack for a few minutes, then split and serve warm with jam and clotted cream (jam first or cream first is a matter of regional pride).

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: AlphaLemur — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-english-scones/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Hot_cross_buns_-_fig_and_pecan.jpg/480px-Hot_cross_buns_-_fig_and_pecan.jpg" alt="Photo of Hot Cross Buns">

#### 🥯 [Hot Cross Buns](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-hot-cross-buns/)
British · snack · medium  
**$0.46/serving** · 246 kcal · P6 · C40 · F7 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 12  

Hot Cross Buns

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — strong/bread flour, about 4 cups, plus extra for the cross paste
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (one packet)
- **Whole milk** — about 1 cup, warmed to lukewarm
- **Butter** — about 6 tbsp, softened
- **Sugar** — about 1/3 cup
- **Eggs** — 2 egg
- **Salt** — 1 tsp fine salt
- **Cinnamon** — 2 tsp ground
- **Nutmeg** — 1/2 tsp freshly grated
- **Allspice** — 1/4 tsp ground; mixed spice blend
- **Raisins** — about 1 cup; or currants/sultanas
- **Orange** — zest only; stands in for candied mixed peel _(optional)_
- **Apricot jam** — about 3 tbsp, for the glaze

**Steps**

1. Warm the milk until just lukewarm (about 100F/38C) and stir in the yeast and a pinch of the sugar; let it sit 5 minutes until foamy. In a large bowl whisk together the flour, remaining sugar, salt, cinnamon, nutmeg, and allspice.
2. Make a well in the dry mix and add the yeasty milk, the softened butter, the eggs, and the orange zest. Stir to a shaggy dough, then knead on a lightly floured surface (or with a dough hook) for 8-10 minutes until smooth, elastic, and slightly tacky.
3. Knead in the raisins until evenly distributed. Shape into a ball, place in a greased bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot for 1 to 1.5 hours, until doubled in size.
4. Punch down the dough and divide into 12 equal pieces (about 80g each). Roll each into a tight smooth ball and arrange just touching in a greased 9x13 baking dish so they bake into pull-apart buns.
5. Cover loosely and let rise again for 45-60 minutes until puffy and the buns are touching. Meanwhile heat the oven to 400F/200C.
6. Make the cross paste: whisk about 1/2 cup flour with 4-5 tbsp water into a thick pipeable paste. Spoon into a piping bag or zip bag with a corner snipped and pipe a continuous cross over the rows of buns.
7. Bake for 18-22 minutes, until golden brown and the buns sound hollow when tapped (internal temp ~190F/88C).
8. While the buns are still hot, warm the apricot jam with a splash of water until loose, push through a sieve if needed, and brush generously over the tops for a glossy finish. Cool slightly and serve warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: jules — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-hot-cross-buns/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Lemon_Drizzle_Slice_-_Caf%C3%A9_W_2025-12-26.jpg/480px-Lemon_Drizzle_Slice_-_Caf%C3%A9_W_2025-12-26.jpg" alt="Photo of Lemon Drizzle Cake">

#### 🍋 [Lemon Drizzle Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-lemon-drizzle-cake/)
British · snack · easy  
**$0.53/serving** · 131 kcal · P3 · C7 · F11 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 10  

A classic British loaf cake — a tender, buttery lemon sponge soaked while warm with a crunchy lemon-sugar drizzle that sets into a sharp, sweet crust. Fragrant with fresh zest and genuinely moist.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 225g unsalted, softened (175g for sponge + extra; use ~1 stick worth)
- **Sugar** — 175g caster sugar for the sponge
- **Eggs** — 3 large eggs, room temperature
- **All-purpose flour** — 175g self-raising flour (or plain + baking powder)
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp
- **Lemon** — 2 for zest into batter, juice of 1.5 for drizzle
- **Milk** — 2 tbsp whole milk to loosen batter
- **Powdered sugar** — 100g granulated/caster sugar for the drizzle topping
- **Salt** — pinch
- **Vanilla extract** — 1/2 tsp, optional

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 180C/350F (160C fan). Grease a 2lb (900g) loaf tin and line it with baking parchment, leaving an overhang on the long sides.
2. Cream 175g softened butter with 175g caster sugar using a wooden spoon or electric beaters for 3-4 minutes until pale and fluffy — this builds the cake's lift.
3. Beat in the 3 eggs one at a time, adding a spoonful of the flour with each to stop the mixture curdling. Mix in the finely grated zest of 2 lemons and the vanilla.
4. Sift in the remaining self-raising flour, baking powder and a pinch of salt, then fold gently with a spatula until just combined. Loosen with 2 tbsp milk to a soft dropping consistency.
5. Spoon into the tin, level the top, and bake for 45-50 minutes until golden and a skewer comes out clean. If it browns too fast, tent loosely with foil.
6. While it bakes, make the drizzle: stir the juice of 1.5 lemons into 100g sugar so it stays grainy (do not dissolve fully — the crunch is the point).
7. As soon as the cake comes out, leave it in the tin and prick all over with a skewer. Spoon the lemon-sugar drizzle slowly over the hot surface so it soaks in and the sugar crusts on top.
8. Cool completely in the tin before lifting out by the parchment. Slice into 10 pieces and serve.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-lemon-drizzle-cake/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Millionaire%27s_Shortbread_-_The_Roastery_2024-06-09.jpg/480px-Millionaire%27s_Shortbread_-_The_Roastery_2024-06-09.jpg" alt="Photo of Millionaire's Shortbread">

#### 🍫 [Millionaire's Shortbread](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-millionaires-shortbread/)
British · snack · medium  
**$0.77/serving** · 135 kcal · P1 · C20 · F6 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 16  

The classic British tray bake done right: a crisp, buttery shortbread base, a thick layer of proper stovetop caramel made from condensed milk and golden syrup cooked to a deep amber, and a snappy dark chocolate top. Cut into neat squares for the ultimate sweet treat.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — ~1.5 cups plain flour for the shortbread base
- **Butter** — unsalted; ~1/2 cup for base + 1/2 cup for caramel
- **Sugar** — ~1/3 cup caster sugar for the shortbread
- **Brown sugar** — ~1/2 cup light brown sugar for the caramel
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — one 14 oz can
- **Corn syrup** — ~1/4 cup golden syrup (light corn syrup as substitute)
- **Dark chocolate** — ~7 oz, 50-60% cacao for the topping
- **Salt** — pinch in base + caramel
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp, optional in caramel _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Heat oven to 350F (175C) and line an 8x8 inch baking tin with parchment, leaving an overhang. For the base, rub or beat together the flour, 1/2 cup softened butter, the caster sugar, and a pinch of salt until it clumps into a soft dough.
2. Press the dough firmly and evenly into the lined tin. Prick all over with a fork and bake for 20-25 minutes until pale golden. Set aside to cool.
3. For the caramel, combine the remaining 1/2 cup butter, brown sugar, golden syrup (corn syrup), the full can of condensed milk, and a pinch of salt in a heavy saucepan over medium-low heat.
4. Stir constantly as it melts, then bring to a gentle simmer. Keep stirring for 8-12 minutes until the mixture thickens and turns a deep golden caramel color (about 225F on a thermometer) and pulls cleanly from the pan base. Stir in the vanilla.
5. Pour the hot caramel over the cooled shortbread, smooth the top, and chill in the fridge for at least 1 hour until fully set and firm.
6. Melt the dark chocolate gently (microwave in 20-second bursts, stirring, or over a bain-marie) until smooth. Pour over the set caramel and tilt to cover evenly.
7. Chill just until the chocolate is matte and almost set, about 15-20 minutes, so it can be cut without cracking. Lift out using the parchment overhang.
8. Using a sharp knife warmed under hot water and wiped dry, trim the edges and cut into 16 neat squares. Store in an airtight container.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-millionaires-shortbread/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1a/Victoria_Sponge_Cake.jpg/480px-Victoria_Sponge_Cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Victoria Sponge Cake">

#### 🍰 [Victoria Sponge Cake](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-victoria-sponge-cake/)
British · snack · medium  
**$0.50/serving** · 102 kcal · P3 · C15 · F3 · ⏱ 75 min · 🍽 8  

The classic British afternoon-tea cake: two layers of light, buttery vanilla sponge made by the creaming method, sandwiched with a generous layer of raspberry jam and softly whipped cream, finished with a dusting of powdered sugar. Tender, golden, and not too sweet.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 225g unsalted, softened to room temperature
- **Sugar** — 225g caster/superfine sugar
- **Eggs** — large, room temperature
- **All-purpose flour** — 225g self-rising flour (or all-purpose + 2 tsp baking powder)
- **Baking powder** — 2 tsp, if using plain flour
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Milk** — 1-2 tbsp, to loosen batter to dropping consistency
- **Salt** — pinch
- **Jam** — about 1/2 cup good raspberry (or strawberry) jam
- **Heavy cream** — about 250ml, whipped
- **Powdered sugar** — 1 tbsp for dusting, plus a little to sweeten cream

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 180C/350F. Grease two 8-inch round cake pans and line the bases with parchment. Make sure butter and eggs are at room temperature.
2. Cream the softened butter and caster sugar together with an electric mixer for 4-5 minutes until very pale, light, and fluffy. This aeration is what gives the sponge its rise.
3. Beat in the eggs one at a time, mixing well after each addition. If the batter starts to curdle, add a spoonful of the flour to bring it back together.
4. Sift the self-rising flour (with baking powder and salt if using plain flour) over the batter and fold it in gently with a spatula until just combined. Fold in the vanilla and enough milk to give a soft dropping consistency.
5. Divide the batter evenly between the two pans and level the tops. Bake for 22-25 minutes until golden, springy to the touch, and a skewer comes out clean.
6. Cool in the pans for 5 minutes, then turn out onto a wire rack, peel off the parchment, and cool completely. Never fill a warm sponge or the cream will melt.
7. Whip the heavy cream with a little powdered sugar to soft peaks. Spread the raspberry jam over the flat side of one sponge, then spread or pipe the whipped cream on top.
8. Set the second sponge on top, flat side down. Dust the top generously with powdered sugar through a sieve. Serve the same day, ideally with tea.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Victoria Spongebob — CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-victoria-sponge-cake/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Butter_tarts_on_cutting_board%288293490785%29.jpg/480px-Butter_tarts_on_cutting_board%288293490785%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Canadian Butter Tarts">

#### 🧈 [Classic Canadian Butter Tarts](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-butter-tarts/)
Canadian · snack · medium  
**$0.46/serving** · 235 kcal · P2 · C26 · F13 · ⏱ 85 min · 🍽 12  

The real deal: a flaky, all-butter pastry shell cradling a gooey brown-sugar-and-corn-syrup filling that's set at the edges and just-runny in the center. Faithful to the Ontario classic, with raisins for the traditionalists and the choice to leave them out. The defining trick is a low-and-slow bake so the filling stays soft, never candy-hard.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 1 1/2 cups all-purpose flour (for the pastry)
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp salt
- **Butter** — 10 tbsp (1 1/4 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed — for the pastry
- **Water** — about 1/4 cup ice water, added gradually
- **Brown sugar** — 3/4 cup packed light brown sugar (filling)
- **Corn syrup** — 1/2 cup light corn syrup (filling)
- **Butter** — 1/4 cup unsalted butter, melted — for the filling
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, lightly beaten
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Vinegar** — 1 tsp white vinegar (balances the sweetness, keeps filling tender)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt (filling)
- **Raisins** — 1/2 cup raisins (optional, but traditional) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the pastry: Whisk the 1 1/2 cups flour and 1/2 tsp salt together. Cut in the cold cubed butter with your fingertips or a pastry cutter until the mixture looks like coarse crumbs with some pea-sized pieces of butter still visible. Drizzle in ice water a tablespoon at a time, tossing with a fork, until the dough just holds together when squeezed. Shape into a disk, wrap, and chill at least 30 minutes.
2. Roll the chilled dough on a lightly floured surface to about 1/8 inch thick. Cut twelve 4-inch rounds (re-roll scraps once). Press each round into the cups of a standard 12-cup muffin tin, easing the pastry up the sides so the top edge sits just below the rim. Chill the lined tin in the fridge while you make the filling.
3. Position a rack in the lower third of the oven and preheat to 375 F (190 C). If using raisins, divide them among the chilled shells (about 2 tsp per tart).
4. Make the filling: Whisk together the 3/4 cup brown sugar, 1/2 cup corn syrup, 1/4 cup melted butter, beaten egg, 1 tsp vanilla, 1 tsp vinegar, and 1/4 tsp salt until smooth. Do not overbeat — you don't want a frothy filling, or it will puff and crack.
5. Spoon or pour the filling into the shells, filling each only about two-thirds full. The filling will bubble up and rise as it bakes, so leaving headroom prevents overflow that glues the tarts to the pan.
6. Bake on the lower rack for 18 to 22 minutes, until the pastry is golden and the filling is puffed and bubbling at the edges but still wobbles in the center. Pull them while the centers look slightly underdone — they finish setting as they cool, and this is the secret to a runny middle instead of a hard one.
7. Cool the tarts in the pan on a wire rack for 10 minutes, then run a thin knife or small offset spatula around each one to release it. Carefully lift the tarts out and finish cooling on the rack. Serve warm or at room temperature; the filling thickens to a glossy, gooey caramel as it cools.

</details>

<sub>📷 Rob Campbell, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-butter-tarts/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Mooncake.jpg/480px-Mooncake.jpg" alt="Photo of Mooncakes (Cantonese Red Bean & Salted Egg Yolk)">

#### 🥮 [Mooncakes (Cantonese Red Bean & Salted Egg Yolk)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-mooncakes/)
Chinese · snack · hard  
**$0.14/serving** · 26 kcal · P1 · C4 · F1 · ⏱ 150 min · 🍽 12  

Classic Cantonese-style baked mooncakes with a tender, glossy golden-syrup skin wrapped around sweet red bean paste and a savory salted egg yolk center. Pressed in a decorative mold and rested for a few days so the skin softens and turns translucent — the way the bakeries do it.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose or cake flour, ~200g for the syrup skin
- **Corn syrup** — golden/invert syrup, ~140g; the defining mooncake-skin syrup
- **Peanut oil** — ~50g neutral peanut oil for the dough
- **Baking soda** — alkaline (lye/kansui) water: tiny pinch dissolved in 1 tsp water — gives color and tender skin
- **Red beans (canned)** — made into ~600g sweet red bean (or lotus seed) paste; or buy ready-made paste
- **White sugar** — ~120g, for cooking down the bean paste
- **Eggs** — 6 salted duck egg yolks split among the cakes; plus 1 egg yolk for the wash

**Steps**

1. Make the paste (or use store-bought): simmer cooked red beans with the sugar and a little peanut oil over low heat, mashing and stirring constantly until thick enough to hold its shape and pull away from the pan; cool completely, then divide into twelve ~50g balls.
2. Prep the salted egg yolks: rinse, pat dry, and bake or steam the duck yolks until just set; halve each so you get twelve pieces, and press one into the center of each paste ball, re-rolling to seal the yolk inside.
3. Make the syrup skin: whisk together the golden/invert syrup, peanut oil, and the alkaline water (a pinch of baking soda dissolved in 1 tsp water). Sift in the flour and fold just until a soft, slightly sticky dough forms — do not knead. Rest, covered, 1 hour.
4. Divide the dough into twelve ~25g pieces. Working one at a time, flatten a piece into a thin round, set a filling ball on top, and gently push the dough up and around the filling until fully enclosed, sealing the seam.
5. Dust a mooncake mold lightly with flour, set the wrapped ball seam-side up, and press firmly to imprint the design; release onto a parchment-lined tray. Repeat with the rest.
6. Spritz the tops lightly with water and bake at 375F (190C) for 5-6 minutes to set the pattern. Remove, let cool 10 minutes, then brush a very thin layer of beaten egg yolk only on the raised surfaces.
7. Return to the oven and bake another 10-12 minutes until golden brown and the sides look set. Cool completely on the tray.
8. Rest the mooncakes in an airtight container at room temperature for 2-3 days (回油): the skin will absorb oil from the filling, soften, and turn glossy and slightly translucent before serving.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Lybil BER — CC BY-SA 3.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-mooncakes/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Dom%C3%A1c%C3%AD_medovn%C3%ADk.jpg/480px-Dom%C3%A1c%C3%AD_medovn%C3%ADk.jpg" alt="Photo of Medovník (Czech Honey Layer Cake)">

#### 🍯 [Medovník (Czech Honey Layer Cake)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-medovn-k/)
Czech · snack · hard  
**$1.27/serving** · 472 kcal · P6 · C48 · F29 · ⏱ 90 min · 🍽 12  

The authentic Central European honey cake: thin, caramel-warm honey sponge layers cooked over a double boiler, baked in minutes, then sandwiched with a tangy sour-cream-and-butter cream. After a day's rest the layers drink in the filling and turn meltingly soft. A genuine showpiece with a deep honey flavor and gentle caramel notes.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Honey** — 1/3 cup honey, for the dough
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar, for the dough
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup (1 stick) unsalted butter, for the dough
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs
- **Baking soda** — 2 tsp baking soda
- **All-purpose flour** — about 3 1/4 cups all-purpose flour (added gradually until a soft dough forms)
- **Cinnamon** — 1 tsp ground cinnamon (optional, traditional in many regions) _(optional)_
- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks) unsalted butter, softened, for the cream
- **Sour cream** — 1 cup full-fat sour cream, for the cream
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — 1 can (14 oz) sweetened condensed milk, for the cream
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp vanilla
- **Walnuts** — 1 cup walnuts, finely chopped, for filling and topping

**Steps**

1. Make the honey dough over a double boiler: in a heatproof bowl set over simmering water, combine the honey, sugar, and 1/2 cup (1 stick) butter. Stir until melted and smooth, then keep over the heat for 2-3 minutes until the mixture is glossy and just beginning to take on a light caramel color.
2. Take the bowl off the heat for a moment, whisk the baking soda into the warm honey mixture, and watch it foam up and pale. Return to low heat for 30 seconds, stirring, until the foam settles slightly. Off the heat, whisk in the beaten eggs quickly so they don't scramble, plus the cinnamon if using.
3. Add the flour about 1/2 cup at a time, stirring, until you have a soft, slightly sticky dough that pulls together (you may not need all the flour). Turn it out onto a floured surface, knead briefly into a smooth ball, then divide into 6 equal pieces. Chill 30 minutes for easier rolling.
4. Preheat the oven to 350F. Roll each piece on a sheet of parchment into a thin round about 9 inches across. Trim to a neat 8-inch circle using a plate as a guide, saving the scraps. Bake each layer on its parchment for 5-7 minutes, until golden and dry; they firm up as they cool. Bake the scraps too, then crush them into crumbs for decoration.
5. Make the cream: beat the softened 1 cup butter until pale and fluffy, 3-4 minutes. Beat in the sweetened condensed milk a little at a time, then the sour cream and vanilla, until silky and spreadable. Chill 15 minutes if it feels loose.
6. Assemble: place one cooled layer on a serving plate, spread with a generous layer of cream, and scatter a little chopped walnuts. Repeat with all layers, ending with cream on top. Use the remaining cream to coat the sides.
7. Press the reserved crumbs and remaining chopped walnuts over the top and sides to fully cover the cake.
8. Refrigerate at least 12 hours, ideally 24, before slicing. This rest is essential: the dry layers absorb the cream and become tender and soft. Serve cold, in thin slices.

</details>

<sub>📷 Dezidor, CC BY 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-medovn-k/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Vanocka.jpg/480px-Vanocka.jpg" alt="Photo of Vánočka (Czech Christmas Braided Bread)">

#### 🍞 [Vánočka (Czech Christmas Braided Bread)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-v-no-ka/)
Czech · breakfast · hard  
**$0.49/serving** · 291 kcal · P6 · C40 · F10 · ⏱ 210 min · 🍽 12  

The classic Czech Christmas bread: a richly enriched, lightly sweet yeasted dough braided into a tall three-tier loaf, studded with rum-soaked raisins and slivered almonds, scented with lemon zest, and glazed gold with egg wash before baking. Soft, buttery, and tender like a Central European challah-meets-brioche, traditionally served Christmas morning with butter.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 4 cups all-purpose flour (plus a little for dusting)
- **Whole milk** — 3/4 cup whole milk, warmed to lukewarm
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant yeast (one 7g packet)
- **Sugar** — 6 tbsp granulated sugar
- **Butter** — 10 tbsp unsalted butter, softened
- **Eggs** — 2 whole eggs plus 2 egg yolks (reserve whites/1 yolk for wash)
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Lemon zest** — zest of 1 lemon (about 1 1/2 tsp)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Raisins** — 3/4 cup raisins
- **Rum** — 2 oz dark rum, for soaking raisins (optional) _(optional)_
- **Slivered almonds** — 1/2 cup slivered almonds, for inside and topping
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting (optional) _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Soak the raisins: warm the rum (or 2 tbsp water) and pour over the raisins; let them plump while you make the dough. Warm the milk to lukewarm (about 100F/38C) and stir in the yeast and a pinch of the sugar; let it sit 5-10 minutes until foamy.
2. Make the dough: in a large bowl combine the flour, remaining sugar, salt, and lemon zest. Add the yeasted milk, the 2 whole eggs and 2 yolks, the vanilla, and the softened butter. Mix to a shaggy dough, then knead 10-12 minutes by hand (or 8 with a stand mixer) until smooth, elastic, and only slightly tacky. The dough should be soft and supple, not dry.
3. First rise: drain the raisins well and knead them in along with half the slivered almonds. Shape into a ball, cover, and let rise in a warm spot 1 to 1.5 hours until doubled.
4. Divide for braiding: gently deflate and divide the dough into 6 portions: 3 large, 2 medium, 1 small. Roll each into a smooth rope, the large ones about 14 inches long. Keep the ropes covered as you work so they don't dry out.
5. Braid the tiers: braid the 3 large ropes into a flat plait on a parchment-lined baking sheet, tucking the ends under. Braid the 2 medium ropes (or twist them) and lay this braid centered on top. Press a chopstick handle down the length to seat it. Twist the small rope and lay it down the center top. Optionally secure the tiers with a couple of toothpicks (remember to remove after baking).
6. Second rise: loosely cover the braided loaf and let it proof 45-60 minutes until puffy and nearly doubled. Meanwhile heat the oven to 350F (175C).
7. Glaze and top: beat the reserved egg yolk with a splash of milk and brush the loaf thoroughly, getting into the crevices. Scatter the remaining slivered almonds over the top.
8. Bake: bake 35-45 minutes until deep golden and the internal temperature reaches about 190F (88C). If the top browns too fast, tent loosely with foil after 20 minutes. Cool completely on a rack before slicing; dust with powdered sugar if desired.

</details>

<sub>📷 Ludek, CC BY-SA 3.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-v-no-ka/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Cheese_danish_-_Massachusetts.jpg/480px-Cheese_danish_-_Massachusetts.jpg" alt="Photo of Cheese Danish">

#### 🧀 [Cheese Danish](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cheese-danish/)
Danish · breakfast · hard  
**$0.96/serving** · 359 kcal · P8 · C30 · F23 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 8  

Flaky, buttery yeasted pastry with a tangy-sweet vanilla cream cheese center and a glossy egg-wash finish. A bakery-style cheese Danish made from scratch with a quick rough-puff laminated dough.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — all-purpose flour, ~2.25 cups for the dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp (1 packet)
- **Sugar** — split: a little in the dough, the rest in the filling
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp fine salt
- **Milk** — 1/2 cup warm whole milk
- **Butter** — 1 stick + 2 tbsp cold; most laminated into dough, 1 tbsp melted to brush
- **Eggs** — 1 whole in dough, 1 yolk in filling + white reserved for egg wash
- **Cream cheese** — 8 oz block, softened
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract
- **Lemon** — zest + a squeeze of juice
- **Powdered sugar** — for a thin glaze drizzle _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Bloom the dough: warm the milk to lukewarm, stir in the yeast and 1 tbsp of the sugar, and let it sit 5 minutes until foamy. Whisk in 1 whole egg.
2. Make the dough: combine flour, 1 tbsp sugar, and salt. Cut in 8 tbsp (1 stick) of cold butter in chunks, leaving visible flakes the size of large peas. Stir in the wet mixture just until a shaggy dough forms. Wrap and chill 30 minutes.
3. Laminate: on a floured surface roll the dough into a rectangle, fold in thirds like a letter, rotate 90 degrees, and roll out again. Repeat this fold-and-roll twice more (3 folds total) to build flaky layers, chilling 10 minutes if the butter softens. Wrap and refrigerate 1 hour.
4. Make the filling: beat the softened cream cheese with the remaining sugar (about 1/4 cup) until smooth, then beat in 1 egg yolk, the vanilla, lemon zest, and a small squeeze of lemon juice until silky.
5. Shape: roll the chilled dough to about 1/4 inch thick and cut into 8 squares. Make a well by pressing the center with your thumb (or fold the corners toward the middle pinwheel-style), then spoon a generous dollop of cream cheese filling into each center.
6. Proof: arrange on a parchment-lined sheet, cover loosely, and let rise in a warm spot 30 to 45 minutes until puffy. Meanwhile heat the oven to 400F.
7. Egg wash and bake: brush the exposed pastry edges with the reserved egg white beaten with a splash of water. Bake 18 to 22 minutes until deep golden and the filling is just set.
8. Finish: cool 10 minutes, then whisk powdered sugar with a few drops of water or lemon juice and drizzle a thin glaze over the warm Danishes.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-cheese-danish/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/ab/Speculaas%2C_Spekulatius_or_Dutch_Windmill_cookie_%285588364895%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Dutch Speculaas">

#### 🍪 [Classic Dutch Speculaas](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-speculaas/)
Dutch · snack · easy  
**$0.22/serving** · 107 kcal · P1 · C15 · F5 · ⏱ 100 min · 🍽 30  

Authentic Sinterklaas spiced biscuits with a deep caramel snap, built on a generous speculaaskruiden blend of cinnamon, clove, nutmeg, ginger, cardamom, and white pepper. Brown sugar and a short rest give them their signature crisp bite and warm, fragrant character.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/2 cups all-purpose flour
- **Brown sugar** — 1 cup packed dark brown sugar
- **Butter** — 3/4 cup (1 1/2 sticks) cold unsalted butter, cubed
- **Cinnamon** — 4 tsp ground cinnamon
- **Whole cloves** — 1 tsp ground cloves
- **Nutmeg** — 3/4 tsp ground nutmeg
- **Ground ginger** — 3/4 tsp ground ginger
- **Cardamom** — 1/2 tsp ground cardamom
- **White pepper** — 1/4 tsp ground white pepper
- **Baking powder** — 1 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 1/2 tsp fine salt
- **Milk** — 3 tbsp whole milk, plus more as needed
- **Slivered almonds** — 1/2 cup slivered almonds, for pressing on top _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. In a large bowl, whisk together the flour, dark brown sugar, cinnamon, cloves, nutmeg, ginger, cardamom, white pepper, baking powder, and salt, breaking up any lumps of brown sugar with your fingers so the spices distribute evenly.
2. Add the cold cubed butter and rub it into the dry mix with your fingertips (or pulse in a food processor) until the mixture looks like coarse, sandy crumbs with no butter pieces larger than a pea.
3. Drizzle in the milk a tablespoon at a time, mixing just until the dough comes together into a firm, slightly stiff mass; add an extra teaspoon of milk only if it stays crumbly. Do not overwork it.
4. Press the dough into a flat disc, wrap tightly, and chill in the refrigerator for at least 1 hour (or overnight) so the spices bloom and the dough firms up for clean cuts.
5. Preheat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment. Roll the dough on a lightly floured surface to a scant 1/4 inch thick and stamp out shapes (windmills, speculaas molds, or simple rectangles), re-rolling scraps.
6. Arrange the cookies about 1 inch apart on the sheets and, if using, gently press a few slivered almonds onto the tops of each one.
7. Bake for 12 to 15 minutes, until the edges are set and the cookies are deeply golden and fragrant; they will still feel slightly soft in the center but will crisp as they cool.
8. Let them rest on the sheet for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack to cool completely until snappy. Store airtight for at least a day before serving for the best flavor and crunch.

</details>

<sub>📷 Turku Gingerbread from Finland, CC BY 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-speculaas/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Russian_tea_cakes.jpg/480px-Russian_tea_cakes.jpg" alt="Photo of Russian Tea Cakes">

#### 🍪 [Russian Tea Cakes](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-russian-tea-cakes/)
Eastern European · snack · easy  
**$0.43/serving** · 110 kcal · P1 · C9 · F7 · ⏱ 45 min · 🍽 24  

Tender, buttery shortbread-style cookies packed with finely chopped pecans and rolled twice in powdered sugar so they look like little snowballs. They melt in your mouth and come together with five pantry staples.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Butter** — 1 cup (2 sticks), unsalted, softened
- **Powdered sugar** — 1/2 cup for dough, plus about 1 cup for rolling/coating
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp
- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups all-purpose
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp
- **Pecans** — 1 cup, very finely chopped

**Steps**

1. Heat the oven to 350F (175C) and line two baking sheets with parchment paper.
2. In a large bowl, beat the softened butter with 1/2 cup powdered sugar until light, pale, and fluffy, about 2 minutes. Beat in the vanilla.
3. On low speed, gradually add the flour and salt, mixing just until a soft dough forms, then fold in the finely chopped pecans until evenly distributed.
4. Scoop the dough into 1-inch balls (about 1 level tablespoon each) and roll between your palms until smooth; space them 1 inch apart on the prepared sheets.
5. Bake for 12 to 14 minutes, until the bottoms are lightly golden but the tops are still pale. Let the cookies rest on the sheet for 5 minutes so they firm up.
6. While the cookies are still warm, roll each one in the reserved powdered sugar to coat fully, then transfer to a rack to cool.
7. Once completely cool, roll the cookies a second time in powdered sugar for the classic snowy, fully-coated finish.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Maryanne Rodriguez — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-russian-tea-cakes/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Chocolate_Babka_-_31706252800.jpg/480px-Chocolate_Babka_-_31706252800.jpg" alt="Photo of Chocolate Babka">

#### 🍫 [Chocolate Babka](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-chocolate-babka/)
Eastern European Jewish · snack · hard  
**$0.82/serving** · 248 kcal · P5 · C30 · F12 · ⏱ 360 min · 🍽 10  

A buttery enriched yeast dough rolled around a deep, fudgy dark-chocolate-and-cocoa filling, twisted to expose ribbons of chocolate, baked into a glossy loaf, and soaked in vanilla sugar syrup. Tender, rich, and worth every minute of the rise.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — bread or all-purpose flour, about 2.5 cups for the dough
- **Instant yeast** — 2 tsp instant yeast
- **Sugar** — split: ~1/4 cup in dough, plus more for filling and syrup
- **Eggs** — 2 large eggs, room temperature
- **Milk** — about 1/3 cup lukewarm whole milk
- **Butter** — split: ~9 Tbsp in dough, ~6 Tbsp in filling
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp fine salt in dough
- **Dark chocolate** — about 4.5 oz, chopped, for the filling
- **Cocoa powder** — 3 Tbsp unsweetened cocoa powder for the filling
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp, split between dough and syrup
- **Powdered sugar** — optional dusting _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Make the dough: in a stand mixer with the dough hook, combine flour, sugar, instant yeast, and salt. Add the eggs and lukewarm milk and mix on low until a shaggy dough forms, then knead 5 minutes until it comes together.
2. With the mixer running, add the softened dough butter a tablespoon at a time, letting each piece absorb before adding more. Knead 8-10 minutes until the dough is smooth, glossy, and elastic. Cover and refrigerate at least 4 hours or overnight (cold dough is essential for clean rolling).
3. Make the filling: melt the chocolate and filling butter together until smooth, then whisk in the cocoa and sugar to a thick, spreadable paste. Let it cool until just spreadable but not stiff.
4. On a floured surface, roll the cold dough into a large rectangle about 10x18 inches and 1/4 inch thick. Spread the chocolate filling evenly to the edges, then roll up tightly from a long side into a snug log.
5. Chill the log 15-20 minutes to firm up, then trim the ends and slice it lengthwise down the middle to expose the layers. With cut sides facing up, twist the two halves over each other into a tight two-strand braid and tuck it into a parchment-lined loaf pan.
6. Cover loosely and let rise at room temperature 1 to 1.5 hours, until puffy and nearly filling the pan. Meanwhile heat the oven to 350F (175C).
7. Bake 30-35 minutes until deep golden and set; tent with foil if it browns too fast. The center should read about 190F.
8. While it bakes, simmer the syrup sugar with an equal splash of water and the vanilla for 2 minutes. As soon as the babka comes out, brush the hot syrup all over the top so it soaks in.
9. Cool in the pan 15 minutes, then lift out and cool fully before slicing. Dust with powdered sugar if you like.

</details>

<sub>📷 Photo: Joy — CC BY 2.0 via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-chocolate-babka/)</sub>

<br clear="left">

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Brazo_de_mercedes.jpg/480px-Brazo_de_mercedes.jpg" alt="Photo of Brazo de Mercedes">

#### 🍮 [Brazo de Mercedes](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-brazo-de-mercedes/)
Filipino · snack · medium  
**$0.94/serving** · 144 kcal · P6 · C16 · F6 · ⏱ 120 min · 🍽 10  

A beloved Filipino rolled meringue cake. A cloud-soft sheet of baked meringue is dusted with powdered sugar, then rolled around a luscious custard filling of egg yolks and sweetened condensed milk. It uses no flour at all, so the whole thing is tender, sweet, and melts on the tongue. The name means "Mercedes' arm" for its rolled, arm-like shape.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Eggs** — 10 large eggs, separated (whites for meringue, yolks for filling)
- **Cream of tartar** — 1 tsp cream of tartar
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup white sugar (for the meringue)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 1/2 tsp vanilla extract (1 tsp for meringue, 1/2 tsp for filling)
- **Sweetened condensed milk** — 1 can (14 oz) sweetened condensed milk (for the custard filling)
- **Butter** — 1 tbsp unsalted butter, for the custard filling
- **Lemon zest** — 1 tsp finely grated lemon zest (brightens the custard)
- **Powdered sugar** — about 3 tbsp powdered sugar, for dusting
- **Cooking spray** — cooking spray, to grease the pan and parchment _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 350F (175C). Line a 13x18-inch rimmed sheet pan with parchment paper, then lightly grease the parchment with cooking spray so the baked meringue releases cleanly.
2. Separate the 10 eggs carefully, putting whites in a large spotlessly clean, grease-free bowl and yolks in a heatproof bowl or saucepan. Even a trace of yolk or fat will keep the whites from whipping, so separate one egg at a time into a cup first.
3. Make the meringue: beat the egg whites with the cream of tartar on medium speed until foamy, then increase to high and gradually add the 3/4 cup white sugar a spoonful at a time. Add 1 tsp vanilla and continue beating until the meringue holds stiff, glossy peaks and the sugar is fully dissolved (rub a little between your fingers; it should feel smooth, not gritty).
4. Spread the meringue evenly into the prepared pan with an offset spatula, smoothing the top. Run the spatula in gentle diagonal lines or shallow waves across the surface for the traditional ridged look. Bake for 18 to 22 minutes, until the top is lightly golden and springs back when touched. Do not overbake or it will crack when rolled.
5. While the meringue bakes, make the custard: whisk the 10 egg yolks with the sweetened condensed milk, 1 tbsp butter, lemon zest, and 1/2 tsp vanilla in the heatproof bowl. Cook over a double boiler (or very low direct heat), stirring constantly, for 10 to 15 minutes until thick enough to coat the back of a spoon and leave a trail. Remove from heat and let cool slightly; it will firm up as it cools.
6. When the meringue is done, immediately dust the top generously with powdered sugar. Lay a sheet of parchment over it, then carefully invert the whole thing onto a clean work surface and gently peel away the baking parchment from what is now the top.
7. Dust the exposed surface with more powdered sugar. Spread the warm custard filling evenly over the meringue, leaving a 1-inch border along one long edge so the filling does not squeeze out when rolling.
8. Using the bottom parchment to lift and guide, roll the meringue snugly from one long side into a tight log, seam-side down. Roll firmly but gently to avoid cracks. Wrap in the parchment and chill at least 1 hour to set.
9. Unwrap, dust with a final layer of powdered sugar, and trim the ends for a clean look. Slice with a serrated knife into thick rounds and serve chilled.

</details>

<sub>📷 VirtualWolf, CC BY-SA 2.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-brazo-de-mercedes/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Fresh_baked_Pandesal.jpg/480px-Fresh_baked_Pandesal.jpg" alt="Photo of Classic Filipino Pandesal">

#### 🍞 [Classic Filipino Pandesal](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pandesal/)
Filipino · breakfast · medium  
**$0.24/serving** · 229 kcal · P6 · C39 · F5 · ⏱ 180 min · 🍽 12  

The beloved Filipino breakfast roll — soft, slightly sweet, and pillowy with a tender crumb and a signature coating of breadcrumbs. An enriched yeast dough that bakes up golden and is best torn open warm, often dunked in coffee or hot chocolate. This is the real deal: no shortcuts, just a properly proofed milk-and-egg dough.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 4 cups all-purpose (or bread) flour, divided
- **Milk** — 1 cup whole milk, warmed to about 110°F
- **Sugar** — 6 tbsp white sugar (a generous 1/3 cup)
- **Butter** — 1/4 cup unsalted butter, melted
- **Eggs** — 1 large egg, room temperature
- **Instant yeast** — 2 1/4 tsp instant yeast (1 standard packet)
- **Salt** — 1 1/2 tsp salt
- **Italian breadcrumbs** — 1/2 cup fine plain breadcrumbs, for coating

**Steps**

1. Bloom the yeast: In the bowl of a stand mixer, combine the warm milk (about 110°F), 1 tablespoon of the sugar, and the instant yeast. Stir and let sit 5–10 minutes until foamy. If it doesn't foam, your yeast is dead — start over with fresh yeast.
2. Build the dough: Add the remaining sugar, melted butter, egg, salt, and 3 cups of the flour. Mix with the dough hook on low until a shaggy dough forms, then add the last cup of flour a few tablespoons at a time until the dough pulls away from the sides but is still slightly tacky.
3. Knead: Knead with the dough hook on medium for 8–10 minutes (or by hand 12–15 minutes) until the dough is smooth, elastic, and passes the windowpane test — a small piece stretches thin without tearing.
4. First rise: Shape into a ball, place in a lightly oiled bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot for 1 to 1.5 hours, until doubled in size.
5. Shape: Punch down the dough and turn it out onto a lightly floured surface. Roll into a log and divide into 12 equal pieces. Roll each into a tight ball, then roll each ball in the breadcrumbs to coat all over.
6. Second rise: Arrange the coated balls cut-side up (or seam-down, spaced apart) on a parchment-lined baking sheet. Cover loosely and let rise 45–60 minutes until puffy and nearly doubled. Meanwhile, preheat the oven to 350°F (175°C).
7. Bake: Bake on the middle rack for 18–22 minutes, until the tops are light golden and the rolls sound hollow when tapped. Rotate the pan halfway through for even color — pandesal should stay pale-gold, not deeply browned.
8. Cool and serve: Let cool on the pan for 5 minutes, then transfer to a rack. Serve warm, torn open and slathered with butter, or dunked in coffee or hot chocolate.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-pandesal/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Filipino_ensaymada_%28ensaimada%29.jpg/480px-Filipino_ensaymada_%28ensaimada%29.jpg" alt="Photo of Filipino Ensaimada">

#### 🍞 [Filipino Ensaimada](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-filipino-ensaimada/)
Filipino · snack · hard  
**$0.57/serving** · 331 kcal · P8 · C42 · F14 · ⏱ 240 min · 🍽 8  

The beloved Filipino merienda bread: a soft, buttery, egg-yolk-enriched brioche coiled into a spiral, baked until golden, then crowned with a smear of butter, a snowfall of sugar, and a generous shower of grated cheese. Pillowy-tender with a salty-sweet finish.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — about 2.75 cups bread or all-purpose flour
- **Instant yeast** — 2.25 tsp (1 packet)
- **Whole milk** — 3/4 cup, warmed
- **Sugar** — 1/3 cup in dough, plus more for topping
- **Eggs** — use 4 yolks in dough; reserve whites for another use
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup softened in dough + extra melted for brushing
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp
- **Shredded cheese** — grated Edam (queso de bola) or sharp cheddar for topping
- **Powdered sugar** — optional fine dusting over the sugar topping _(optional)_

**Steps**

1. Bloom and mix: Whisk the warm milk (about 110F), yeast, and 1 tablespoon of the sugar; let it foam 5-8 minutes. In a large bowl combine the flour, remaining sugar, and salt, then stir in the egg yolks and the foamy milk to form a shaggy, sticky dough.
2. Knead: Turn out and knead 10-12 minutes (or 8 minutes in a stand mixer with dough hook) until smooth and elastic. Gradually work in the softened butter a tablespoon at a time, kneading until the dough is glossy, supple, and passes the windowpane test.
3. First rise: Place in a buttered bowl, cover, and let rise in a warm spot 1.5-2 hours until doubled. The high-yolk, high-butter dough is rich, so be patient.
4. Shape the coils: Punch down and divide into 8 pieces. Roll each into a thin rope or flatten into a long strip, brush generously with melted butter, then roll up and twist into a tight spiral, tucking the end underneath. Place in buttered round molds or on a lined tray.
5. Second rise: Cover loosely and proof 1 to 1.5 hours until puffy and nearly doubled.
6. Bake: Bake at 350F for 15-18 minutes until light golden; ensaimada should stay pale and soft, not browned hard. Cool 5 minutes.
7. Finish: While still warm, brush the tops with melted butter, sprinkle generously with granulated sugar, then blanket with grated Edam or cheddar. Dust with powdered sugar if you like and serve warm.

</details>

<sub>📷 CC0 · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake-filipino-ensaimada/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Ube_cake.jpg/480px-Ube_cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Filipino Ube Cake (Purple Yam Chiffon)">

#### 🍠 [Filipino Ube Cake (Purple Yam Chiffon)](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-ube-cake/)
Filipino · snack · medium  
**$1.13/serving** · 465 kcal · P8 · C49 · F27 · ⏱ 110 min · 🍽 12  

A genuinely classic Filipino ube cake: a cloud-light chiffon sponge built on the meringue method, deeply flavored with ube halaya and ube extract for that signature violet color and nutty-vanilla yam flavor, layered with a tangy ube cream cheese frosting. Soft, moist, and just sweet enough — the version you'd proudly bring to a fiesta.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **All-purpose flour** — 2 1/4 cups cake flour (sub all-purpose, see substitutions)
- **Baking powder** — 4 tsp baking powder
- **Salt** — 3/4 tsp salt
- **Sugar** — 1 1/2 cups granulated sugar, divided
- **Vegetable oil** — 1/2 cup vegetable oil
- **Eggs** — 7 large eggs, separated (yolks + whites)
- **Whole milk** — 1/2 cup whole milk
- **Ube halaya (purple yam jam)** — 1/2 cup ube halaya (purple yam jam); if unavailable use mashed taro or extra extract
- **Vanilla extract** — 2 tsp ube extract (or 1.5 tsp ube + 0.5 tsp vanilla)
- **Cream of tartar** — 1/2 tsp cream of tartar
- **Cream cheese** — 8 oz cream cheese, softened (for frosting)
- **Butter** — 1/2 cup unsalted butter, softened (for frosting)
- **Powdered sugar** — 2 cups powdered sugar (for frosting)

**Steps**

1. Position a rack in the center of the oven and preheat to 325F (165C). Line the bottoms of two 8-inch round cake pans with parchment; leave the sides UNGREASED so the chiffon batter can climb. In a bowl, whisk together 2 1/4 cups cake flour, 4 tsp baking powder, and 3/4 tsp salt; set aside.
2. Make the yolk batter: In a large bowl whisk the 7 egg yolks with 3/4 cup of the sugar (12 tbsp) until pale and thick, about 1 minute. Whisk in 1/2 cup oil, 1/2 cup whole milk, 1/2 cup ube halaya, and 2 tsp ube extract until smooth and an even deep purple. Sift the flour mixture over the top and whisk just until no dry streaks remain — do not overmix.
3. Make the meringue: In a clean, grease-free bowl beat the 7 egg whites with 1/2 tsp cream of tartar until foamy. With the mixer running, gradually stream in the remaining 3/4 cup sugar (12 tbsp) and beat to firm, glossy peaks that hold their shape but aren't dry.
4. Fold: Stir about one-third of the meringue into the yolk batter to lighten it. Then gently fold in the rest in two additions with a spatula, cutting down through the center and lifting up around the sides, until just combined and no white streaks remain. Keep the batter airy.
5. Divide the batter evenly between the two prepared pans and smooth the tops. Rap each pan once firmly on the counter to release any large bubbles.
6. Bake at 325F for 32 to 38 minutes, until the tops spring back when lightly pressed and a toothpick inserted in the center comes out clean. Immediately invert the pans onto a wire rack (rest the rims on cans if needed) and cool completely upside down — this keeps the delicate chiffon from collapsing.
7. While the cakes cool, make the frosting: Beat the softened cream cheese and softened butter together until smooth and fluffy, 2 to 3 minutes. Add 2 cups powdered sugar gradually and beat until light. For an ube-purple frosting, beat in 1/2 tsp ube extract or a spoonful of ube halaya; refrigerate 15 minutes if too soft to spread.
8. Run a thin knife around the pan sides to release the cooled cakes and turn them out. Place one layer on a plate, spread with a generous layer of frosting, top with the second layer, then frost the top and sides. Chill 20 minutes to set before slicing.

</details>

<sub>📷 Roderick Suñaz Sumalinog, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-ube-cake/)</sub>

<img align="left" width="240" src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/bc/Sans-rival-meringue-layer-cake.jpg" alt="Photo of Sans Rival">

#### 🍰 [Sans Rival](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sans-rival/)
Filipino · snack · hard  
**$1.01/serving** · 399 kcal · P4 · C33 · F28 · ⏱ 240 min · 🍽 12  

The classic Filipino dacquoise — four layers of crisp, chewy cashew-meringue (dacquoise) stacked with a rich, silky French buttercream and blanketed in toasted chopped cashews. Named "without rival" for good reason: it's the celebration cake of the Philippines, all crunch, butter, and toasted-nut perfume.

<details><summary>📋 Ingredients & steps</summary>

**Ingredients**

- **Liquid egg whites** — 10 egg whites, room temperature (about 2.5 cups / use fresh-separated whites)
- **Cream of tartar** — 1/2 tsp cream of tartar
- **Sugar** — 1 cup granulated sugar (for the meringue)
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract (for the meringue)
- **Cashews** — 2 cups toasted cashews, finely chopped, for folding into the meringue
- **Butter** — 1 1/2 cups (3 sticks) unsalted butter, softened, for the French buttercream
- **Sugar** — 3/4 cup granulated sugar (for the buttercream syrup)
- **Water** — 1/4 cup water (to cook the sugar syrup)
- **Eggs** — 5 egg yolks (left over from the whites), for the buttercream
- **Vanilla extract** — 1 tsp vanilla extract (for the buttercream)
- **Salt** — 1/4 tsp salt
- **Cashews** — 1 cup toasted cashews, chopped, for coating the assembled cake

**Steps**

1. Prep and toast: Heat oven to 325F (165C). Spread all 3 cups of cashews on a baking sheet and toast 8-10 minutes until fragrant and lightly golden, then cool completely. Finely chop 2 cups for the meringue and reserve 1 cup chopped for coating. Line the backs of two baking sheets with parchment and draw two 8-inch squares (or circles) on each — four total, two per sheet.
2. Make the dacquoise: Beat the egg whites with cream of tartar on medium until foamy, then add the 1 cup sugar a tablespoon at a time, raising speed to high, until you have a stiff, glossy meringue. Beat in 1 tsp vanilla. Using a spatula, gently fold in the 2 cups finely chopped toasted cashews in three additions, keeping as much volume as possible.
3. Bake the layers: Divide the meringue evenly among the four drawn squares and spread to about 1/4 inch thick, filling each outline. Bake at 325F for 25-30 minutes, rotating the pans halfway, until the meringues are pale golden, dry, and crisp on top. Cool completely on the pans — they crisp further as they cool. Peel from the parchment only once fully cooled.
4. Start the French buttercream: Bring the butter to a soft, room-temperature state. In a small saucepan combine the 3/4 cup sugar and 1/4 cup water and cook over medium heat without stirring to the soft-ball stage, 238-240F on a thermometer (the syrup turns thick and slow-bubbling).
5. Cook the yolks: While the syrup heats, beat the 5 egg yolks in a stand mixer until pale and thick. With the mixer running, pour the hot syrup down the side of the bowl in a thin steady stream (avoid the whisk). Whip on high until the mixture is fluffy and cooled to room temperature to the touch, about 5-7 minutes — this cooks and stabilizes the yolks.
6. Finish the buttercream: With the mixer on medium, add the softened butter a couple of tablespoons at a time, beating until each is absorbed. It may look loose or curdled mid-way — keep beating and it will come together into a smooth, glossy buttercream. Beat in 1 tsp vanilla and 1/4 tsp salt.
7. Assemble: Place one dacquoise layer on a serving board and spread with a generous layer of buttercream. Repeat with the remaining layers, finishing with buttercream on top. Spread a thin coat of buttercream around the sides as well.
8. Coat and chill: Press the reserved 1 cup chopped toasted cashews over the top and sides. Chill at least 2-3 hours (ideally overnight) to firm up. For the cleanest slices and classic crunch-to-cream contrast, let it sit 10 minutes at room temperature, then cut with a hot, dry knife.

</details>

<sub>📷 Sara McCleary, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Wikimedia Commons · [Open in app →](https://justinsuo.github.io/waivy/recipes/bake2-sans-rival/)</sub>
